{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

1 Executive Summary
Product Introduction
Tivozanib is a kinase inhibitor (KI) that inhibits phosphorylation of vascular endothelial growth
factor receptor (VEGFR) 1, VEGFR 2 and VEGFR 3 and inhibits other kinases including c-kit and
W'&Z ɴ Ăƚ ĐůŝŶŝĐĂůůǇ ƌĞůĞǀĂŶƚ ĐŽŶĐĞŶƚƌĂƚŝŽŶƐ, as demonstrated via in vitro cellular kinase
assays.
The proposed dosing schedule is 1.34 mg taken orally once daily for 21 days on treatment
followed by 7 days off treatment for a 28-day cycle until disease progression or unacceptable
toxicity.
Tivozanib is a new molecular entity that was not approved in the U.S. for any indication at the
time of submission of this application.

Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
The review team recommends approval of tivozanib, according to 21 Code of Federal Regulations
(CFR), Part 314.50, Subpart B, for the following indication:

“FOTIVDA is indicated for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory
advanced renal cell carcinoma (RCC) following two or more prior systemic therapies”
The basis for the recommendation is a favorable benefit-risk profile for tivozanib in patients with
advanced RCC. In a Phase 3 trial, TIVO-3, 350 patients with advanced renal cell carcinoma were
randomized in a 1:1 ratio to tivozanib at a dose of 1.34 mg once daily for 21 consecutive days
followed by 7 days off or to sorafenib 400 mg twice daily. Randomization was stratified by
International Metastatic RCC Database Consortium (IMDC) risk category and prior therapy (2
prior tyrosine kinse inhibitor (TKI) vs. 1 prior TKI + prior checkpoint inhibitors (CPI) vs. 1 prior TKI
+ any other agent). To be eligible, patients were required to have had previous treatment with
2 prior lines of systemic therapy ĂŶĚ ƚŽ ŚĂǀĞ ĂŶ K' W^ ч1. Baseline characteristics were
balanced between arms on TIVO-3; approximately half of patients on both arms received 2
prior TKIs, approximately one quarter of patients on both arms received 1 prior TKI + prior CPI,
and approximately one quarter of patients on both arms received 1 prior TKI and another
systemic agent.

The major efficacy outcome measure was progression free survival (PFS) in all randomized
patients per Blinded Independent Review Committee (BIRC) review of imaging. A statistically
significant improvement in PFS was observed for patients randomized to tivozanib (n=175) vs.
sorafenib (n=175). The estimate of the hazard ratio (HR) for PFS was 0.73 (95% CI: 0.56 to 0.95,
p-value of 0.016). Median PFS estimate was 5.59 months (95% CI: 4.83, 7.33) in the tivozanib
12
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
arm vs. 3.88 months (95% CI: 3.71 to 5.55) in the sorafenib arm. Consistent results were
observed across the pre-specified subgroups. The final median overall survival (OS) estimate
was 16.39 months for the tivozanib arm vs. 19.15 months for the sorafenib arm; however, the
HR was 0.97 (95% CI: 0.75 to 1.24). The objective response rate (ORR) was 18% (95% CI: 12%,
24%) in the tivozanib arm vs. 8% (95% CI: 4%, 13%) in the sorafenib arm; these results were
supportive of the overall efficacy of tivozanib vs. sorafenib.
Progression-free survival is considered by FDA to be an acceptable endpoint to demonstrate the
effectiveness of new therapeutics across many oncology indications and has been the primary
basis for approval of therapies indicated for the treatment of advanced renal cell carcinoma.
Although the overall difference in median PFS between arms was 1.71 months, which is shorter
than the 8 week scanning interval on the trial, a post-hoc interval censoring analysis conducted
by FDA to explore the robustness of these results with respect to the imaging assessment
interval was consistent with the primary analysis. Because the study was a replacement trial
design compared to standard of care therapy, the benefit in PFS was deemed appropriate to
support substantial evidence of effectiveness.
The OS HR estimate was >1 favoring sorafenib [i.e. HR of 1.12 (0.4-1.51)] at an interim analysis
of OS and thus FDA recommended further follow up. The final OS analysis showed a HR of 0.97
(95% CI: 0.75 to 1.24), which reflected a more mature timepoint in the overall survival curve
than the earlier analysis. The estimate of median OS was longer on the sorafenib arm, but the
median OS is thought to represent only one timepoint on the survival curve and not the overall
curve. With that, the FDA conducted a post-hoc exploratory analysis in which the restricted
mean survival time (RMST) difference between the two arms was calculated using different
cutoff timepoints. Considering the small difference observed between the two treatment arms
in the RMST analysis, together with the overlapped survival curves and the HR estimate of 0.97
in favor of the tivozanib arm, the review team concluded that overall, no decrement in overall
survival was observed for patients on the tivozanib arm.
The PFS and OS results were also interpreted in the setting of an active control arm of
sorafenib, which had previously demonstrated a PFS and OS advantage in patients with
advanced renal cell carcinoma compared to placebo. Any efficacy observed in the tivozanib arm
in terms of a PFS advantage was thus considered additive to the effect that sorafenib had on its
own in this setting. Consistent efficacy was also demonstrated in the ~25% of patients who
were pretreated with a CPI, which would be more similar to a contemporary US population in
the later-line setting with the recent approval of multiple CPI-containing combinations in the
first-line metastatic setting.
The safety profile of tivozanib was considered within range for this patient population and was
similar to that observed with other kinase inhibitors. For patients receiving tivozanib on TIVO-3,
deaths due to TEAEs occurred in 7.5% including pneumonia, hepatobiliary disorders, respiratory
failure, myocardial infarction, cerebrovascular accident, and subdural hematoma. Grade 3-4
TEAEs occurred in 67%, and treatment discontinuations occurred in 21%. The most common
TEAEs observed for patients on the tivozanib arm included fatigue, hypertension, diarrhea,
13
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
decreased appetite, nausea, dysphonia, hypothyroidism, cough, and stomatitis. Hypertension
and hypertensive crisis, cardiac failure, cardiac ischemia and arterial thromboembolic events,
venous thromboembolic events, hemorrhagic events, proteinuria, thyroid dysfunction, risk of
impaired wound healing, reversible posterior leukoencephalopathy syndrome, embryo-fetal
toxicity, and allergic reactions to tartrazine are included as Warnings and Precautions in the
tivozanob labeling.
Overall, patients receiving tivozanib on TIVO-3 had lower rates of dose reduction, interruption,
or permanent discontinuation than those receiving sorafenib. Although no Patient-Reported
Outcome data (PROs) were collected on TIVO-3, analysis of PROs from TIVO-1 which also
randomized patients with advanced RCC to tivozanib vs sorafenib (in the first-line setting)
suggested an improvement in the analysis of overall treatment bother results. This suggests an
overall improved tolerability over an accepted standard-of-care according to several clinically
relevant metrics. The safety profile of tivozanib was further supported by review of safety data
from an additional 835 patients in other cinical trials who received the dose of tivozanib under
current review.
Tivozanib had previously been evaluated in TIVO-1, which randomized patients with treatmentnaïve advanced RCC to receive tivozanib versus sorafenib. Although TIVO-1 met its primary
endpoint (PFS HR = 0.80 [95% CI: 0.64, 0.99], p = 0.04), a trend towards a detrimental effect on
survival was observed with tivozanib (OS HR = 1.25 [95% CI: 0.95, 1.62], p = 0.11). The FDA team
convened a meeting of the Oncologic Drug Advisory Committee (ODAC) on May 2, 2013 to
discuss this application. The Applicant presented their perspective that the reason for the
overall survival results was the one-way crossover aspect of the study design. However, ODAC
voted 13:1, “No,” in response to the question “Has the Applicant demonstrated a favorable
benefit to risk evaluation for the treatment of renal cell carcinoma in an adequate and wellcontrolled trial?”, citing concerns that included the observed decrement in the HR estimate for
OS and confounding factors in the trial design.
Despite the negative previous ODAC vote for tivozanib and the subsequent CR letter issued on
June 6, 2013 for tivozanib based on review of TIVO-1, the review team felt that the totality of
the TIVO-3 data warranted an approval in this case given the PFS advantage, supportive ORR,
detailed safety analysis, and the HR for OS that ultimately did not demonstrate a decrement in
patients who were required to have had previous treatment with 2 prior lines of therapy.
Although the Applicant had requested an indication that included all patients with advanced
renal cell carcinoma, the FDA review team granted an indication that reflected the TIVO-3
enrolled population and not the TIVO-1 population, i.e. patients who progressed on two prior
lines of therapy.
Labeling was drafted with the input and the agreement of all discipline-specific review teams
and the Applicant. All disciplines were in favor of a regular approval. There are no
postmarketing requirements or postmarketing commitments for this application.

14
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

Benefit-Risk Assessment
Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
Patients with relapsed or refractory advanced renal cell carcinoma following two or more prior systemic therapies may receive a TKI (different
than one received previously) or an mTOR inhibitor on progression; however there is no therapy specifically approved in this line of treatment.
Thus patients with advanced RCC who progressed on two prior lines of therapy, including a TKI, were evaluated in TIVO-3, which randomized
350 patients to tivozanib or sorafenib with a primary endpoint of PFS per BIRC assessmant.
The primary efficacy analysis for TIVO-3 demonstrated a PFS benefit per BIRC assessment for treatment with tivozanib (n=175) vs. sorafenib
(n=175). The estimate of the hazard ratio for PFS was 0.73 (95% CI: 0.56 to 0.95, p-value of 0.016). Median PFS estimate was 5.59 months (95%
CI: 4.83, 7.33) in the tivozanib arm vs. 3.88 months (95% CI: 3.71 to 5.55) in the sorafenib arm. Subgroup analyses were consistent across the
prespecified stratification factors of IMDC risk category and prior therapy (2 prior TKIs vs. 1 prior TKI + prior CPI vs. 1 prior TKI + any other
agent). The final median OS estimate was 16.39 months for the tivozanib arm vs. 19.15 months for the sorafenib arm, with a HR of 0.97 (95%
CI: 0.75 to 1.24) for tivozinib. The ORR was 18% (95% CI: 12%, 24%) in the tivozanib arm vs. 8% (95% CI: 4%, 13%) in the sorafenib arm; these
results were supportive of the overall efficacy of tivozanib vs. sorafenib.
The safety profile of tivozanib for patients on TIVO-3 was in line with the known safety profile described with other TKIs. Deaths due to an
adverse event (excluding disease progression) occurred in 7.5% of tivozanib-treated patients on TIVO-3 and included pneumonia, hepatobiliary
disorders, respiratory failure, myocardial infarction, cerebrovascular accident, and subdural hematoma. Permanent treatment discontinuation
due to adverse events occurred in 21%. Grade 3-4 adverse events occurred in 67% of patients and the most common (>20%) all-grade adverse
events were fatigue, hypertension, diarrhea, decreased appetite, nausea, dysphonia, hypothyroidism, cough, and stomatitis. Warnings and
Precautions for tivozanib include hypertension and hypertensive crisis, cardiac failure, cardiac ischemia and arterial thromboembolic events,
venous thromboembolic events, hemorrhagic events, proteinuria, thyroid dysfunction, risk of impaired wound healing, reversible posterior
leukoencephalopathy syndrome, embryo-fetal toxicity, and allergic reactions to tartrazine.
According to 21 CFR, Part 314.50, Subpart B a regular approval for tivozanib is recommended. All review disciplines are in agreement.

2 Therapeutic Context
Analysis of Condition
Renal cell carcinoma (RCC) is a serious and life-threatening condition. In 2021, an estimated
76,080 new diagnoses of kidney cancer will be made and 13,780 deaths will occur in the U.S.[1]
Approximately 80-90% of kidney cancers are RCC, and 16% of kidney cancers are metastatic at
diagnosis. Kidney cancer deaths comprise 2.4% of cancer deaths in the U.S. The 5-year survival
of newly diagnosed metastatic kidney cancer is 13%.[2] Clear cell RCC is the most common
histologic subtype in adults, comprising approximately 80% of RCC cases.[3]

Analysis of Current Treatment Options
Advanced RCC is a malignancy that was historically difficult to treat with systemic therapy due
to its chemoresistant nature. The approval of sorafenib and sunitinib in 2005 and 2006 offered
patients the first kinase inhibitors for advanced RCC. This was followed by subsequent approval
of other kinase inhibitors for treatment of patients with advanced RCC including pazopanib,
axitinib, cabozantinib, and lenvatinib, and kinase inhibitors have become a mainstay of
treatment for patients with advanced RCC.
Other treatment options available in the U.S., although used less frequently, include
bevacizumab, a monoclonal antibody against VEGFR, and mammalian target of rapamycin
(mTOR) inhibitors such as everolimus.
Nivolumab was the first CPI approved for the second line treatment of advanced RCC in 2016.
Since that time, the treatment landscape in RCC has continued to evolve with the approval of
several CPI–containing combination regimens in frontline setting beginning in 2018. These
combinations include nivolumab plus ipilimumab, pembrolizumab plus axitinib, avelumab plus
axitinib, and nivolumab plus cabozatinib. The standard second-line therapy has not been
established since these frontline approvals occurred. However, since there are multiple options
for frontline therapy, guidelines generally suggest considering any one of a number of regimens
as reasonable choices, including any of the frontline options to which the patient has not been
exposed, as well as kinase inhibitors that could be used as monotherapy or in combination.
These include cabozantinib with or without nivolumab, nivolumab with or without ipilimumab,
axitinib with or without avelumab, and lenvatinib with everolimus.
There is currently no therapy specifically approved in the third-line setting for treatment of
advanced RCC, however several KIs have a line-agnostic approval for treatment of advanced
RCC.
Table 1. Summary of Kinase Inhibitors (KI) Approved for Advanced RCC

3 Regulatory Background
U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
Tivozanib is not approved in the U.S. It has been approved by regulatory agencies in Europe
(European Medicines Agency [EMA]) and New Zealand.

Summary of Presubmission/Submission Regulatory Activity
September 27, 2012 (NDA 204408): The Applicant submitted an NDA for tivozanib for the
proposed indication of adults with unresectable or metastatic RCC based on the results of TIVO1. TIVO-1 was a multicenter trial that randomized patients with KI-naïve advanced RCC to
receive tivozanib versus sorafenib. See also Section 8.1.3.
The study met its primary endpoint (PFS HR = 0.80 [95% CI: 0.64, 0.99], p = 0.04). However, a
trend towards a detrimental effect on survival with tivozanib (OS HR = 1.25 [95% CI: 0.95, 1.62],
p = 0.11) was observed. Therefore, the FDA team decided to discuss the application at an
Oncologic Drug Advisory Committee (ODAC).
May 2, 2013: An ODAC was convened to discuss TIVO-1 results and considerations for approval.
The Applicant presented their perspective that the reason for the overall survival results was
the one-way crossover aspect of the study design. ODAC voted 13:1, “No,” in response to the
question “Has the Applicant demonstrated a favorable benefit to risk evaluation for the
treatment of renal cell carcinoma in an adequate and well-controlled trial?” Committee
members summarized the main concerns as being primarily a lack of confidence in the benefitrisk evaluation due to the decrement in the overall survival trend, trial design with confounding
factors that limited interpretation of the results, and low enrollment from the U.S. that might
impact the generalizability of results.[16]
June 6, 2013: FDA issued a Complete Response letter for NDA 204408. The letter cited one
Clinical issue and one Product Quality issue:
Clinical Deficiency: The inconsistent PFS and OS results and imbalance in post study
treatments make the trial’s results uninterpretable and inconclusive when making a riskbenefit assessment necessary for drug approval. FDA recommended that the sponsor
perform an adequate and well-controlled randomized trial(s) of tivozanib using PFS as
the primary endpoint and OS as a secondary endpoint. The sponsor should ensure the
applicability of the results from this trial to the US population. The trial should be
powered to detect a difference in PFS and adequately sized to reassure us that there is
no adverse effect on OS. The design, conduct, and results of this trial will determine
whether one additional trial will be sufficient for approval purposes.
(b) (4)
(b)
Product Quality Deficiency: The proposed dissolution acceptance criterion (Q= (4)% at
minutes) is not supported by the provided dissolution data and is not acceptable. The

data support a dissolution acceptance criterion of Q= (4)% at minutes for your
product. Implement this criterion at release and on stability. When the NDA is

May 2015 (IND 075547): A Pre-Phase 3 Meeting was held to obtain feedback on the design of a

FDA agreed generally that the proposed study could support the proposed indication in the
third-line setting. FDA’s additional feedback was that the sponsor should provide justification
for the statistical assumptions around PFS, should state assumptions for OS, and should justify
the decision to allow patients to continue study therapy after receiving radiation therapy for
progressive disease.
November 6, 2015: The sponsor submitted the statistical analysis plan (SAP) for TIVO-3, which
stated that: “The interim OS analysis will be done at the time of the final PFS analysis. Assuming
a median OS for sorafenib of 12 months and a median OS for tivozanib of 16 months, 245 OS
events at the time of final OS analysis and 149 OS events at the time of final PFS analysis will
yield approximately 61% power to demonstrate longer OS for tivozanib using the log-rank test,
given 2.5% type I error (one-sided). The final OS analysis will be performed when all subjects
have been lost to follow-up, have withdrawn consent, or have died, or when all subjects in
follow-up have been on-study for at least 2 years, whichever occurs first.”
November 8, 2018: The sponsor requested a Pre-NDA Meeting after conducting the first
interim analysis (IA), which showed a statistically improved PFS with HR 0.73 (0.56, 0.94),
p=0.0165 and OS HR 1.12 (0.4-1.51) based on 183 OS events (70% information fraction).
January 24, 2019: Pre-NDA meeting. FDA’s preliminary comments discouraged the sponsor
from submitting an NDA at the time due to the overall survival results, which showed a trend
towards a detriment in OS in both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3. The sponsor agreed to not submit an
NDA. The sponsor stated that they were considering conducting a formal interim analysis at a
later date with a Type I error allocation based on the percentage information.
February 21, 2019: The SAP version 4 submitted to FDA, where the final OS analysis was
changed to the second IA, to be done when all patients had been on study for 2 years. An
additional final OS analysis was added which would occur when 75% of patients would
experience an event, at 263 OS events. See Table 5 in Section 8.1 for details.
October 23, 2019: Type B Pre-NDA meeting to discuss the second IA OS results: OS HR 0.99

4 Significant Issues from Other Review Disciplines Pertinent to Clinical
Conclusions on Efficacy and Safety
Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI)
For TIVO-3, four clinical investigators [Drs. Ornstein (Site 101), Pal (Site 118), Hauke (Site 124),
and Kollmannsberger (Site 201)] and the study sponsor (Aveo Pharmaceuticals) were selected
for clinical inspections. The inspections of Drs. Kollmannsberger and Pal were conducted as
remote regulatory assessments due site and/or travel restrictions related to the COVID-19
pandemic. The inspections of Dr. Ornstein, Dr. Hauke, and Aveo Pharmaceuticals were
conducted on-site.
Dr. Pal had been suspended from study enrollment by the sponsor for a period of 7 months due
to non-compliance with the transcription of source patient data to the eCRF. No regulatory
violation could be issued to Dr. Pal due to the remote nature of the assessment. Aveo’s
oversight of Dr. Pal was adequate, including daily oversight during the period of
noncompliance. There was no evidence of harm to patients or data integrity related to the
delay in data transcription.
The onsite inspections of Drs. Ornstein and Hauke and remote investigation of Dr.
Kollmannsberger revealed no significant findings. There was no evidence of underreporting of
serious adverse events or significant protocol deviations. Based on the results of these
inspections, the study appears to have been conducted adequately and the data generated by
the inspected clinical investigators and the sponsor appear to be acceptable in support of the
NDA.

CMC information submitted in the NDA and acceptable status for all manufacturing facilities.

5 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
Executive Summary
The nonclinical studies for tivozanib hydrochloride were reviewed on May 24, 2013 under NDA
# 204408 (Applicant-owned) for the the treatment of patients with advanced renal cell
carcinoma (RCC). See Appendix 19.3. In the current executive summary, risk estimates are
calculated based on the maximum recommended human dose of 1.34 mg/day for adult
patients with relapsed or refractory advanced RCC.
Tivozanib hydrochloride is an anticancer agent of the Vascular Endothelial Growth Factor
Receptor (VEGFR) Tyrosine Kinase inhibitor class and is being developed for the treatment of
advanced renal cell carcinoma. In vitro cellular kinase assays demonstrated that tivozanib is an
inhibitor of VEGF receptors -1, -2, and -3 (IC50 0.107, 0.081, and 0.122 ng/mL, respectively) and
other kinases such as c-Kŝƚ ĂŶĚ W'&Zɴ Ăƚ ŚŝŐŚĞƌ /50’s (0.83 and 0.88 ng/ml, respectively). The
IC50 of these kinases is well below the Cmin value achieved in RCC patients (16 to 30.9 ng/mL)
following repeat dosing at the recommended dose at 1.34 mg/day, suggesting continuous
inhibition of these kinases throughout the treatment period.
In vivo pharmacodynamic studies in mice and rats bearing human or murine xenograft tumors
demonstrated that tivozanib inhibited tumor growth at a dose of 0.2 mg/kg in rats, via
inhibition of angiogenesis and vascular permeability. This dose is associated with plasma levels
of tivozanib in rodents (Cmax of 70 - 108 ng/ml; AUC of 1640 ng*hr/ml), which were within range
of the Cmax and AUC achieved in patients with solid tumors following repeat dosing at the
recommended dose of 1. 34 mg/day (Cmax was - 86.9 ng/mL; AUC0-24 was 1510 ng*hr/mL).
The selection of animal models to be used in toxicology and safety pharmacology studies (rats
and monkeys) was based on PK and/or metabolic profiles of tivozanib. The oral route was
utilized in nonclinical studies as this is the intended clinical route of exposure. The tivozanib
toxicokinetic profile following repeat oral administration was approximately linear in rats and
monkeys, with Cmax and AUC increasing in an approximately dose proportional manner. No
gender differences were shown in the rat or monkey. Interspecies differences in the rate of
absorption (Tmax) and elimination half-life (t1/2) were reported. The elimination t1/2 in rats (9-45
hours) was greater than that reported in monkeys (8-12 hours), but was considerably shorter
than that reported for humans (4.5-5.1 days). To account for these differences, the dosing
regimen in the nonclinical toxicology studies was daily as compared to the clinical regimen
(once daily, 3 weeks on and 1 week off). In in vitro distribution studies conducted using animal
or human plasma, it was shown that tivozanib bound to plasma proteins at > 97.6%, and
protein binding was predominantly to albumin, with low biŶĚŝŶŐ ƚŽ ɲ-1-acid glycoprotein.
Tivozanib mainly partitioned into the plasma/serum component of blood. In vivo metabolism
studies demonstrated that the parent compound (tivozanib) was the major component in
serum and feces of rats and monkeys. Tivozanib appeared to be metabolized mainly by
26
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
oxidation, demethylation and glucuronidation. All prominent human metabolites (M1 through
M24) were present in both rat and monkey, with the exception of M11, which was unique to
human hepatocytes (minor >0.5%), and M10, which was not observed in rat hepatocytes. M14
was present at >10% and was designated as a major metabolite in human, monkey and rat.
Comparison of the metabolic profile of tivozanib in liver microsomes showed that monkey most
closely resembled the human, followed by the rat. These data provided support for species
selection. Excretion studies in rats and monkeys showed that tivozanib was mainly excreted in
feces in both species, and through the bile in rats.
In safety pharmacology studies, no effects on respiration rate, tidal volume, neurobehavioral
endpoints or physiological parameters were reported in rat studies. In single and repeat-dose
cardiovascular studies in telemetered cynomolgus monkeys, a dose of 3 mg/kg (HED= 58
mg/day; 43-fold of the recommended human dose) resulted in increases in systolic, diastolic
ĂŶĚ ŵĞĂŶ ĂƌƚĞƌŝĂů ďůŽŽĚ ƉƌĞƐƐƵƌĞ͘ ƚ ĚŽƐĞƐ шϭ mg/kg/day in the monkey (HED=19.5 mg/day;
15-fold of the recommended human dose), decreases in heart rate, cardiac output, renal
arterial blood flow and cardiac arterial blood flow were reported. A decrease in vascular
relaxation response and decrease in sensitivity to acetylcholine were also reported at ĚŽƐĞƐ шϭ
mg/kg/day, which may have played a role in the reported hypertension response in monkeys.
Using the core battery of genetic toxicology studies recommended in ICH guidances, tivozanib
was not shown to be mutagenic, aneugenic or clastogenic; however, tivozanib produced

6 Clinical Pharmacology
Executive Summary
Tivozanib hydrochloride is a tyrosine kinase inhibitor of vascular endothelial growth factor
(VEGFR)-1, -2, and -3. In the current original NDA submission, the applicant seeks approval of
tivozanib for the treatment of patients with relpased or refractory RCC. The Applicant proposed
dosing regimen is 1.34 mg oral once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with or without
food.
The primary evidence of efficacy and safety is provided by a pivotal randomized, open-label,
controlled trial (TIVO-3) in patients with relapsed or refractory advanced RCC, who received
prior systemic treatments (2 TKIs, a TKI plus a checkpoint inhibitor or a TKI plus other agent).
Safety data included 1008 patients with RCC treated with tivozanib who were enrolled in 6
monotherapy studies that included patients from study TIVO-3 comparing tivozanib to
sorafenib.
The clinical pharmacology review focused on dose selection and the acceptability of tivozanib
dosing recommendations for general population, for patients with organ dysfunction, and for
drug-drug interactions.
Recommendations
The office of clinical pharmacology has reviewed the information contained in NDA 212904.
This NDA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with
specific recommendations/comments are summarized below:
Review Issue

evidence of effectiveness

acceptable and is supported by the improvement in PFS,
compared to the active control, observed in patients with

Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
Tivozanib demonstrated linear pharmacokinetics (PK) in the dose range of 0.89 -1.78 mg
following both single dose and repeated doses. At the recommended dosage of 1.34 mg QD,
the steady state Cmax (%CV) of Tivozanib was 86.9 (66%) ng/mL and the steady state area
under the concentration-time curve (AUC0-24h) (%CV) was 1510 (67%) ng*hr/mL. The plasma
elimination half-life (T1/2) is 111 hours. Following repeated 1.34 mg QD administration, steadystate was achieved by14 days after the first dose and accumulation at steady-state is
approximately 6- to 7- fold the exposure observed at single-dose levels.
ADME properties
Absorption: The median time to peak concentration (Tmax) ranged from 3 to 24 hours in most
studies. This is most likely due to enterohepatic recirculation of tivozanib, which was also seen
in non-clinical mouse, rat and monkey studies. The Cmax of tivozanib decreased by 23% (Cmax
GMR 0.77, 95%CI: 0.729-0.824) and the AUC0-ь did not change (AUC0-ь GMR 1.074, 95%CI:
1.028-1.123) when tivozanib was taken with a high-calorie, high-fat meal (approximately 500600 fat calories, 250 carbohydrate calories and 150 protein calories) compared to those in the
fasted state.
Distribution: The typical apparent volume of distributaion of tivozanib is 121 L, volume of
distribution was found to increase nearly proportionally with body weight. In vitro protein
binding of tivozanib is >99%. The blood-to-plasma ratio is 0.495 to 0.615.
Metabolism͗ dŝǀŽǌĂŶŝď ĐĂŶ ďĞ ŵĞƚĂďŽůŝǌĞĚ ďǇ zWϯϰ ĂŶĚ zWϭϭ ŝŶ ǀŝƚƌŽ͘ Ɛ zWϭϭ ŝƐ ŵĂŝŶůǇ
expressed in extrahepatic tissues such as the lung and intestine, it is unlikely that this isoform
would be extensively involved in generating the metabolites observed following incubations
with human liver microsomes. Following a single oral dose of 1.34 mg of ;ϭϲϬ ʅŝͿ ŽĨ ΀14C]tivozanib, unchanged tivozanib was the predominant circulating component in serum, ranging
from approximately 77.6% to 95.1% (median = 90.1%) of the total radioactivity. Of the minor
unknown metabolites detected, each represented less than or equal to a mean of ~4% of the
total radioactivity.

Elimination: The T1/2 of tivozanib was 111 hours. Typical CL/F was 0.75 L/h.
Excretion: Following a single oral dose of 1.34 mg of ;ϭϲϬ ʅŝͿ ŽĨ ΀14C]-tivozanib to healthy male
patients, 79.3% of the radioactive dose was recovered in feces as both unchanged tivozanib
(7.82% to 46.1%, median = 26.2%) and metabolites (the predominant metabolites together
represented a total of 6.35% to 34.3%, median=20.5%) and 11.8% in urine (unchanged tivozanib
not detected).
General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
General Dosing
The proposed dosing regimen is 1.34 mg oral once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off,
with or without food.
Dose Selection Rationale and Dose- and Exposure-Response Relationships
The doses of 0.89, 1.34, and 1.78 mg were evaluated in the first-in-human dose escalation study
(Study AV-951-03-B01). The selection of the proposed dose was supported by dose-dependent
effect of tivozanib on dose-limiting toxicity and hypertension in the first-in-human study, and
also supported by pharmacodynamic analysis, which demonstrated dose-dependent increases
in serum VEGF-A levels, dose-dependent decreases in serum soluble VEGFR-2 levels and a dosedependent reduction in tumor perfusion. Due to the incidence of dose-limiting toxicities (DLTs)
at the highest dose level tested (1.78 mg), the 1.34 mg dose of tivozanib was identified as the
highest dose level with an acceptable safety/tolerability profile.
With regard to the on/off period, the first-in-human study was initiated with a 4 weeks on/2
weeks off schedule, and then altered to a 3 weeks on/1 week off schedule due to the following
reasons: 1) Adverse events in FIH Study AV-951-03-B01 were rapidly reversible upon stopping
treatment, supporting a shorter rest period; 2) Nonclinical studies showed that tumors regrew
after cessation of tivozanib.
The proposed dosing regimen (1.34 mg oral once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with
or without food) was subsequently studied in four studies: AV-951-07-201, AV-951-10-202, AV951-09-301 (TIVO-1), AV-951-15-303 (TIVO-3). The primary evidence of efficacy and safety is
provided by pivotal, randomized, open-label, controlled trial (TIVO-3) in patients with relapsed
or refractory advanced RCC, who received prior systemic treatments (2 TKIs, a TKI plus a
checkpoint inhibitor or a TKI plus other agent). Safety data included 1008 RCC patients treated
with FOTIVDA who were enrolled in 6 monotherapy studies that included patients from the
pivotal randomized study TIVO-3 comparing tivozanib to sorafenib. The proposed dosage is
effective and has a manageable safety profile.
Dose-response relationship was not established, the exposure-response analysis was based on
only one dose level, which is the proposed dosage 1.34 mg. Exposure-response analysis
demonstated that increased tivozanib exposure by Cavg quartile was associated with a trend
32
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
towards increased PFS, the highest tivozanib exposure quartile (Q4) an estimated relative risk
reduction of approximately 33% compared to the lowest tivozanib exposure quartile (Q1). In
addition, exposure-dependent increases in rates of hand-foot syndrome (HFS, a.k.a., palmarplantar erythrodysesthesia [PPE]) was also demonstrated.
The above data supports the selection of the highest dose level with an acceptable
safety/tolerability profile, which is the proposed dosing regimen 1.34 mg oral once daily (QD)
for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with or without food.
Therapeutic Individualization
No clinically significant differences in the PK of tivozanib were observed based on gender, race
(93% Caucasian, 3% African American, 2% Asian, 2% others), age (18-84 yrs), body weight (39158 kg), mild to severe renal impairment, or mild hepatic impairment.
Patients with moderate hepatic impairment eliminated Tivozanib more slowly, mean tivozanib
exposure (AUCʏ) increased 1.62-fold. Reduce the recommended dosage of tivozanib to 0.89 mg
capsule taken orally once daily for 3 weeks on treatment followed by 1 week off treatment for a
28-day cycle for patients with moderate hepatic impairment.
The effect of severe hepatic impairment on the pharmacokinetics of tivozanib is unknown. For
patients with severe hepatic impairment, tivozanib is not recommended.
Outstanding Issues
None.

Clinical Pharmacology Questions
Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
zĞƐ͕ ƚŚĞ ƉƌŝŵĂƌǇ ĞǀŝĚĞŶĐĞ ŽĨ ĞĨĨĞĐƚŝǀĞŶĞƐƐ ǁĂƐ ŽďƚĂŝŶĞĚ ĨƌŽŵ a pivotal, randomized, openlabel, controlled trial (TIVO-3). A summary of the clinical effectiveness and safety results based
on Study TIVO-3 are provided in Section 7.
Exposure-response analyses for efficacy demonstrated exposure-dependent increases in
progression-free survival (PFS), providing supportive evidence of effectiveness for tivozanib.
Exposure-response analyses for safety demonstrated exposure-dependent increases in rates of
hand-foot syndrome (HFS, a.k.a., palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia [PPE]). In addition, the
proposed dose reduction strategy (1.34 mg to 0.89 mg) to manage tolerable чgrade 3 AEs was
proposed and it appears reasonable.
Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the
indication is being sought?
zĞƐ͕ ƚŚĞ ƉƌŽƉƐĞĚ ĚŽƐŝŶŐ ƌĞŐŝŵĞŶ ŝƐ ĐŽŶƐŝĚĞƌĞĚ ĂƉƉƌŽƉƌŝĂƚĞ ĨƌŽŵ ƚŚĞ ĨŽůůŽǁŝŶŐ ĂƐƉĞĐƚƐ͗
1) The dose-dependent, dose-limiting toxicities (DLTs) and hypertension in the first-inhuman study, as well as the dose-dependent increases in serum VEGF-A levels, dosedependent decreases in serum soluble VEGFR-2 levels, and dose-dependent reduction in
tumor perfusion supported the selection of a highest dose level (1.34 mg) with an
acceptable safety/tolerability profile. Due to the incidence of DLTs at the highest dose
level tested (1.78 mg) in the dose-escalation study, and the acceptable safety profile in
patients with RCC in the pivotal TIVO-3 study, the 1.34 mg dose of tivozanib was
considered appropriate.
2) With regard to the on/off period, the first-in-human study was initiated with a 4 weeks
on/2 weeks off schedule, and then altered to a 3 weeks on/1 week off schedule due to
the following reasons: a) Adverse events in FIH Study AV-951-03-B01 were rapidly
reversible upon stopping treatment, supporting a shorter rest period; b) Nonclinical

36
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
studies showed that tumors regrew after cessation of tivozanib. The proposed
treatment schedule was considered appropriate due to the manageable safety profile.
3) The proposed dosage of 1.34 mg QD, 3 weeks on, 1 week off is supported by the
improvement in PFS, compared to the active control in the pivotal TIVO-3 study. The
high relative dose intensity (88.3%) despite the longer mean exposure than the active
control, suggests that tivozanib is well-tolerated.
Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based
on intrinsic patient factors?
Gender
Although gender has an effect on the clearance of tivozanib, females had a 24.5% lower
clearance (typical value 0.651 L/h) versus males (typical value 0.862 L/h). The effect of gender
on PFS was not clinically meaningful, therefore, a dose adjustment is not warranted.
Hepatic Impairment
zĞƐ͕ ĨŽƌ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ ǁŝƚŚ ŵŽĚĞƌĂƚĞ ŚĞƉĂƚŝĐ ŝŵƉĂŝƌŵĞŶƚ͕ the dosage should be reduced to 0.89 mg
oral once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off, with or without food; for patients with
severe hepatic impairment, tivozanib is not recommended.
The effect of hepatic impairment on the PK of tivozanib was assessed in a dedicated hepatic
impairment study and by population PK analysis.
In the dedicated study, the patients were originally classified according to Child-Pugh Scores,
there were 8 patients with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A), 8 patients with moderate
hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B) and 6 patients with severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh
C). Results showed that the exposure to tivozanib in patients with mild hepatic impairment was
similar to that in patients with normal hepatic function (GMR of AUC00-ь: 1.1 ). However,
tivozanib was eliminated more slowly in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment,
mean tivozanib exposure (AUC0-ь) increased 2.2-fold in patients with moderate (Child-Pugh B)
hepatic impairment and 3.4-fold in patients with severe (Child-Pugh C) hepatic impairment.
After re-classifying the patients in the dedicated study by NCI-ODWG criteria, there were 12
patients with mild hepatic impairment, 5 patients with moderate hepatic impairment, 0
patients with severe hepatic impairment. Mean tivozanib exposure (AUC0-ь) increased 1.79-fold
in patients with mild hepatic impairment and 3.49-fold in patients with moderate hepatic
impairment. The effect of hepatic impairment on the clearance of tivozanib was lager when
patients were classified by NCI-ODWG criteria compared to Child-Pugh criteria in the dedicated
study.
Therefore, in the PopPK analysis, NCI-ODWG criteria was used to classify the patients, not only
because that dosing recommendation based on NCI-ODWG criteria is more conservative, given
37
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
the larger fold changes in AUC observed, but also that the majority of the patients were cancer
patients. In the whole PopPK dataset, there were 570 patients with normal hepatic function, 83
patients with mild hepatic impairment, 10 patients with moderate hepatic impairment, 0
patients with severe hepatic impairment. The PopPK analysis indicated that mild hepatic
impairment did not have an effect on the tivozanib CL/F, while patients with moderate hepatic
impairment were found to have a 38.9% lower tivozanib CL/F compared to patients with
normal hepatic function or mild hepatic impairment.
The PopPK analysis, using all data with more patients for both mild hepatic impairment and
moderate hepatic impairment, provided a reliable estimate for the hepatic impairment effect,
given disease status was not found to be a significant covariate. Therefore, simulations were
conducted based on the PK metrics generated from PopPK analysis. Simulation results indicated
that 0.89 mg once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off in patients with moderate hepatic
impairment is estimated to result in tivozanib exposure comparable to that in patients with
normal hepatic function treated with 1.34 mg QD tivozanib for 3 weeks on and 1 week off.
Therefore, based on the safety profile in the clinical trials, 0.89 mg once daily (QD) for 3 weeks
on and 1 week off is recommended for patients with moderate hepatic impairment.
Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate
management strategy?
There is no clinically relevant food-drug interactions.

Effect of Food on Tivozanib
The Cmax of tivozanib decreased by 23% (Cmax GMR 0.77, 95%CI: 0.729-0.824) and the AUC0-ь
did not change (AUC0-ь GMR 1.074, 95%CI: 1.028-1.123) when tivozanib was taken with a highcalorie, high-fat meal (approximately 500-600 fat calories, 250 carbohydrate calories and 150
protein calories) compared to those in the fasted state. Tivozanib is recommended to be
administrated with or without food.

Review Strategy
Dr. Elaine Chang conducted the clinical review and Dr. Lijun Zhang conducted the statistical
review.
The clinical and statistical review included the following:
1. Literature review of advanced relapsed and refractory RCC.
2. Research of the FDA database for the regulatory history of the tivozanib IND 075547 and
review of the ODAC materials and transcript for the TIVO-1 review, and meeting
minutes conducted during drug development.
3. Review of applicant submitted CSR, protocol, protocol amendments, statistical analysis
plan, and selected datasets for TIVO-3 and TIVO-1.
4. Review of selected case report forms (CRFs) for TIVO-3.

6. Review of the Clinical Outcomes Assessment (COA) data submitted for TIVO-1.
7. Review of response to clinical, biostatistical, and select clinical pharmacology queries
sent to applicant.
8. Review of consultation reports including from the Office of Scientific Investigations,
Interdisciplinary Review Team for Cardiac Safety Studies (QT Study Review), and DRISK.

8 Statistical and Clinical and Evaluation
Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
TIVO-3 (AV-951-15-303)
Trial Design
Study TIVO-3 was an open-label, randomized, controlled, multi-national, parallel-arm study to
compare treatment with tivozanib to treatment with sorafenib in adult patients with
histologically or cytologically confirmed RCC with a clear cell component who failed 2 or 3 prior
systemic regimens, including a TKI other than sorafenib or tivozanib.
Patients were randomized 1:1 to receive either tivozanib or sorafenib using the Interactive Web
Response System (IWRS). Randomization was stratified by the following factors:
x IMDC prognostic category (favorable vs. intermediate vs. poor)
x Prior therapy (2 prior TKIs vs. a prior checkpoint inhibitor plus a TKI vs. a prior TKI plus
any other systemic agent)
Treatment began within 14 days of randomization. Tivozanib was administered 1.5 mg orally
daily 3 weeks on/ 1 week off in each 4-week cycle and sorafenib 400 mg BID was administered
orally continuously throughout each cycle. Note that 1.5 mg in the protocol refers to the dose
of tivozanib hydrochloride, which is equivalent to the recommended dosage of 1.34 mg,
referring to tivozanib free base.
Eligible patients were required to have recurrent metastatic RCC with a clear cell component
that had progressed after 2 or 3 prior systemic regimens, one of which included a VEGFR TKI
other than sorafenib or tivozanib. Postoperative or adjuvant systemic therapy was not counted
as a prior therapy unless a recurrence was detected within 6 months of completion of adjuvant
treatment. Patients were also required to have measurable disease per RECIST criteria and
ECOG performance status of 0 or 1.
Key exclusion criteria included untreated brain metastases, significant cardiovascular disease
(symptomatic left ventricular failure, severe angina or acute coronary syndrome within 6
months prior to trial enrollment, uncontrolled hypertension defined as systolic blood pressure
[sBP] >150 mmHg or diastolic blood pressure [dBP] >100 mmHg on 2 or more anti-hypertensive
medications, documented on 2 consecutive measurements taken at least 2 hours apart, or
cardiac arrhythmias requiring anti-arrhythmic medications, except for well-controlled atrial
fibrillation), grade ш 2 bleeding within 6 months prior, non-healing wound, inflammatory bowel
disease or risk/history of gastrointestinal perforation or fistula, thromboembolic event or
bleeding disorder within 6 months prior, and patients with HIV infection or taking immune
suppressive therapy for organ transplant.
42
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Patients were treated until a discontinuation criterion was met. Discontinuation criteria
included documented disease progression of significant clinical deterioration indicative of
progressive disease, unacceptable toxicity considered by the investigator to require removal of
the patient from the trial, and treatment interruption for > 4 weeks. Patients with stable
disease or with objective response at the time of treatment discontinuation, who discontinued
for reasons other than radiological evidence of progressive disease, continued to have periodic
tumor assessments.
Radiology assessments (CT scan or MRI) were performed every 8 weeks following Cycle 1, Day 1
to assess disease status until documented radiographic disease progression. Scans were
evaluated by a blinded independent radiology review committee (BIRC).
Study Endpoints
The primary efficacy endpoint of the study was progression-free survival (PFS) per BIRC
assessment based on RECIST v 1.1. PFS was defined as the time from randomization to the first
date of radiological disease progression or death date (due to any reason), whichever occurred
earlier.
Secondary endpoints included overall survival (OS) and objective response rate (ORR).
OS was defined as the time from randomization to the date of death due to any reason.
ORR was defined as the proportion of patients with a best overall response of confirmed
complete response or a confirmed partial response, according to the RECIST v1.1 criteria per
BIRC assessment.
Statistical Analysis Plan
Sample Size Consideration
The study was designed to have 90% power to detect a hazard ratio (HR) of 0.67 for PFS at a
two-sided significance level of 5%. Based on an assumed median PFS of 4 months in the
sorafenib arm, this targeted treatment effect represents an increase in the median PFS of
approximately 2 months (4 months vs. 6 months) in the tivozanib arm. With that,
approximately 255 PFS events were required for the primary analysis. A total of 322 patients
were planned to be randomized.
Per the statistical analysis plan (version 5), the final OS analysis was to be conducted using a
cutoff date of May 1st, 2020. Based on an estimate of approximately 4 OS events occurring per
month, approximately 263 OS events would occur at that time. That would have 64% power to
detect a hazard ratio of 0.75 at a two-sided significance level of 0.05 for OS.
Analysis Populations
The primary analysis population for all efficacy endpoints was the intent-to-treat (ITT)
population. All randomized patients were to be included in the ITT population. Patients were to
be grouped according to the treatment assigned at randomization.
43
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
A sensitivity analysis population to be used for the primary efficacy endpoint was the per
protocol (PP) population. This population was defined as all randomized patients who received
at least 8 weeks of protocol treatment, had no major protocol deviations that would confound
the effects of treatment, and met all eligibility criteria. Patients were to be analyzed as treated.
The primary analysis population for safety was the safety analysis population. This population
included all patients who received at least one dose of study drug, with treatment assignment
designated according to actual study treatment received.
Efficacy Analysis Methods
The primary efficacy endpoint, PFS per BIRC, was to be summarized using the Kaplan-Meier
method, and compared between the two treatment arms using a log-rank test stratified by the
randomization stratification factors, i.e., IMDC risk category and prior therapy, as collected in
the IWRS system. The treatment effect size was to be estimated by hazard ratio with a twosided 95% confidence interval derived from a stratified Cox proportional hazards model with
the same two stratification factors used in the stratified log-rank test.
PFS was to be censored following the rules below in the primary analysis:
x

If there were no tumor assessments after randomization, PFS would be censored on the
date of randomization.

x

If a patient was known to be alive and progression-free, PFS would be censored on the
day of the last tumor assessment.

x

If a patient was given anti-tumor treatment (anti-cancer surgery/procedure, anti- cancer
radiotherapy, and anti-cancer systemic therapy other than the study treatment) prior to
disease progression or death, PFS would be censored on the date of the last tumor
assessment prior to the start date of the anti-tumor treatment.

x

If treatment follow-up ceased prior to objective disease progression, PFS would be
censored on the date of the last tumor assessment.

x

If a patient missed 2 or more consecutive post-baseline tumor assessments and the
following assessment was disease progression, PFS would be censored on the date of
the last tumor assessment.

Secondary endpoints included OS, ORR, and duration of response (DOR). OS was to be analyzed
in the same manner as for PFS; i.e., the two treatment arms were compared using the stratified
log-rank test and the hazard ratio was estimated using the stratified Cox proportional hazards
model. The number and proportion of patients achieving confirmed response (i.e., confirmed
complete response or partial response) were to be summarized and compared between
treatment arms using a Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) test with the same stratification
factors used for the PFS analysis. Duration of response was to be summarized for patients who
have achieved a confirmed response.
Interim Analysis

44
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
An interim analysis was to be conducted after 128 PFS events were observed for futility
evaluation. A Lan-DeMets spending-type function with an O’Brien-Fleming stopping boundary
was applied at this interim look. There was no interim efficacy analysis for PFS.
Two interim analyses were planned for OS in the final version of the study SAP. The first interim
analysis was to be conducted at the time of the primary PFS analysis and the 2nd interim
analysis was to be conducted after all patients were followed for at least 2 year. The final
analysis was to be conducted at a pre-specified cutoff date of May 1st, 2020. A Lan-DeMets
spending-type function with an O’Brien-Fleming stopping boundary was used for OS interim
analyses.
Multiplicity Adjustment
There was no multiplicity adjustment for secondary endpoints. PFS was the only primary
endpoint and it was tested at 2-sided alpha of 0.05. The secondary endpoints, including overall
survival, were intended to provide supportive evidence. All secondary endpoints were assessed
without adjustment for multiple comparison.
Reviewer comments:
The sample size calculation and analysis plan for the primary endpoint of PFS is acceptable.
The analysis plan for OS has been revised several times when the study was ongoing. In the
initial SAP, the study only planned one interim OS analysis at the time of PFS analysis. Due to the
observed negative trend of OS at the time of the first interim analysis, the applicant revised the
statistical analysis plan by considering the original planned final OS analysis as the second
interim analysis and adding a new final OS analysis with longer follow-up. See the SAP
amendment summary table for details. The change of analysis plan for OS could inflate the
overall type I error rate if the study had intended to make any efficacy claims on OS. Per the
statistical design, the study did not allocate any alpha for OS tests and was not adequately
powered for OS analyses; therefore, no statistical inference could be made for OS. Results of all
secondary endpoints are considered exploratory. Overall, the statistical analysis plan appears
acceptable.

table below. The first distributed protocol was Version 2 (October 2015). A total of 161 patients
were randomized during the time that Version 2 was in use, including 3 patients enrolled under
France-specific protocol amendment 2.1 and 13 patients enrolled under UK-specific protocol
amendment 2.2. Subsequently, a total of 189 patients were randomized during the time that
Version 3 was in use, including 10 patients enrolled under France-specific protocol amendment
3.1 and 16 patients under UK-specific protocol amendment 3.2. No patients were enrolled
under Version 4.

Reviewer Comments
The FDA noted that the OS analysis plan was revised after the first interim analysis results were
available. In general, the FDA does not recommend revising analysis plan of major endpoints
based on unblinded data if not pre-specified. However, given that in this study analyses of OS
were to provide supportive evidence on efficacy and safety, and no efficacy claims were to be
made on OS, and an OS analysis based on more mature data is expected to have a more
accurate overall estimate of survival for the totality of the trial, the revision appeared
acceptable in this application.
In the SAP Amendment 6, the Applicant revised censoring rules for the primary PFS analysis by
adding a censoring rule for missing 2 or more visits per FDA’s recommendation. As this revision
occurred after unblinding PFS data in 2018, the applicant conducted a sensitivity PFS analysis, in
which this censoring rule was removed, to evaluate its impact and results are shown in Table 14.
This approach appeared acceptable.

The sponsor made a formal statement that study was conducted in compliance with ICH E6
current Good Clinical Practice and the principles of the Declaration of Helsinki or the laws and
regulations of the country in which the research was conducted, whichever afforded the
greater protection to the individual. Informed consent was obtained from all patients prior to
initiation of the study.

There was no financial information (equity interest, significant payments of other sorts, other
compensation by the sponsor and propriety) to disclose for the 802 clinical investigators who
participated in TIVO-3, as reported on FDA Form 3454. See Section 19.2.

Patient Disposition
Between May 2016 and August 2017, 457 patients (456 unique patients) were screened for
TIVO-3 and 351 patients (350 unique patients) were randomized. For the 106 patients who
were screened but not enrolled, reasons for screen failure were not captured by the electronic
data capture system and therefore not included in any dataset. However, because FDA inquired
about these details via an Information Request, the sponsor provided a listing of screened
patients, date of screening, and reason for screen failure. Reasons that patients were ineligible
for enrollment are summarized below.

For example, “general conditions” comprised reason for screen failure in half of patients, while

Of the 350 patients enrolled, 175 patients were randomized to the tivozanib arm and 175
patients were randomized to the sorafenib arm. Eight (8) patients (2 in the tivozanib arm and 6
in the sorafenib arm) were randomized but not treated. Twenty patients (11%) in the tivozanib

a

One patient was randomized twice: first to the tivozanib arm and not dosed, then to the sorafenib arm and dosed.
This patient was reported in the intent-to-treat population under tivozanib and in the safety population under
sorafenib.
b
Patients continued until progressive disease or discontinued for other reasons.

As of the May 1, 2020 cutoff, 14 patients (13 in the tivozanib arm and 1 in the sorafenib arm)
were receiving ongoing treatment with the study drug.

Reviewer Comment: The FDA notes the relatively low overall use (~25%) of prior checkpoint
inhibitors in the TIVO-3 patient population, which is likely a function of the fact that enrollment
began in 2016, several years prior to initial FDA approval of nivolumab in the second-line
setting, and certainly prior to the subsequent approval of several combination regimens
involving checkpoint inhibitors in the first-line metastatic setting. However, the proportion of
patients who had received prior checkpoint inhibitor is still higher than that of other recent
randomized trials demonstrating clinical benefit of agents in refractory RCC, including
axitinib,[17] cabozantinib,[18] and nivolumab.[19] The FDA performed an exploratory analysis
of efficacy of tivozanib specifically in those patients who received prior checkpoint inhibitor
therapy as those patients might be more similar to the patient receiving their 3rd line of
treatment in the contemporary treatment setting, and these results are discussed in the efficacy
results section below.

Use of subsequent therapies
Administration of subsequent anti-cancer therapy was summarized in Table 11. Overall, the
percentage of patients receiving any subsequent anti-cancer therapy was similar between the
two arms (49% vs. 51%). A higher proportion of patients (23%) who were randomized to receive
sorafenib received subsequent checkpoint inhibitor compared to patients randomized to
receive tivozanib (16%). As this was an open-label study, patients and investigators were aware
of the study therapy received by the patient during the trial and subsequent decisions regarding
therapy were presumably made with this knowledge.

Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
Progression-free survival by BIRC was the primary efficacy endpoint of study TIVO-3. PFS was
defined as the time from randomization to the first evidence of disease progression or death
due to any cause. At the clinical cutoff date of 04 October 2018, 246 PFS events had occurred
(123 in each arm), with a median follow-up time for PFS of approximately 18 months on the
tivozanib arm and 16 months on the sorafenib arm.
Table 12 presents the results of the primary analysis of PFS by BIRC assessment in the ITT
population. A statistically significant improvement in PFS was observed in the tivozanib arm
compared to the sorafenib arm with a hazard ratio estimate of 0.73 (95% CI: 0.56, 0.95). The
estimate of median PFS was 5.59 months for the tivozanib arm and 3.88 months for the
sorafenib arm. The Kaplan-Meier curves of PFS are shown in Figure 2.

Reviewer Comment: Figure 2 shows that separation of the two PFS curves started around the
2nd scheduled tumor assessment time point, and the proportional hazards assumption may not
hold. A Schoenfeld residual test for non-proportional hazards had a p-value of 0.01 which
suggested a departure from the proportional hazards assumption. With that, the review team
also estimated PFS rates at 6 months and 12 months as supplementary: the estimates of 6month PFS rates were 45% vs. 37%, and 12-month PFS rates were 28% vs. 11% for tivozanib arm
and sorafenib arm, respectively.
The FDA notes that although the absolute magnitude of improvement in the median PFS
between arms was somewhat modest (<2 months), the fact that this improvement was against
an active control rather than a placebo or best supportive care arm was reassuring in terms of
the efficacy benefit attributed to tivozanib.

Reviewer Comment: It was noted that 31 patients (5 on the tivozanib arm vs. 26 on the
sorafenib arm) were censored within 8-weeks post randomization (the first tumor assessment
timepoint). Of the patients who were censored within the first 8 weeks after randomization, 0
patients in the tivozanib arm were censored due to receiving a new anti-cancer therapy without
evidence of progression of disease, compared to 13 of 26 patients in the sorafenib arm. This
suggests informative censoring in the sorafenib arm (e.g., patients or physicians may have
believed that sorafenib was an inferior treatment). The impact of this imbalanced early
censoring on efficacy results was not clear and thus evaluated in a sensitivity analysis (see the
FDA’s sensitivity analyses section).

FDA’s Analysis of Discordance between BIRC and INV Assessment of Disease Progression
Per the study design, disease status was assessed by both BIRC and INV. The primary PFS
analysis was based on the BIRC assessment, with the INV-based PFS as a supportive analysis.
Discordance rates between BIRC and INV assessments are shown in Table 15. The discordance
rate in terms of PFS status (event vs. censoring) was 19% in the tivozanib arm and 14% in the
sorafenib arm. The discordance rates are similar between arms.

FDA’s sensitivity analyses of PFS
1. Death post study discontinuation: Three patients on the tivozanib arm died within 16
weeks post randomization, but the death occurred after study discontinuation and
these 3 patients were not counted as PFS events in the primary PFS analysis. In this
sensitivity analysis, the 3 patients were considered as events at the time of death, and
the estimate of hazard ratio was 0.75 (95% CI: 0.58, 0.97).
2. Early censoring: As shown in Table 16, more patients randomized to the sorafenib arm
were censored within 8 weeks post randomization. To evaluate the impact of imbalance
57

Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
in the early censoring rates (3% vs. 15%), we performed a resampling analysis of PFS per
BIRC assessment by imputing early censored patients (patients censored at a timepoint
ч ϴ ǁĞĞŬƐ from randomization) using PFS data randomly sampled from patients
censored or progressed after 8 weeks and repeated this procedure for 1000 times. The
median of HR estimates based on the 1000 samples was 0.71 (95% CI: 0.55, 0.90) and
the median of PFS median difference between arms was 1.7 months.

3. Interval censoring: The observed benefit of tivozanib in median PFS was modest which is

analysis was conducted to explore the robustness of the primary analysis results of PFS
per BIRC assessment with respect to the imaging assessment interval. In this analysis the
estimate of stratified hazard ratio was 0.70 (95% CI: 0.54, 0.92) and the estimate of
median PFS was 5.32 months (95% CI: 3.78, 5.75) and 3.75 months (95% CI: 3.22, 5.09)
for the tivozanib arm and the sorafenib arm, respectively.

Reviewer Comments:
All of the subgroup analyses presented are considered exploratory or hypothesis-generating and
no formal statistical inference may be drawn.
No apparent outliers were observed in the subgroup analyses. In the subgroup of patients with
poor IMDC risk level (n=62), the point estimate of hazard ratio is 1.12; however, the sample size
is small and the wide confidence interval includes 1.
The FDA notes that the patients who received prior checkpoint inhibitor therapy appear to
derive consistent PFS benefit compared to those patients who did not receive prior checkpoint
inhibitor therapy. While the number of patients who received prior checkpoint inhibitor is low,
the results of this subgroup analysis may be considered supportive data with clinical relevance.
Since the approvals of frontline combination regimens containing checkpoint inhibitor (20182020) by FDA, data to support use of specific second- or third-line agents in the new treatment
paradigm is limited. The PFS-BIRC HR is similar to that of the overall population (0.67 95% CI,
0.39, 1.16]), reassuring the review team that tivozanib will have a role in the context of
contemporary regimens.

{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints
The study statistical analysis plan did not pre-specify any multiplicity adjustment method for
analyses of secondary endpoints. Although an O’Brien-Fleming boundary was used for alpha
allocation at the OS interim and final analyses, no alpha was pre-specified to be allocated to OS
after the primary endpoint PFS reached statistical significance. Therefore, analyses of all
secondary endpoints are considered exploratory.
Overall Survival
Overall survival was one of the secondary endpoints. In the initial SAP, only one interim analysis
was planned for overall survival to be conducted at the time of primary PFS analysis. After the
primary PFS analysis was conducted, the SAP was amended several times, and the final version
of SAP included two interim and one final analyses of OS. The first OS interim analysis was
performed at the time of primary PFS analysis and results numerically favored sorafenib with a
hazard ratio estimate of 1.12 (95% CI: 0.40, 1.51) based on 183 death events. The second
interim analysis was conducted after all patients were on study for at least 2 years. At the
second interim analysis (cutoff date: August 15, 2019), 227 death events occurred, and the
hazard ratio estimate was 0.99 (95% CI: 0.76, 1.29) which numerically favored tivozanib. The
final analysis was performed based on a data cutoff date of May 1st, 2020. As of that cutoff
date, 251 death events have occurred. The results of the OS final analysis are summarized in
Table 18 and the Kaplan-Meier plot is provided in Figure 3. The median follow-up time for
overall survival was 38 months on the tivozanib arm and 40 months on the sorafenib arm as of
the data cutoff date for the final analysis.

Reviewer Comments:
Per the applicant’s communication with the FDA on 22 May 2020, the applicant found the actual
date of death for one patient who was censored in the applicant’s OS final analysis due to
uncertainty of the death date at the time of the database lock. The review team used the actual
death date of that patient in the final OS analysis as presented in Table 17 and Figure 3.
As of the data cutoff date for the final OS analysis, 99 patients were censored, of whom 82
patients (46 tivozanib vs. 36 sorafenib) were still on study and under long-term follow-up for
survival. All but one patient was censored within 2 months before the cutoff date for the final OS
analysis. Therefore, the censoring distribution is acceptable.
The review team noted that the point estimate of OS hazard ratio is close to 1 and the median
estimate of the tivozanib arm is approximately 3 months shorter than that of the sorafenib arm.
Therefore, possible detrimental effect of tivozanib on OS is a concern. Given that the median is
only one timepoint on the survival curve and the difference at one timepoint may not reflect the
overall curves, the review team also conducted a post-hoc exploratory analysis using restricted
mean survival time (RMST) method to estimate the treatment effect. Using a cutoff time at
month 44 for RMST estimation, the difference of RMST estimate between tivozanib and
sorafenib is -0.4 months (95% CI: -3.7, 2.9). To explore RMST estimates at different cutoff
timepoints, RMST difference between the two arms by cutoff timepoint is shown in Figure 2.
Overall, the point estimates of RMST differences using various cutoffs are between -0.51 and
0.02 months (negative values favor sorafenib) and the confidence intervals are wide. Given that
the Kaplan-Meier plot shows overlapping survival curves, a hazard ratio less than one in favor of
61

Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
the tivozanib arm, and a small difference of OS between the two arms observed using the RMST
approach, it appears that tivozanib does not have an apparent detrimental effect on OS.
The review team also performed additional sensitivity analyses on final overall survival. The
unstratified OS analysis had a hazard ratio point estimate of 1.003 (95% CI: 0.78, 1.28) and the
OS analysis stratified by stratification factors data collected on eCRFs had a hazard ratio point
estimate of 0.97 (95% CI: 0.75, 1.25). Results are similar to the primary findings of OS.

Reviewer Comments: All of the subgroup analyses presented are considered exploratory or
hypothesis generating and no formal statistical inference may be drawn.
The point estimate of hazard ratio was around 1 in most of the subgroups analyzed. In the
subgroup of patients with one metastatic site and subgroup with high blood pressure at
baseline had their point estimate of hazard ratio as 1.77 and 1.54, respectively. However, the
sample size in both subgroups is small and the wide confidence interval includes 1.
FDA sent an Information Request to the sponsor for potential explanations for the OS HR of 1.54
among patients with baseline high sBP or dBP. The sponsor emphasized the possibility of small
random sample variability. The sponsor also conducted additional analyses that suggested that

there is no negative effect on OS for tivozanib in patients who are hypertensive at baseline.

conclusions. However, the use of subsequent therapy may be a possible confounding variable.”
The FDA also notes the hazard ratio and point estimate for patients with prior CPI, which
appeared similar to the overall population results. This is also reassuring and useful data as
combination CPI is currently the standard initial therapy in the frontline. Furthermore, as noted
in the Reviewer comment pertaining to Table 11, the number of patients who received a
checkpoint inhibitor was higher in the sorafenib arm compared to the tivozanib arm, which
would be expected to weight the efficacy results in favor of the sorafenib arm.

8.1.3 TIVO-1 (AV-951-09-301)
The Applicant submitted supportive efficacy data of tivozanib in patients with KI-naïve
advanced RCC based on data from a single clinical trial (TIVO-1). FDA conducted a full review of
this data in NDA 204408. These results were also presented and discussed at ODAC in May 2013
(see Section 3.2 of this review).
TIVO-1 was a multicenter trial that randomized 517 patients with advanced RCC without prior
therapy or having received one prior systemic cytokine therapy. Patients were randomized to
receive tivozanib versus sorafenib. The primary endpoint was PFS according to BIRC.
The study met the primary endpoint: PFS HR 0.80 (95% CI: 0.64, 0.99), median difference of 2.8
months (11.9 vs. 9.1 months). The primary analysis used a stratified analysis based on number
of prior treatments and number of metastatic sites. The reasons for censoring of patients were
generally balanced between the two treatment arms.
Overall survival was the first of the secondary endpoints. The final OS analysis used data
65
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
collected through August 27, 2012 when all patients had been followed for a minimum of two
years and when there were 219 OS events. This analysis demonstrated a decrement in OS of
tivozanib as compared to sorafenib that was not statistically significant with a hazard ratio of
1.25 (95% CI: 0.95, 1.62; p=0.11). While the median difference in survival was two weeks, the
review team stated that this estimate might not be reliable since few patients had died (118
patients in the tivozanib arm vs. 101 in the sorafenib arm) and the survival time estimate at the
fortieth percentile was four months longer in the sorafenib arm.
The review team at the time concluded that the OS analysis was greatly confounded by a
significant design flaw, which was one-way crossover: patients who progressed on sorafenib
could receive tivozanib through an extension study (AV-951-09-902), but patients who
progressed on tivozanib were not given any similar opportunity to receive second-line
therapy. Therefore, a differential use of subsequent targeted therapies in patients with
disease progression between the two arms was thought to have biased the results for overall
survival. One hundred and fifty-four (60%) patients randomized to sorafenib received
targeted therapy, the majority having crossed over to tivozanib, as compared to only 31 (12%)
patients from the tivozanib arm. While the assessment of PFS was a true comparison of the
tivozanib to sorafenib, the analysis of OS was thought to essentially be a comparison of
tivozanib against sequential targeted therapies. Thus, the overall survival results were
considered not interpretable by FDA.
The applicant presented several justifications to support their contention that the decrement in
overall survival was due to differential treatment of patients following disease progression. This
included the fact that the apparent toxicity of tivozanib was similar or less than sorafenib
(similar rates of adverse events leading to death), the absolute overall survival for the tivozanib
arm in this trial was similar to that of previous trials, and that there was not a decrement in
overall survival in patients receiving tivozanib post progression. While many of the Applicant’s
arguments were considered plausible, they were all post hoc and were thought to not
completely rule out the possibility that either sorafenib was a superior drug when it comes to
overall survival or that there was some aspect of tivozanib that accelerates demise following
disease progression.
8.1.4 Extension Study (AV-951-09-301)
As noted in Section 8.1.3, patients who progressed on sorafenib could receive tivozanib through
an extension study (AV-951-09-301) (see also Table 4). The extension study was a single-arm
study with no primary efficacy endpoint (N=161). The Applicant provided investigator-assessed
efficacy results: ORR of 18%, PFS of 11 months, OS of 21.6 months. These results represent
exploratory efficacy data in patients who received second-line tivozanib. Since the extension
study was never designed to assess efficacy and time-to-event endpoints are uninterpretable in
single-arm trials, FDA does not consider these results demonstration of substantial evidence of
effectiveness of tivozanib for the second-line indication.
66
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
The sponsor submitted this data as supportive efficacy data as justification for the proposed
indication statement of “patients with relapsed or refractory RCC.” As TIVO-3 did not enroll
patients with RCC whose disease had progressed on only 1 prior systemic regimen, FDA revised
the sponsor’s proposed indication for the approval from “patients with relapsed or refractory
RCC” to “patients with relapsed or refractory RCC with disease progression on or after two or
more prior lines of therapy.”

reviewed in Sections 8.1.1 and 8.1.2.

8.1.6 Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
The efficacy of tivozanib in patients with histologically or cytologically confirmed RCC that has
progressed on 2 or 3 prior systemic regimens is supported by a single, multi-national, openlabel randomized controlled clinical study, TIVO-3. The primary endpoint of BIRC assessed PFS
was prolonged in patients randomized to receive tivozanib (median estimate 5.59 months)
67
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
compared to sorafenib (median estimate 3.88 months). The FDA agrees that the observed
benefit on PFS was both statistically significant (HR estimate 0.73, p-value 0.016) and clinically
meaningful considering that the comparison is between tivozanib and an active control arm,
sorafenib. Sorafenib’s activity in this disease setting over placebo has been demonstrated in a
randomized controlled trial.[4] Results of sensitivity analyses and subgroup analyses of BIRCPFS supported the primary findings.
Survival curves of the two arms are close to each other with a hazard ratio of 0.97 (95% CI: 0.75,
1.24). No apparent detrimental effect of tivozanib on survival was observed. Results of ORR are
supportive, with PRs reported in 22% vs. 11% (tivozanib vs. sorafenib, respectively).
Tivozanib had previously been evaluated in TIVO-1, which randomized patients with treatmentnaïve advanced RCC to receive tivozanib versus sorafenib. Although TIVO-1 met its primary
endpoint, a trend towards a detrimental effect on survival was observed with tivozanib. An
ODAC vote on May 2, 2013 was 13:1 no:yes in response to the question “Has the Applicant
demonstrated a favorable benefit to risk evaluation for the treatment of renal cell carcinoma in
an adequate and well-controlled trial?”. Voting members cited concerns that included the
observed decrement in the HR estimate for OS and confounding factors in the trial design.
Despite the negative ODAC vote for tivozanib in the KI-naïve RCC setting and the subsequent CR
letter issued on June 6, 2013 for tivozanib based on review of TIVO-1, the review team felt that
the totality of the TIVO-3 data warranted an approval in this case given the PFS advantage,
supportive ORR, acceptable safety for the intended population, and the HR for OS that
ultimately did not demonstrate a decrement in patients who were required to have had
previous treatment with 2 prior lines of therapy. Although the Applicant had requested an
indication that included all patients with advanced renal cell carcinoma based also on extension
study AV-951-09-301, the FDA review team granted an indication that reflected only the TIVO-3
enrolled population, i.e. patients who progressed on two prior lines of therapy.
In conclusion, the efficacy of tivozanib for patients with RCC that has progressed on 2 or 3 prior
systemic regimens is supported by the result of the primary endpoint, secondary endpoints,
subgroup analyses, and sensitivity analyses. The results supported by scientific knowledge
about the effectiveness of other drugs in the same pharmacologic class, is adequate
demonstration of substantial evidence of effectiveness of tivozanib, as stated by the
Demonstrating Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness for Human Drug and Biological Products
Draft Guidance for Industry (December 2019).[21] The submitted evidence meets the statutory
evidentiary standard for regular approval of tivozanib for this indication.
