8.2.3 Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments
Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
The NDA submission contained all required components of the eCTD. The overall quality and
(b) (4)
integrity of the application was adequate for substantive review to be completed.
monitored sites for protocol adherence, completion and retention of eCRFs and source
documentation. For a summary of FDA Clinical Inspection findings, refer to Section 4.1 of this
review (Office of Scientific Investigation).
Categorization of Adverse Events
Adequate definitions for adverse events (AEs) were provided. All AEs were reported regardless
of causality. AEs were coded using MedDRA Version 19.1 or later and graded using NCI CTCAE
Version 4.03. TEAEs were defined as any AE with an onset date from the first dose of study
treatment through 30 days after last dose of study treatment.

protein.
d Coagulation parameters included prothrombin time (PT) and/or international normalized ratio (INR), and partial thromboplastin time (PTT).
e Total T3 (triiodothyronine) (optional), free T4 (thyroxine), and thyroid stimulating hormone (TSH) levels.
f Performed during every even-numbered cycle beyond Cycle 2 (i.e., Cycle 4, 6, 8 etc.).
g Urinalysis included protein, glucose, ketones, urobilinogen, occult blood, and microscopic sediment evaluation. A 24-hour urine for protein collected

h Prior to Cycle 1: Performed prior to first dose; not assessed again if ч 7 days since corresponding assessment performed at screening.
i Pregnancy test: Serum ɴ-hCG required for premenopausal, non-sterilized females.
j Screening CT/MRI scan performed up to 30 days prior to first dose as part of standard of care, and prior to signing of the informed consent if
performed in compliance with the protocol requirements. Screening scan included head, chest, abdomen, and pelvis.
k Not needed if last scan was within 30 days prior to End-of-Treatment (EOT) Visit and showed progressive disease (PD), as confirmed by independent
radiology review. Starting with version 5 of the protocol, all clinical response decisions were based on local radiological review.
l For patients with stable disease or objective response at the time of treatment discontinuation, who discontinued for reasons other than radiological
evidence of progressive disease, tumor assessments continued to be collected (every 2 months during the first year [beginning from the date of the last
scan performed prior to treatment discontinuation], every 3 months during the second year, and every 6 months thereafter) until disease progression
confirmed by independent radiology review or start of another therapy. A ±3 day visit window applied to tumor assessments for patients who
discontinued study drug. Starting with version 5 of the protocol, all clinical response decisions were based on local radiological review.
m
Two blood samples for pharmacokinetic (PK) analysis were collected at the following time points from all patients: Cycle 1, Day 1-4 hours post-dose
and Cycle 1, Day 15- pre-dose (within 2 hours before dosing).
n After the 30-Day Follow-up visit, patients were contacted once every 3 months by the site to collect long-term survival data. Additional contacts may
have been made to support key analyses, such as the analysis of overall survival at the time of the final PFS analysis, and the final analysis of overall
survival. A ±7 day window applied for the scheduled long term follow-up contacts.
o A ±2 day window applied to all visit procedures except study drug administration.
p The duration of treatment for tivozanib is 21 of 28 days, and for sorafenib is 28 of 28 days.
q All patients had an EOT visit when they permanently discontinued any further study treatment. The EOT visit occurred up to 7 days after the last dose of
study drug. Assessments performed within 3 days of the EOT visit did not need to be repeated and may have been documented as the EOT assessment
(except for the response assessment which did not need to be repeated if performed within 30 days and showed progressive disease). Patients starting
new anti-cancer therapy after the last dose of study drug completed the EOT Visit assessments in advance of starting the new anti-cancer therapy.
r Assessments obtained/ performed/ measured as clinically indicated.

8.2.4 Safety Results
Deaths
All deaths on the TIVO-3 study are summarized in the table below. A total of 29 deaths occurred
within 30 days of last study treatment and 21 of these deaths were associated with an AE: 12
patients (7.5%) in the tivozanib arm and 9 patients (5.3%) in the sorafenib arm.

*These are grouped together because the narratives contained little information and the reviewer’s assessment is that the cause
of death is unclear.

Narratives for the TEAEs, excluding “neoplasm progression,” leading to death are provided
below. None of the AEs leading to death were considered “related” to tivozanib by the
investigator. Of the 12 TEAEs leading to death, half (6/12) occurred concurrently with
documented disease progression which may have contributed to death.
According to this reviewer’s assessment, 4 of the remaining 6 cases may have been related to
(b) (6)
tivozanib. Patient
pneumonia is unlikely to be related to the drug because 1-2 doses
of a drug that is dosed daily continuously is typically not adequate exposure to cause an
(b) (6)
infection. Patient
multiple organ dysfunction syndrome is also unlikely to be related to
the drug because the direct cause of death was that he stopped drinking and eating, which
occurred a few weeks after stopping tivozanib. Therefore, the 4 remaining patients whose
deaths were due to an AE which did not occur in the context of disease progression may have
been related to the study drug, as the narratives did not provide information to suggest
otherwise.

69-year-old man with PMH of aortic valve replacement: on
day 90, the patient had symptoms of stroke. MRI was
suggestive of possible embolic event. Started therapeutic
enoxaparin. Approximately 3 weeks later, tivozanib was
permanently discontinued due to PD. Two weeks after the
last dose, he fell and had subdural hematoma.
59-year-old woman with metastases to bones and liver, prior
treatment with sunitinib, axitinib, and nivolumab, was
hospitalized for pneumonia on study day 2.
zĞƐ
79-year-old man with metastases to lung had
cachexia/general deterioration for the first 1.5 months on
study leading to tivozanib discontinuation on day 46. Twelve
days later, the patient died but there was no explanation of
the cause of respiratory failure.
51-year-old woman with metastases to bones and liver and
PMH of a “liver disorder since 1990” was found to have
hyperammonemia with confusional state on day 132. She was
treated in the hospital for 9 days but re-admitted for the
same reason on day 153. She died day 169 due to hepatic
failure, related to disease progression and underlying
unspecified liver condition.
zĞƐ
74-year-old man with metastases to bone and liver, PMH of
COPD since 2007; hypertrophic cardiomyopathy, HTN, and TIA
2016, had stable disease on tivozanib for the first 4 months
(through day 127). Day 101: grade 3 COPD and CHF
exacerbation, grade 3 HTN. Discharged 12 days later. Day 142,
he died without any anticipatory symptom.
60-year-old woman with metastases to liver and lungs and
PMH of DM, HTN, and hypothyroidism, had stable disease for
the first 4 months (through day 114). On day 143, she had
grade 2 dyspnea and fever, which led to discontinuation of
tivozanib. Symptoms worsened with discontinuation. She died
3 weeks later.
Reviewer’s assessment: since the patient had disease in lung
and she worsened with discontinuation, the narrative
suggests her symptoms were due to progression which may
not have been shown radiographically.

61-year-old man with left temporal lobectomy and RT to brain
and intrabronchial RT to lung had stable disease on tivozanib
for the first 4 months (through day 114). Day 164: ischemic
stroke which led to pneumonia 3 days later. He died 2 days
later from respiratory failure and pneumonia. Reviewer’s
assessment: Each patient should have only one grade 5 AE.
Therefore, this reviewer only counts pneumonia, because it is
a more specific term and to avoid counting patients twice,
when summarizing grade 5 AEs.
62-year-old man without history of hypertension or other risk
factors had well-controlled blood pressure throughout the
first 5 months of tivozanib. He had progression of disease on
day 113 and discontinued therapy on day 159. Day 162: CVA.
Died 2 weeks later.
Reviewer’s assessment: The narrative does not contain
adequate information to discern the cause of CVA, but PD with
hypercoagulability due to cancer is a potential cause.
51-year-old man with metastases to liver and pancreas was
hospitalized on day 29 for hyperbilirubinemia (direct bilirubin
4.4 mg/dL), bone pain, vomiting, severe depression.
Permanent discontinuation of study drug that day. He
stopped drinking, eating, and taking medications a few weeks
later which led to death on day 50.
Reviewer’s assessment: The narrative is unclear but it appears
that the patient may have been depressed, which may have
been partly due to disease progression, which led to
malnutrition.
66-year-old man with history of CHF, COPD, and HTN was
taking amiodarone, furosemide, enoxaparin for prophylaxis
cardiac comorbidities. On day 74, he was hospitalized for lung
infection due to PD. COPD exacerbation and delirium
resulted. Tivozanib was interrupted; 20 days after last dose,
he had cardio-respiratory arrest due to lung infection
68-year-old man with metastases to liver had scans
demonstrating PD (per both investigator and independent
review) after 10 months of tivozanib. Tivozanib discontinued
on day 310. Day 330: U/S showed stone in duct. Direct bili
was 235 U/L (13.7mg/dL). Patient died the next day. Autopsy
performed but results not provided.
Reviewer’s assessment: The sequence of events suggests that
progression of liver metastases was the cause of death,
because scans unequivocally showed PD 6 weeks prior to

Reviewer comment: As noted in Table 25, the Grade 5 adverse event Preferred Term was
“Neoplasm progression.” After review of the narratives, this reviewer agrees that the 4 deaths
were due to progressive disease. These are not included in the table above or in the label for the
purpose of consistency with other patients who died due to disease progression. These other
patients (n=59) were coded as having DTHREA (reason for death) in the ADSL dataset of
“DISEASE UNDER STUDY” and would not have any Grade 5 adverse events in the ADAE dataset.
As noted in Table 24, the Grade 5 adverse event Preferred Term was “Neoplasm progression.”

disease. These are not included in the table above or in the label for the purpose of consistency
with other patients who died due to disease progression. These other patients (n=59) were
coded as having DTHREA (reason for death) in the ADSL dataset of “DISEASE UNDER STUDY”
and would not have any Grade 5 adverse events in the ADAE dataset.

dysphonia, cough, and hypothyroidism. TEAEs that differ by >5% between the two arms and
that are higher in the sorafenib arm include: diarrhea and palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia
syndrome.

The denominator used to calculate the rate varied from 139 to 171 based on the number of patients with a baseline value and at
least one post-treatment value. Thus, percentages among lab abnormalities may be the same while the number of patients with
that lab abnormality are different (e.g., for phosphate, 165 patients were evaluable, so n=62 is 38% of the tivo arm, while for
sodium, 171 patients were evaluable, so n=62 is 36% of the tivo arm).

Reviewer comment: Laboratory abnormalities that differ by >5% and that were higher in the
tivozanib arm include: hemoglobin increased, creatinine increased, glucose increased, albumin
decreased, sodium decreased, calcium increased, and activated partial thromboplastin time
prolonged. However, for each of these laboratory parameters, the difference between the
tivozanib and sorafenib arm incidence of grade 3-4 events was less than 3%, with some of them
being higher in the sorafenib arm, suggesting that most of these differences between arms are
not clinically meaningful.
Both low and high hemoglobin were common. The upper quartile for hemoglobin values among
all patients in TIVO-3 ranged from 15.6 to 21.2 mg/dL. The lower quartile ranged from 8.3
mg/dL to 13 mg/dL. Increased hemoglobin has been noted in association with VEGF inhibitors,
which may be due to stimulation of erythropoietin production
(https://ashpublications.org/blood/article/112/11/3453/59882/Increased-HemoglobinAssociated-with-VEGF).
Vital Signs
Systolic blood pressure changes from baseline ranged from -14.0 mmHg to 8.2 mmHg (tivozanib
arm) and -9.7 mmHg to 6.6 mmHg (sorafenib arm). Diastolic blood pressure changes from
baseline ranged from -0.7 mmHg to 7.4 mmHg (tivozanib arm) and -3.0 mmHg to 7.0 mmHg
(sorafenib arm).

Other than the effects on blood pressure, no clinically relevant trends in vital signs were evident

Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
The ECG data showed no evidence of any clinically meaningful effect of treatment on heart rate
or ECG morphology.
QT
The effect of tivozanib was evaluated in an open-label, multiple dose, single arm study in
patients with advanced solid tumors (Study # AV-951-10-112). The highest dose evaluated was
1.5 mg (tivozanib hydrochloride) once daily for 21 days, which covers the therapeutic exposures
of tivozanib. The data were analyzed using the exposure-response analysis as the primary
analysis.
No large (>20 msec) QTc prolongation effect of tivozanib was detected. However, FDA is
reluctant to draw conclusions of lack of an effect in an absence of a positive control or large
exposure margin, or an integrated nonclinical safety assessment conduct according to best
ƉƌĂĐƚŝĐĞƐ ;/, ^ϳď YΘ ϭ͘1 and 1.2).
Immunogenicity
Not applicable.
8.2.5 Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
Discussion of the Submission-Specific Safety Issues will focus on the Warnings and Precautions
of labeling. Issues are presented in the order listed in Section 5 of labeling, followed by other
AEs of interest. All AEs listed in the section below are reported in other labeling for drugs
targeting the VEGF pathway.
8.2.5.1

Hypertension and Hypertensive Crisis

Hypertension was the most common grade 3-4 event in TIVO-3 in either treatment arm. In both
TIVO-1 and TIVO-3, patients in the tivozanib arm had a higher incidence of hypertension
compared to patients in the sorafenib arm. This was true for all-grade events as well as grade 34 events.

Hypertension occurred in 454/1008 (45%) of patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the
proposed dose in the overall safety population, with 22% of the events > Grade 3. Median time
to onset of hypertension was 2 weeks (range: 0 – 192 weeks).
One patient in TIVO-1 inadvertently overdosed, taking 3 x 1.5 mg capsules daily for 2 days (9 mg
total) of tivozanib and experienced a death due to hypertension. Information about this death
due to tivozanib overdose is included in section 5.1 of the tivozanib label.
Section 5.1͕ ƚŚĞ tĂƌŶŝŶŐ Θ WƌĞĐĂƵƚŝŽŶ ĨŽƌ ,ǇƉĞƌƚĞŶƐŝŽŶ͕ ŽĨ ůĂďĞůŝŶŐ recommends providers to
“Control blood pressure prior to treatment with FOTIVDA. Monitor blood pressure after 2
weeks and at least monthly thereafter during treatment with FOTIVDA,” consistent with the
TIVO-3 protocol. Additionally, Section 2.2 recommends dose interruption followed by reduction
for grade 3 hypertension not controlled by anti-hypertensive therapy, and permanent
discontinuation for grade 4 hypertension, which is consistent with the TIVO-3 protocol as well.
8.2.5.2

Cardiac Failure

Cardiac failure occurred in 16/1008 (1.6%) of patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the
proposed dose in the overall safety population, with 10 (1.0%) events ш 'ƌĂĚĞ ϯ͕ ĂŶĚ 6 (0.6%)
events were fatal. This made it the most common adverse event leading to death.
Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 excluded patients with significant cardiovascular disease, including
symptomatic heart failure, uncontrolled hypertension, history of severe angina or acute
coronary syndrome within 6 months prior, or serious/uncontrolled arrhythmia. In both trials,
cardiac failure occurred at comparably low rates in the tivozanib vs. sorafenib arms.

8.2.5.6

Proteinuria

Proteinuria occurred in 81/1008 (8%) of patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the
proposed dose in the overall safety population, with 20 (2%) Grade 3 events, and ŶŽ 'ƌĂĚĞ ш ϰ
events. There were no nephrotic syndrome events reported. Of the 81 patients with
proteinuria, only 3 patients developed acute kidney injury (defined as acute kidney injury,
anuria, azotemia, oliguria, or renal failure). Thus, there is lack of strong evidence that
proteinuria is prognostic of deterioration in renal function.
Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 monitored for proteinuria by requiring a urinalysis on day 1 of every
cycle; if urinalysis dipstick showed protein 4+, a 24-hour urine collection was required. In TIVO3, the proportion of patients with proteinuria was higher in the tivozanib arm than in the
sorafenib arm. In TIVO-1, rates of proteinuria were comparable across treatment arms.

8.2.5.7

Thyroid Dysfunction

Hypothyroidism occurred in 115/1008 (11%) of patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the
proposed dose in the overall safety population, with 3 (0.3%) Grade 3 events and no 'ƌĂĚĞ ш ϰ
events. Hyperthyroidism occurred in 1% of patients.
Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 monitored for thyroid dysfunction by requiring thyroid function tests
on day 1 of every cycle. In both trials, the proportion of patients with hypothyroidism events in
the tivozanib arm compared to the sorafenib arm is more than double (24% vs. 11% and 10%
vs. 4.3%). Additionally, the risk of hypothyroidism appears to increase by more than two-fold
from the first-line setting to the third-line setting (from 10% to 24% among tivozanib-treated
patients, and from 4% to 11% among sorafenib-treated patients). This suggests residual
treatment effects from earlier lines of therapy.

8.2.5.8

Risk of Impaired Wound Healing

Among the 1008 patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall
safety population, no reports of impaired wound healing were reported. The sponsor has not
conducted any formal studies to characterize the risk of impaired wound healing. However, the
Office of Oncologic Diseases (OOD) has decided that all drugs claiming anti-VEGF inhibition
activity should state a duration of time for wound healing to hold the drug prior to surgery and
ƚŚĞ ƚŝŵĞ ƚŽ ƌĞƐƵŵĞ ƚŚĞ ĚƌƵŐ ƉŽƐƚŽƉĞƌĂƚŝǀĞůǇ ŝŶ ƚŚĞ tĂƌŶŝŶŐƐ Θ WƌĞĐĂƵƚŝŽŶƐ ƐĞĐƚŝŽŶ͘ dŚĞ
rationale is to provide consistent and proportional communication across drug products
regarding adverse reactions related to VEGF pathway antagonism.
88
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
After multi-disciplinary discussion, in the absence of clinical data, OOD’s policy is to recommend
withholding the drug for 5 half lives prior to scheduled surgery and for 2 weeks (and after
clinical assessment of adequate wound healing) after surgery.
The half-life of tivozanib is 111 hours. Therefore, the recommendation per labeling is to hold
tivozanib for 24 days prior to major surgery.
8.2.5.9

Reversible Posterior Leukoencephalopathy Syndrome

Reversible posterior leukoencephalopathy syndrome (RPLS, also known as PRES), a syndrome of
subcortical vasogenic edema diagnosed by MRI, has been reported in association with drugs
inhibiting the VEGF pathway.[22] It is unclear if this syndrome occurred in patients treated with
tivozanib due to lack of diagnostic imaging with MRI. However, due to the potential risk of RPLS,
section 6.1 of tivozanib labeling includes the sentence, “Clinically relevant adverse reactions in
ф ϭϱй ŽĨ ƉĂƚŝĞŶƚƐ ǁŚŽ ƌĞĐĞŝǀĞĚ &Kd/s ŝŶĐůƵĚĞĚ … delirium.”
The sponsor reported all patients with “Non-Infectious Encephalopathy” as potential RPLS
cases. These included Preferred Terms of delirium, encephalopathy, or toxic encephalopathy.
The proportion of patients with non-infectious encephalopathy was similarly low in either
treatment arm in both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 (1% in each arm in each trial).
8.2.5.10

FDA requested the sponsor to conduct a risk assessment of “your perception of risk associated
with tartrazine in tivozanib. This should include a review of your global safety database of
ĂůůĞƌŐŝĐ ƌĞĂĐƚŝŽŶƐ ƚŽ ƚŚĞ ƚĂƌƚƌĂǌŝŶĞ ŝŶ ƚŝǀŽǌĂŶŝď͘ zŽƵ ƐŚŽƵůĚ ĂůƐŽ ŐĞŶĞƌĂůůǇ ƉƌŽǀŝĚĞ ǇŽƵƌ
assessment overall of this risk.”
The sponsor performed a comprehensive cumulative review of the global safety database
representing patient exposure corresponding to 1,064 patient-years with commercial product
from the date of European Union approval (August 24, 2017) through November 23, 2020 for
any suspicion of allergic reactions to the tartrazine in tivozanib. Cumulatively through
November 23, 2020, there were a total of 10 events, the symptoms of which, while nonspecific, could be part of a hypersensitivity reaction. These events were reported as (1-2 events
each) mouth swelling, chills, peripheral swelling, pruritis, rash, skin necrosis, and skin ulcer.

89
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
There were no reports of anaphylaxis nor urticaria, and the events reported did not appear to
suggest allergic reactions. The sponsor concluded that the risk of allergic reaction was low.
FDA agrees that the risk of allergic reaction due to tartrazine sensitivity is low. Among the 1008
patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population,
141 (14%) of patients potentially had an allergic reaction. AE terms related to rash comprised
most of these "allergic reactions." Only 1 patient of 1008 had wheezing, which was Grade 1-2,
and none had asthma. Similarly, in TIVO-3, 16% of patients had potential allergic reactions
(dermatitis, dermatitis acneiform, drug eruption, erythema, erythema multiforme,
photosensitivity reaction, pruritus, rash, rash erythematous, rash generalised, rash macular,
rash maculo-papular, rash morbilliform, rash pruritic, skin exfoliation, swelling face, toxic skin
eruption, urticaria), with two Grade 3 events and no Grade ш 4 events. In contrast, 50% of
patients in the sorafenib arm had allergic reactions (again, mostly rash), with 15% Grade 3. The
only patient in TIVO-3 who had wheezing was in the sorafenib arm.
See also Skin Reactions below.
8.2.5.11

Skin Reactions

Skin reactions are not included in the Warnings and Precautions section of the tivozanib
labeling, but are a known adverse effect of kinase inhibitors. The proportion of patients with
skin reactions in TIVO-3 and TIVO-1 was noticeably lower in the tivozanib arms compared to the
sorafenib arms. The difference was accentuated in the rate of grade 3-4 rash in TIVO-3 (15% vs.
5.1%), which may underscore the additive effects of kinase inhibitors used in sequence that
have overlapping dermatologic toxicities.

8.2.5.12

Aneurysm or Artery Dissection

Aneurysm or Artery Dissection is not a Warning/Precaution in tivozanib labeling. Among the
1008 patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety
population, aneurysm or artery dissection occurred in 3 patients. One of the events was grade
1-2 (aortic dilatation), one was grade 3 (aortic aneurysm, in a patient that was treated in TIVO-1
on the sorafenib arm and then crossed over to tivozanib) and one of the events was grade 5
(aortic aneurysm rupture). Since determination of causality was confounded in the grade 3

Dissection was sparse. The review team decided to include the grade 5 event in the
Hemorrhagic Events category.
8.2.5.13

Osteonecrosis

Osteonecrosis is not a Warning/Precaution in tivozanib labeling. Among the 1008 patients with
RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall safety population, osteonecrosis
occurred in 3 patients. All events were grade 1-2. However, osteonecrosis has been reported in

8.2.5.14

Gastrointestinal Fistula/Perforation

Gastrointestinal fistula or perforation is not a Warning/Precaution in tivozanib labeling. These
AEs have been reported following initiation of treatment with other VEGF-targeting drugs.
Among the 1008 patients with RCC treated with tivozanib at the proposed dose in the overall
safety population, gastrointestinal fistula occurred in 1 patient, which was grade 4. No patients
had perforations. Both TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 excluded patients with active peptic ulcer disease,
inflammatory bowel disease, ulcerative colitis, or other gastrointestinal condition with
increased risk of perforation; history of abdominal fistula, gastrointestinal perforation, or intraabdominal abscess within 4 weeks prior to first dose of study drug.

Reviewer comment: Blacks were under-represented in the demographics of TIVO-3 (n=2, both in
the sorafenib arm) vs the expected numbers based on the U.S. population. The incidence of
kidney and renal pelvis cancers is highest among Black men (24.7 per 100,000), followed by
White men (22.0 per 100,000).[23] As noted by Sims et al,[23] “In the United States, White
populations have the lowest prevalence of RCC compared to other ethnic groups, while Black
Americans suffer disproportionally from the adverse effects of RCC.. During the past two
decades, the incidence rate of RCC has significantly declined among Caucasians but has rapidly
increased among African Americans… A disparity in relative survival still persists between
African Americans and Caucasians patients with kidney cancer even when controlling for
treatment and other prognostic factors including stage, tumor size, and grade… The cause of
this disparity in mortality is unknown.” Possible factors include tumor genomic differences which
may predict different responsiveness to therapy.[24]
During the ODAC for TIVO-1, the underrepresentation of Black patients was discussed: “Another
committee member questioned the limited exposure data in African American patients,
94
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
describing these patients as having unique needs in the areas of kidney cancer and
hypertension.”
FDA has reminded sponsors that racial and ethnic minorities should be represented
appropriately in clinical trials. FDA consistently advises sponsors to ensure that clinical trial sites
include geographic locations with a higher concentration of racial and ethnic minorities to
recruit a diverse study population, referring them to the Guidance for Industry: Clinical Trial
Populations — Eligibility Criteria, Enrollment Practices, and Trial Designs at
https://www.fda.gov/media/127712/download
8.2.8

Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials

The sponsor conducted a hepatic impairment study (AV-951-12-118) between in hepatically
impaired patients and matched normal controls, both without cancer, using the Child-Pugh
categories of hepatic impairment, between August 2012 and September 2013. After a 10-hour
fast, 44 patients received 1 dose of 1.5 mg tivozanib hydrochloride. Pharmacokinetic results are
described in Section 19.4 (OCP Appendices).
Overall, 11/22 (50%) of patients with hepatic impairment and 5/22 (23%) of patients with
normal hepatic function at baseline had TEAEs. The highest grade TEAEs were two grade 3 AEs
of hypertension, one in a patient with moderate hepatic impairment and one in a patient with
severe hepatic impairment. Hypertension was also the most common TEAE in the entire study,
occurring in 25-37.5% (of 8 patients enrolled in each of the mild and moderate hepatic
impairment groups, and 6 patients in the severe hepatic impairment group) of patients with
mild, moderate, or severe hepatic impairment, and in 23% of patients with normal hepatic
function.
In conjunction with the pharmacokinetic results, these findings support the recommendation in
labeling to adjust the dosage in patients with moderate hepatic impairment and avoid use in
patients with severe hepatic impairment.

N/A
Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
No specific studies for overdose, drug abuse potential, withdrawal, or rebound are relevant for
this application.
Two patients in TIVO-3 overdosed on tivozanib due to patient error. The first patient took 32
days of tivozanib continuously without interruption, and suffered no adverse event related to
the exposure. The second patient took two capsules on the same day in cycle 1, Day 1. The
patient experienced a Grade 2 hypertension which lasted 5 days and then resumed tivozanib.
One patient in TIVO-1 overdosed due to patient error, which is described in Section 8.2.5.1.
8.2.10

Safety in the Postmarket Setting

Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
No safety concerns have been identified through postmarket experience. FDA specifically
requested the sponsor provide postmarket data regarding potential allergic reactions
(described in Section 8.2.5.10) and safety data in patients with severe renal impairment.
The sponsor identified no additional safety data through a search of postmarket experience in
Europe from the time of EU approval (August 2017) through February 23, 2020.
FDA also requested the sponsor to provide any additional postmarket safety data in patients
with severe renal impairment. The sponsor stated that there were no additional postmarketing
safety reports in this population. Although no patients with severe renal impairment (CrCl 15-29
mL/min) were enrolled in TIVO-3, FDA ultimately decided that no dosage modification is
recommended for patients with mild to severe renal impairment. This was due to lack of
change in PK/exposure in patients with severe renal impairment (see Clinical Pharmacology
section), relatively low renal excretion of the drug, and lack of extraordinary toxicity in the 2
patients with borderline severe renal impairment in TIVO-1.
Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
TIVO-3 excluded patients with several baseline factors that might be expected to raise the
likelihood of developing adverse events, ŝŶĐůƵĚŝŶŐ K' ƉĞƌĨŽƌŵĂŶĐĞ ƐƚĂƚƵƐ ш Ϯ͕ ƐĞƌƵŵ
creatinine > 1.5 × ULN, untreated brain metastases, cardiovascular disease, poorly-controlled
hypertension, and history of recent thromboembolism. The use of tivozanib in these patients
could constitute a safety concern in the postmarketing setting. However, risk mitigation for
many of these comorbidities is addressed in labeling, and FDA has not identified a relevant

96
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
scientific question that would be effectively answered using postmarket data. Thus, FDA has
not required the sponsor to conduct additional postmarket safety studies.
8.2.11

Integrated Assessment of Safety

FDA’s integrated assessment of safety focused on the 1008 patients with RCC who received the
proposed dose of tivozanib. The risks associated with tivozanib in this overall safety population
are described in section 8.2.5, Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues. The safety profile in
the overall safety population was generally similar to the safety profile in TIVO-3, with the
exception of thyroid dysfunction. Patients who were not treated in one of the phase 3 trials
(TIVO-1 or TIVO-3) likely were not monitored with thyroid function tests as closely and did not
have as much exposure to study drug, which may explain the higher rates of thyroid
dysfunction in tivozanib-treated patients in TIVO-1 and TIVO-3 compared to the other patients
in the overall safety population.
The safety profile of tivozanib was adequately assessed in the submitted database.
At minimum, the toxicity was within range for this patient population, with adverse reactions
manageable with dose reduction, temporary treatment discontinuation, and/or standard
medical care. In fact, the lower rates of dose reduction, interruption, or permanent
discontinuation, supported by the overall treatment bother results from analyses of TIVO-1 PRO
results, demonstrate a likely overall improved tolerability over an accepted standard-of-care
according to several clinically relevant metrics. Furthermore, the toxicity profile is consistent
with other drugs affecting the VEGF pathway, which increases the familiarity of providers with
risk management practices in this patient population.

8.3

Statistical Issues

The FDA efficacy evaluation was based on data from the TIVO-3 study. Study TIVO-3 met its
primary objective of PFS per BIRC assessment. The PFS results were consistent across sensitivity
analyses, and no outliers were observed in the subgroup analyses. In addition, no apparent
detrimental effect of tivozanib on survival was observed. There are no major statistical issues
with this application.

8.4

Conclusions and Recommendations

The review team recommends regular approval for tivozanib. Tivozanib is a kinase inhibitor
indicated for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory advanced renal cell
carcinoma (RCC) following two or more prior systemic therapies. Overall, the similar efficacy
observed between tivozanib and sorafenib in TIVO-3, treatment with tivozanib at the
recommended dose will provide an additional treatment option for patients with relapsed or
refractory RCC following at least 2 prior lines of therapy. The benefit to patients and providers
of an additional treatment alternative is augmented by the slightly different toxicity profile of
97
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
tivozanib in comparison to other approved treatments for this patient population, including
lower rates of diarrhea and skin reactions, potentially better overall tolerability, and the unmet
need in third-line RCC.

12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
The Division of Risk Management and review team agreed that a REMS is not necessary to
ensure the benefits outweigh the risks of tivozanib. This decision was made after considering
several factors: the risks associated with tivozanib are well-described in Sections 5 and 6 of
labeling, the life-threatening nature of the disease of relapsed/refractory advanced RCC, the
limited treatment options available, and the clinically meaningful efficacy results seen in TIVO3.

19.3 Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
The nonclinical studies for tivozanib hydrochloride were reviewed on May 24, 2013 under NDA
# 204408 (Applicant-owned) for the the treatment of patients with advanced renal cell
carcinoma (RCC).

1 Executive Summary
1.1 Introduction
NDA 204408 for tivozanib was submitted to the U.S. Food and Drug Administration as a New
Drug Application (NDA) for the indication of the treatment of patients with advanced renal cell
carcinoma (RCC). Tivozanib is a new molecular entity kinase inhibitor, which inhibits multiple
kinases including vascular endothelial growth factor receptor (VEGFR)-1, VEGFR-2, and VEGFR3. The recommended maximum human dose is 1.5 mg taken orally once daily, on a schedule of
3 weeks-on treatment followed by 1 week-off. Nonclinical pharmacology, pharmacokinetic and
toxicology studies have been submitted to support the approval of tivozanib for the proposed
indication.

1.2 Brief Discussion of Nonclinical Findings
Tivozanib hydrochloride is an anticancer agent of the Vascular Endothelial Growth Factor
Receptor (VEGFR) Tyrosine Kinase inhibitor class and is being developed for the treatment of
advanced renal cell carcinoma. In vitro cellular kinase assays demonstrated that tivozanib is an
inhibitor of VEGF receptors -1, -2, and -3 (IC50 0.107, 0.081, and 0.122 ng/mL, respectively) and
other kinases such as c-Kŝƚ ĂŶĚ W'&Zɴ Ăƚ ŚŝŐŚĞƌ /50’s (0.83 and 0.88 ng/ml, respectively). The
IC50 of these kinases is well below the Cmin value achieved in RCC patients (16 to 30.9 ng/mL)
following repeat dosing at the recommended dose at 1.5 mg/day, suggesting continuous
inhibition of these kinases throughout the treatment period.
In vivo pharmacodynamic studies in mice and rats bearing human or murine xenograft tumors
demonstrated that tivozanib inhibited tumor growth at a dose of 0.2 mg/kg in rats, via
inhibition of angiogenesis and vascular permeability. This dose is associated with a plasma
levels of tivozanib in rodents (Cmax of 70 - 108 ng/ml; AUC of 1640 ng*hr/ml), which were within
range of the Cmax and AUC achieved in RCC patients following repeat dosing at the
recommended dose of 1.5 mg/day (Cmax was 67.5 - 94.3 ng/mL; AUC0-24 was 1180 -1641
ng*hr/mL).
The selection of animal models to be used in toxicology and safety pharmacology studies (rats
and monkeys) was based on PK and/or metabolic profiles of tivozanib. The oral route was
utilized in nonclinical studies as this is the intended clinical route of exposure. The tivozanib
toxicokinetic profile following repeat oral administration was approximately linear in rats and
monkeys, with Cmax and AUC increasing in an approximately dose proportional manner. No
gender differences were shown in the rat or monkey. Interspecies differences in the rate of
absorption (Tmax) and elimination half-life (t1/2) were reported. The elimination t1/2 in rats (9-45
hours) was greater than that reported in monkeys (8-12 hours), but was considerably shorter
than that reported for humans (4.5-5.1 days). To account for these differences, the dosing
regimen in the nonclinical toxicology studies was daily as compared to the clinical regimen
(once daily, 3 weeks on and 1week off). In in vitro distribution studies conducted using animal
118
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
or human plasma, it was shown that tivozanib bound to plasma proteins at > 97.6%, and
protein binding was predominantly to albumin, with low biŶĚŝŶŐ ƚŽ ɲ-1-acid glycoprotein.
Tivozanib mainly partitioned into the plasma/serum component of blood. In vivo metabolism
studies demonstrated that the parent compound (tivozanib) was the major component in
serum and feces of rats and monkeys. Tivozanib appeared to be metabolized mainly by
oxidation, demethylation and glucuronidation. All prominent human metabolites (M1 through
M24) were present in both rat and monkey, with the exception of M11, which was unique to
human hepatocytes (minor >0.5%), and M10, which was not observed in rat hepatocytes. M14
was present at >10% and was designated as a major metabolite in human, monkey and rat.
Comparison of the metabolic profile of tivozanib in liver microsomes showed that monkey most
closely resembled the human, followed by the rat. These data provided support for species
selection. Excretion studies in rats and monkeys showed that tivozanib was mainly excreted in
feces in both species, and through the bile in rats.
In safety pharmacology studies, no effects on respiration rate, tidal volume, neurobehavioral
endpoints or physiological parameters were reported in rat studies. In single and repeat-dose
cardiovascular studies in telemetered cynomolgus monkeys, a dose of 3 mg/kg (HED= 58
mg/day; 39-fold of the recommended human dose) resulted in increases in systolic, diastolic

was not shown to be mutagenic, aneugenic or clastogenic; however, tivozanib produced

In repeat-dose general toxicology studies, the toxicity profiles of tivozanib in the rat and
monkey showed some overlap. The predominant toxicities in both species were associated
with the following systems/organs: adrenal gland, gastrointestinal system (stomach,
duodenum, and pancreas), kidney, bone (femur and tibia), liver, brain, pituitary gland, heart,
incisors and reproductive system (ovaries and uterus). Clinical pathology changes and body
weight decreases were also reported in both species.
Oral administration of tivozanib to rats for 13 weeks resulted in mortalities and severe clinical
observations (clinical signs typically associated with morbidity, malocclusion, missing teeth and
white teeth) at ĚŽƐĞƐ ш Ϭ͘ϭ ŵŐͬŬŐͬĚĂǇ ;,сϬ͘ϵϳ ŵŐͬĚĂǇ͖ Ϭ͘ϲϱ-fold of the recommended
human dose). The observed dental toxicity correlated with microscopic findings in the incisors
of the rats which may have contributed to the reported decreases in body weights and food
consumption. The effect on incisors is unlikely to be relevant to targeted patient population
(adults) due to the difference in incisor growth patterns in rats and humans. Clinical pathology
ĐŚĂŶŐĞƐ ǁĞƌĞ ƌĞƉŽƌƚĞĚ Ăƚ ĚŽƐĞƐ ш Ϭ͘Ϭϯ ŵŐͬŬŐͬĚĂǇ ;,сϬ͘Ϯϵ͖ Ϭ͘ϭϵ-fold the recommended
human dose) and included changes in hematology (increases in RBC and WBC parameters),
clinical chemistry (increases in iron metabolism, liver parameters, LDH/creatinine, triglycerides
and renal parameters and decrease in protein parameters) and urine parameters (increases in
protein, urobilinogen, bilirubin and hyaline casts). ƚ ĚŽƐĞƐ ш Ϭ͘ϭ ŵŐͬŬŐͬĚĂǇ (HED=0.97; 0.65fold the recommended human dose) the target organs with significant toxicity were the adrenal
glands, kidney, bone-femur, liver/ bile duct, brain, pituitary, incisors, pancreas and duodenum.
Less pronounced (lower incidence and/or severity) microscopic findings were reported in the
heart, reproductive organs, bone marrow, adrenal medulla and stomach. Partial recovery of

Oral administration of tivozanib in rats for 26 weeks produced non-adverse decreases in body
weights, abnormal whitening of the teeth and changes in hematology/clinical chemistry at 0.08
mg/kg/day. No test article-related findings were reported by the end of the treatment or the
recovery periods. The NOAEL was established to be the highest dose tested, 0.08 mg/kg/day
(HED = 0.78g/day; 0.5-fold of the recommended human dose). The doses assessed in this study
were low, and given the lack of appreciable toxicity, were considered insufficient to enable an
adequate determination of dose-response relationships.

gastric system (stomach, duodenum and pancreas), reproductive system (ovary and corpora

majority of the findings were recoverable after a 2- or 5-week recovery period. The NOAEL was

In a 39-week oral toxicity study in monkeys, the highest dose tested 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=1.95
g/day; 1.3-fold of the recommended human dose) was well tolerated and considered to the
established NOAEL. As with the 26-week rat study, the doses assessed in the 39-week monkey
study were low, and given the lack of appreciable toxicity, were considered insufficient to
enable an adequate determination of dose-response relationships.
Ocular and cutaneous phototoxicity studies with tivozanib in rats did not show skin or ocular
phototoxicity.

2.6 Proposed Clinical Population and Dosing Regimen
The proposed tivozanib hydrochloride regimen for the treatment of patients with advanced
RCC is 1.5 mg daily for 3 weeks (21 days) followed by a 1-week (7 days) rest period. When dose
reduction is needed, the dose may be reduced to 1.0 mg daily for 3 weeks followed by a 1-week
rest period.

4.1.1 Mechanism of action:
Tivozanib hydrochloride (referred to as KRN951, AV-951 or tivozanib) is an anticancer agent of
the vascular endothelial growth factor receptor (VEGFR) Tyrosine Kinase inhibitor class which is
being developed for the treatment of advanced renal cell carcinoma (RCC). In vitro biochemical
and cellular based kinase assays demonstrated that tivozanib inhibits phosphorylation of
vascular endothelial growth factor receptors (VEGFR) -1, -2 and -3 (IC50 of 0.21, 0.16 and 0.24
nM, respectively), and inhibited C-kit and PDGFR ɴ at higher concentrations (IC50 of 1.63 and
1.72 nM, respectively). Other kinases were inhibited at IC50 ш 229 nmol/L. Tivozanib in in vivo
tumor model studies was shown to inhibit angiogenesis and vascular permeability in tumor
tissues at doses of 0.2 mg/kg/day and 5 mg/kg/day in rats and mice, respectively. Tivozanib
administration resulted in anti-tumor effects, including tumor necrosis, growth inhibition,
hypoxia and decreased angiogenesis.

Overall summary:
In vitro studies demonstrated that tivozanib is an inhibitor of VEGFRs and other kinases (e.g., cKit and PDGFR ɴ).
x Tivozanib was tested in an in vitro biochemical kinase assay using a panel of 26
recombinant kinases at concentrations ranging from Ϭ͘ϯ ŶD ƚŽ ϭϬ ʅD. Tivozanib inhibited
the phosphorylation of VEGF receptors -1, -2, and -3, with IC50 values of 30, 6, and 15 nM,
respectively.
131
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
x Tivozanib at concentrations ranging from 0.1 to 100 nM, was screened in an in vitro
biochemical kinase assay using a panel of 129 recombinant kinases. At concentration of

x In an in vitro cellular kinase assay performed in cultured human umbilical vein endothelial
cells (HUVEC), tivozanib inhibited the activation of VEGF pathway by blocking the
phosphorylation of VEGFR-1 (IC50 of 0.21 nmol/L) and VEGFR-2 (IC50 of 0.16 nmol/L).
Tivozanib also inhibited VEGFR-3 autophosphorylation via VEGF with IC50 of 0.24 nmol/L in
the murine 3T3 cell line. Activity against other kinases was also reported, as tivozanib
inhibited c-Kit and PDGFR ɴ with IC50’s of 1.63 nmol/L and 1.72 nmol/L, respectively. In
other cell types, kinases including FGFR-1, Flt3, c-Met, EGFR and IGF-I R were inhibited

In in vivo tumor xenograft model studies in mice and rats, it was demonstrated that tivozanib
inhibited tumor growth of various tumor cell types via inhibition of angiogenesis and vascular
permeability.
x

The inhibitory activity of tivozanib on tumor growth through VEGFR inhibition was
supported by data from an in vivo study in which the inhibition of human tumor cell
growth (A549, Calu-6, HT29, Colo205, SW620, DU145, LNCaP and PC-3) by tivozanib was
evident, but at much higher concentrations (IC50 ш ϭ ʅmol/L) relative to what was reported
for VEGF-induced vascular endothelial cell growth (IC50 of 0.67 nmol/L). These data
suggest that the antitumor activity of tivozanib on human tumors is a result of antiangiogenic activity rather than direct growth suppression of cancer cells.
x Oral treatment with tivozanib at 5 and 20 mg/kg/day for 14 days resulted in tumor growth
inhibition of a panel of 8 human cancer cell lines (lung, prostate, colon and renal)
implanted in nude or ICR/SCID (renal model) mice. Tumor growth regression was reported
in tivozanib-treated SCID mice xenografted with murine lung tumor cells and mice
xenografted with human prostate carcinoma (DU145) tumor cells.
x Oral treatment for up to 14 consecutive days with tivozanib (0.2 and 1 mg/kg/day) in nude
rat xenograft models bearing 15 different human cancer cell lines (pancreas, kidney, lung,
glioma, prostate, colon, breast, ovary and liver) resulted in inhibition of tumors in a dosedependent manner. At 0.2 mg/kg/day, tumor growth inhibition (TGI) was evident in 11
out 15 of the cell line models ;d'/шϱϬйͿ; and at 1 mg/kg/day in 14 out 15 models (d'/ ш
86%).
x In a human lung carcinoma (A549) nude rat model treated orally for 7 days at 1 mg/kg/day
and immunostained with a vascular endothelial cell-specific marker, intratumor
angiogenesis inhibition was evident by the reduction in intratumor vascular endothelial
cells. In another study using a similar rat model, the quantity of Evans blue dye (identifies
vasculature permeability) in tumor tissues was evaluated. It was demonstrated that
tivozanib at 0.2 and 1 mg/kg/day, inhibited angiogenesis and vascular permeability.
x In a repeat-dose study (up to 5 days) in nude mice bearing murine breast HER2 xenograft
tumors, tivozanib at 5 and 20 mg/kg/day was reported to result in a dose-dependent

Study numbers 951-03, 951-09 and 951-41 (combined review)
Title: Assessment of VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 tyrosine kinase inhibiting effect of tivozanib.
GLP: No
Objectives: To evaluate the autophosphorylation inhibitory activity of tivozanib on
vascular endothelial growth factor receptor-1 (VEGFR-1), receptor-2 (VEGFR-2) and
receptor-3 (VEGFR-3).

Study #:
951-03,
951-09
and 95141

Methods:
x Lot Number: CDM-001
x Cell used for VEGFR-1: Transfect Mouse Fibroblast (NIH3T3-Flt-1).
x Cell used for VEGFR-2 and VEGFR-3: Human Umbilical Vein Endothelial Cells
(HUVEC)
x Tivozanib DMSO solution, was added to cell culture solution for final tivozanib
concentrations of 0.01 nmol/L to 300 nmol/L.
x After stimulation for 5 minutes with VEGF at a concentration of 50 ng/mL, cell
extract was prepared and VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 were immunoprecipitated using
anti-VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 antibodies, respectively. Immunoblotting was
performed with anti-phosphorylated tyrosine antibodies to the
immunoprecipitated fraction.
x The levels of VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 tyrosine phosphorylation, and the IC50 of the
inhibitory activity of tivozanib on VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 autophosphorylation in
this system were determined.
Results:
x Tivozanib inhibited tyrosine-phosphorylation of VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 in a
concentration-dependent manner.
x IC50 for tivozanib VEGFR-1, -2 and -3 autophosphorylation was determined to be
0.21 nmol/L, 0.16 nmol/L and 0.24 nmol/L, respectively.
x 95% confidence intervals were determined to be from 0.16 to 0.30 nmol/L, 0.13
to 0.20 nmol/L and 0.16 to 0.30 nmol/L, for VEGFR-1, -2 and -3, respectively.
Conclusion:
The Applicant concluded that tivozanib inhibited VEGFR-1, -2 and -3
autophosphorylation via VEGF with IC50 of 0.21 nmol/L, 0.16 nmol/L and 0.24 nmol/L,
respectively. The provided data supported this conclusion.

Objectives: To evaluate the autophosphorylation inhibitory activity of tivozanib on cMet, EGFR and IGF-I R in order to investigate specificity of tivozanib on receptor
tyrosine kinase.
Methods:
x Lot Number: CDM-001
x Cell Name: A431 (Human Squamous Carcinoma Cells) or HT29 (Human
colorectal adenocarcinoma).
x Tivozanib DMSO solution or DMSO was added to culture medium for final
concentrations of 3 nmol/L to100 μmol/L.
x HGF, EGF and IGF-I was added and cells were stimulated for 5-10 minutes at a
final concentration of 20-50 ng/mL.
x After stimulation, cells were immunoprecipitated using anti-c-Met, anti-EGFR or
anti-IGF-I R antibodies.
x Immunoblotting was performed with anti-phosphorylated tyrosine antibodies
to the immunoprecipitated fraction.
x The level of tyrosine phosphorylation, and the IC50 of the inhibitory activity of
tivozanib on c-Met, EGFR and IGF-I R autophosphorylation in this system were
determined.
Results:
Study # 951-23:
Tivozanib inhibited tyrosine-phosphorylation of c-Met via HGF in a concentrationdependent manner. The IC50 for tivozanib c-Met autophosphorylation was
determined to be 1.36 μmol/L and 95% confidence interval was determined to be
from 0.73 to 2.54 μmol/L.
Study # 951-34 and 951-36:
Tivozanib did not reach 50% or greater inhibition of EGFR or IGF-I R
autophosphorylation at the highest concentrations used in these studies.
Precipitation in culture medium was reported with tivozanib Ăƚ ĚŽƐĞƐ ш ϭϬ ʅmol/L. A
sigmoid concentration-inhibition curve could not be generated, therefore, IC50
calculations were not established.
Conclusion:
The Applicant concluded:
x Tivozanib inhibited c-Met autophosphorylation via HGF with IC50 of 1.36 μmol/L.
x Tivozanib inhibitory activity of EGFR and of IGF-I R autophosphorylation was
weak and therefore, IC50 were not calculated.
The provided data supported this conclusion.

Title: Antitumor Efficacy of tivozanib hydrochloride on the Nude Rat SubcutaneouslyImplanted Human Lung Carcinoma A549
GLP: No
Objectives: To evaluate the angiogenesis inhibitory activity of tivozanib, using a nude
rat model implanted with human lung carcinoma A549
Methods:
x Human lung carcinoma A549 tumor cells were implanted by subcutaneous
injection to female nude rats (F344/N Jcl-rnu).
x Treatment: orally administered once daily at dosages of 0.04, 0.2 and 1
mg/kg/day (Lot# CDM-001) for a continuous 7- day period.
x Tumors were extracted the day following the final administration, were then
sectioned and immunostained with anti-rat CD31 antibodies or stained with
,Θ͘
x Endpoints evaluated: tumor volumes were measured twice a week, daily body
weight and tumor condition were recorded twice weekly.
Results:
x Dosing at 1 mg/kg/day resulted in a significant inhibition of intratumor vascular
endothelial cell density (2.01% vs. 9.22% in the control group).
x Dosing at 1 mg/kg/day resulted in a decrease in tumor volume (22% and 40%
on days 4 and 7, respectively), and showed tumor antiproliferative activity with
inhibition of tumor growth of 50% or greater.
x Regression activity of 23% was reported at 1 mg/kg/day.
Conclusion:
The Applicant concluded that tivozanib administered orally at 1 mg/kg/day for 7 days
resulted in inhibition of intratumor angiogenesis in the implanted human lung
carcinoma A549 nude rat. The provided data supported this conclusion.

x Cell: Human prostate carcinoma DU145
x Animals: male BALB/cAJcl-nu mice
x Tumor cells were implanted by subcutaneous injection.
x Treatment: Orally.
Dosing schedule:

145
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

(excerpted from Applicant’s report)

x Schedule #1 was a 2 and 20 mg/kg dose of KRN951 per day for 10 weeks.
x Schedule #2 was a cycle of 2 and 20 mg/kg dose of KRN951 per day for two
weeks, then no dosing for two weeks, repeated three times.
x Schedule #3 was a cycle of 2 and 20 mg/kg dose of KRN951 per day for four
weeks, then no dosing for two weeks, repeated two times.
x Endpoints evaluated: Body weight, tumor volume, tumor weight, antitumor
efficacy, tumor antiproliferative activity and tumor regression.
Results:
x Tumor volumes were significantly decreased in all test article treated animals
compared to the control group.
x At 2 and 20 mg/kg/day, %TGI was greater than 150%.
x Schedule #1: KRN951 at 2 and 20 mg/kg/day sustained regression of the
tumor and on the day after the final dose, the relative tumor volume weight
(RVx) was less than 1 (0.64 and 0.23).
x Schedule # 2 and Schedule #3 (had no dosing periods): tumor growth was
reported during the periods when dosing was stopped but TGI were reported
when dosing was started again.
x On day 70 the control group had a relative tumor volume weight (RVx) of
7.15, in contrast all other dose groups with the different schedules had values
of less than 1 (RVx 0.23 㹼 0.99).
Conclusion:
The Applicant concludes that doses of 2 and 20 mg/kg/day after a repeat dosing (up
to 10 weeks) resulted in TGI. Even after a dosing rest period (2 weeks), tumor
inhibition was reported once dosing was initiated for the next treatment cycle. The
data suggest that no tolerance for KRN951 was developed. The data provided
supported this conclusion.

4.2

Safety Pharmacology

Overall Summary:
A total of five safety pharmacology studies were performed, one in vitro hERG study, two in
vivo cardiovascular studies in monkeys, one in vivo respiratory study in rats, and one central
nervous system study in rats.
The in vitro hERG Tail Current study for tivozanib hydrochloride was designed with a single
concentration. As such, a concentration response curve was not established (per ICHS7B), and
the study provided no information regarding IC50. Accordingly, this was not considered a valid
148
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
study. An in vivo telemetry study was performed in male cynomolgus monkeys that received a
single oral dose of KN951 (0.015, 0.3 or 3 mg/kg). A dose of 3 mg/kg resulted in increases in
systolic, diastolic and mean arterial blood pressure 90 minutes post-dose. There were no test
article-related effects on heart rates, RR, PR, QT, QTcF, QTcQ intervals and the QRS duration at
any dose level. In an in vivo repeat-dose telemetry study (1 or 3 mg/kg/day for 28 or 14 days,
respectively) in male cynomolgus monkeys, increases in systolic, diastolic and mean blood
pressure were evident at 3 mg/kg/day. As such the single- and repeat-dose telemetry study
findings in monkeys at 3 mg/kg were consistent. ƚ ĚŽƐĞƐ ш ϭ ŵŐͬŬŐͬĚĂǇ in the monkey,
decreases in heart rate, cardiac output, renal arterial blood flow and cardiac arterial blood flow
were reported. A decrease in vascular relaxation response and decrease in sensitivity to
acetylcholine were also reported at ĚŽƐĞƐ ш ϭ ŵŐͬŬŐͬĚĂǇ ;, ϭϵ ŵŐͬĚĂǇͿ͘
An in vivo respiration study in male rats receiving a single oral dose of tivozanib (0.3, 3, 30, and
400 mg/kg) showed no effects on respiration rate, tidal volume, neurobehavioral endpoints or
physiological parameters (Irwin test).
-

The in vivo cardiovascular system (monkey) NOAEL: 0.3 mg/kg/day (HED 5.8 mg/day).
The in vivo central nervous system (rat) NOAEL: 0.3 mg/kg/day (HED 2.9 mg/day).
The in vivo respiratory system (rat) NOAEL: 0.3 mg/kg/day (HED 2.9 mg/day).
Title: Effect of KRN951 on hERG Tail Current Recorded from Stably Transfected
HEK293 Cells
Conducting Laboratory:

Study #:
DRZZ1020

(b) (4)

Date of study initiation: 07 July, 2003
GLP compliance: zĞƐ
QA statement: zĞƐ
Drug and lot #: KRN951, CDM-001
Objective and Methods
The objective of this study was to examine the effect of the KRN951 (3 μg/mL) on
the hERG tail current recorded from stably transfected HEK293 cells with hERG
cDNA.
KRN951 was tested at a single concentration. The concentration tested (3 μg/mL)
was the maximum soluble concentration of KRN951 as determined in solubility
testing. E-4031 (100 nM) was used as a positive control and tested in two cells of
the vehicle and test article. The effect of KRN951 (3 μg/mL) was investigated in 56 cells and the vehicle (0.1% DMSO) was investigated in 5-6 cells.

149
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Results
x Analysis of formulation: The final concentration was determined to be
27.3% of the intended concentration (3 μg/mL). Therefore, a
concentration of 0.82 μg/mL was the actual concentration of the solution
applied to cells in the recording chamber.
x The positive control at 100 nM inhibited hERG tail current by 88.8%
(expected outcome).
x KRN951 inhibited hERG tail current by 28% versus 18.9% at the control
group. The difference was not statistically significant.
Conclusion
KRN951 at a concentration of 0.82 μg/mL, produced no inhibition of hERG tail
current.
Reviewer comment:
x Only one concentration was tested (at maximum soluble concentration).
Ascending concentrations should have been tested to establish a
concentration response curve (per ICHS7B, section 3.1.2).
x The final concentration used was 27.3% (0.82 μg/mL) of the nominal
concentration and the concentration did not produce inhibition of hERG
tail current.
x Given the lack of concentration response curve this study provides no
information regarding IC50 and therefore the study was not considered to
be valid.

Reviewer comment:
In contrast to the Applicant’s conclusion stating the minor rise after dosing in systolic,
diastolic and mean blood pressure was not of a large enough magnitude to be considered a
test article-related, this reviewer concludes that the observed increases in systolic, diastolic
and mean blood pressure at 3 mg/kg/day were of sufficient magnitude to be considered a
safety signal and test article-related. In addition, these results are in agreement with the
findings reported in a prior cardiovascular study (#DRZZ1019), in which the test article
produced increases in systolic, diastolic and mean blood pressure by up to 20%, 21% and
19%, respectively. The Applicant based their conclusion on standards established in the
protocol, where an effect was considered only if there was a rise of more than 15 mm Hg at
the end of dosing (3 day period), as compared to the average value of the 3 day period
before the start of dosing. But using the control group for comparison the magnitudes are
large enough to be test article-related.

5

Pharmacokinetics/ADME/Toxicokinetics

Overall Summary:
Tivozanib TK profile following oral administration was approximately linear in mice, rats and
monkeys, with Cmax and AUC increasing in an approximately dose proportional manner. No
significant gender differences in exposure were reported. The rate of absorption was moderate
and unaffected by dose level or duration of treatment in rats and monkeys, while, the rate of
absorption (Tmax) and elimination half-life (t1/2) were different between species.
In in vitro distribution studies conducted using animal or human plasma, it was shown that the
test article bound to plasma proteins at > 97.6%, and protein binding was predominantly to
albumin, with low biŶĚŝŶŐ ƚŽ ɲ-1-acid glycoprotein. KRN951 mainly partitioned into the
plasma/serum component of blood.
Quantitative data on the tissue distribution of test article-related radioactivity in whole body of
rats showed that the distribution of radioactivity was maximal at 4 hours post-dose. The highest
radioactivity tissue concentrations were measured in the preputial gland, harderian gland,
white fat and the liver. The concentrations in both the ovary and uterus were similar to the
blood levels, and those in testes were significantly less than in the blood. The majority of the
radiation was eliminated from tissues at 24 hours post-dose, and by 72 hours post-dose the
radiations levels were below the limit of quantification. In pigmented rats, melanin binding to
159
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
the choroid layer of the eye was apparent and levels decreased steadily over 120 hours postdose.
In vitro and in vivo metabolism studies with KRN951 were performed and showed that in
serum, the parent compound (KRN951) was the predominant circulating component (91% and
78% of the total radioactivity exposure in rat and monkey serum, respectively) with the
metabolites M4 (mono-oxidation)/M5 (N-oxide of tivozanib) co-eluting and M63 in rats only.
The parent compound was not detected in the urine, and metabolites detected in urine
represented less than 0.5% of the administered radioactive dose. In feces, the parent
compound was the main component and other metabolites detected represented less than 5%
of the administered radioactive dose. In vitro metabolism studies showed that KRN951
metabolism was low in all species studied, as was evident by the low hepatic clearance, and
that KRN951 appeared to be metabolized mainly by oxidation and demethylation. All
prominent human metabolites (M1 through M24) were present in both rat and monkey with
the exception of M11 (glucuronide), which was unique to human hepatocytes (minor >0.5%)
and M10 which was not observed with rat hepatocytes. M14 was present at >10% of total
radioactivity and was designated as a major metabolite in human, monkey and rat. The
metabolic profile of the monkey was the closest to human, followed by the rat. Comparison of
the metabolic profile of tivozanib in human, monkey, dog and rat liver microsomes showed that
monkey is the closest to human followed by the rat and dog is the most different. zWϯϰ ĂŶĚ
zWϭϭ ĂƌĞ ƚŚĞ ŵĂũŽƌ ŚƵŵĂŶ ŚĞƉĂƚŝĐ zW ŝƐŽĨŽƌŵ ŝŶǀŽůǀĞĚ ŝŶ ƚŚĞ ŵĞƚĂďŽůŝƐŵ ŽĨ tivozanib.
In rats and monkeys the main excretion route was through the feces, and to a smaller extent (23%) through urine.
Overall the data supported the use of rats and monkey in nonclinical studies, as these species
would be expected to provide adequate coverage for potential human metabolites and
associated toxicities.

Data generated from the bile duct-cannulated rats, suggested that [14C]KRN951 is also
excreted through the bile (up to 37% of the total radioactivity). Another non-biliary
route of excretion of tivozanib and/or its metabolites from the systemic circulation was
the gastrointestinal tract which showed approximately 40% of the radioactivity.
In monkeys, after an oral dose of [14C] KRN951, the radioactivity excreted in the urine
and feces, were 3.40% and 27.4%, respectively, within 24 hours postdose. These data
confirm that the main excretion route of the test article, through the feces, was indeed
similar in the rat and monkey.

Distribution studies:
x In an in vitro study conducted using animal or human plasma, it was shown that the test
article bound to plasma proteins of the rat (99.3%), human (99.7%) and monkey
(97.6%), with no apparent gender differences.
x In human plasma, the protein binding was mainly to albumin, witŚ ůŽǁ ďŝŶĚŝŶŐ ƚŽ ɲ-1acid glycoprotein.
x In vitro studies conducted with human blood indicated that KRN951 was mainly
partitioned into the plasma/serum component of blood. Similarly, the reported data
from the rat study suggested limited association of KRN951 within red blood cells.
x Quantitative data on the tissue distribution of test article-related radioactivity in whole
body of male and female albino and male pigmented rats showed that the distribution
of radioactivity was similar between the genders and strains. The extent of the tissue
penetrance was low, with tissue/blood ratios of less than 1 in the majority of tissues.
Maximal tissue concentrations were observed at 4 hours post-dose. The highest
radioactivity tissue concentrations were measured in the preputial gland, harderian
gland, white fat and liver. The lowest radiation levels were in the spinal cord and brain.
The concentrations in both the ovary and uterus were similar to the blood levels, and
those in testes were significantly less than in the blood. In albino rats, the majority of
the radiation was eliminated from tissues at 24 hours post-dose, and by 72 hours postdose the radiations levels were below the limit of quantification. In pigmented rats,
melanin binding to the choroid layer of the eye was apparent and levels decreased
steadily over 120 hours post-dose.

followed by the rat and dog. Metabolites formed by rat, dog and monkey were the same
as those produced in human liver microsomes with the exception of one metabolite in

species. Overall the data supported the use of rats and monkey in nonclinical studies, as
these species would provide adequate coverage for potential human metabolites and
potential associated toxicities.
zWϯϰ ĂŶĚ zWϭϭ ǁĞƌĞ ƚŚĞ ŵĂũŽƌ ŚƵŵĂŶ ŚĞƉĂƚŝĐ zW ŝƐŽĨŽƌŵ ŝŶǀŽůǀĞĚ ŝŶ ƚŚĞ
metabolism of KRN951.
Substantial levels of 14C-KRN951 desmethyl glucuronide metabolites were formed by
UGT isoenzymes. Metabolite M14, a desmethyl glucuronide metabolite, was the major
metabolite formed by six UGT enzymes. Other observed glucuronide metabolites were
M10 and M25. Since the major metabolite (M14) was formed by six different UGT
isoenzymes, inhibition of any one of these enzymes is not likely to cause substantial
drug-drug interactions.
KRN951 inhibited glucuronidation of UGT substrates 4-MU and p-NP to a small extent.
Therefore, tivozanib is not a potent inhibitor of UGT activities.

Single Intravenous Administration of [14C] KRN951
GLP: zĞƐ
Objectives: To evaluate the excretion of total radioactivity into urine, feces and
expired air following a single oral and a single IV administration of [14C]-tivozanib

{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
hydrochloride to male rats. In addition, this study evaluated the PK and
distribution of tivozanib hydrochloride to red blood cells.
Methods:
Male (treated orally and intravenously (IV)) and female (treated IV) rats were
treated based on the schedule listed in the table (below) and were housed in
individual glass metabolism cages. Urine, feces and expired air were collected:

(excerpted from Applicant’s report)

Results:
x The concentrations of radioactivity in the expired air trap were below the
limit of quantification at all collection time points.
x Following oral administration in male rats, the majority of the test article
was excreted in feces and urine by 72 hours post-dose as was evident by
the total radioactivity recovered (87.8% and 1.70%, respectively).
x IV administration of the test article to male and female rats resulted in
similar outcome to the oral administration, suggesting that the main
excretion route of the test article is fecal excretion.
x At the end of the collection period (168 hours postdose) for male and
female rats, a mean total of 95.7% and 93.9% of the dose was recovered in
urine, feces, cage wash, expired air traps and carcass following oral and IV
administration, respectively.
x Limited association of radioactivity with red blood cells was suggested
based on the data that following oral dose of the test article, the transfer
of total radioactivity to red blood cells was evident at the later time points
when radioactivity concentrations were low and the blood cell transfer
ratios increased with time.
x Following oral dose the mean Cmax and AUC were 1764 ng equiv/mL and
2279 ng equiv/mL and 29243 and 38732 ng equiv.h/mL for male and
female rats, respectively.
x Following IV administration, mean AUCinf values were 26019 and 29933 ng
equiv.h/mL in male and female rats, respectively.

Conclusion:
In rats administered an oral or IV dose of [14C] KRN951, the majority of test

Study #:
Pk0403

Title: Pharmacokinetic Study of AV951 – Biliary Excretion of [14C] AV951 in Rats
GLP: No
Objectives:
To evaluate the amount of [14C]KRN951 eliminated through the bile, after a
single intravenous administration, by measuring the amount of radioactivity in
the urine, feces and bile.
Methods:
Bile duct cannulated male rats were dosed intravenously once at 0.5 mg/kg with
[14C]KRN951 and radioactivity in urine and feces and bile were measured up to
72 hours post-dose.
Results:
At seventy two hours post-dose, greater than 80% of the radiation was excreted,
with the percentage of excretion into the feces (via gastrointestinal tract) and
bile being about the same at 39.8% and 36.6%, respectively and less than 5%
excreted in urine.
Conclusion:
The data suggested that in rats, [14C]KRN951 was excreted through the bile and
feces.

x The radio-HPLC analysis showed that the oral dose was absorbed and the
parent compound (tivozanib) was the predominant circulating
component representing an average of approximately 78% of the total
radioactivity exposure in serum. Major metabolites M4 (monooxidation)/M5 (N-oxide of tivozanib) co-eluting, represented an average
of approximately 17% of the total radioactivity exposure in serum. Minor
unknown metabolite M44 represented 1% of the total radioactivity
exposure in serum.

Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
x

x

In urine, an average of 3.40% of the administered radioactive dose was
eliminated within 24 hours postdose. Parent compound was not
detected in the urine. None of the metabolites detected in urine
represented an average of greater than 0.5% of the administered
radioactive dose. Except for M4/M5 co-eluting, all the other metabolites
observed in urine could not be identified.
In feces, an average of 27.4% of the administered radioactive dose was
eliminated within 24 hours postdose. Parent compound was the
predominant radioactive component (average of 20.6%) of the
administered radioactive dose. Each of the other metabolites detected
[M37/57 co-eluting, M42, and M7 (O-demethylation) represented an
average of less than 1% of the administered radioactive dose.

Conclusion:

circulating component (78% of the total radioactivity exposure in serum) with the
metabolites M4 (mono-oxidation)/M5 (N-oxide of tivozanib) co-eluting. The
parent compound was not detected in the urine and none of the metabolites
detected in urine represented an average of greater than 0.5% of the
administered radioactive dose. In feces, the parent compound was the main
component and each of the other metabolites detected [M37/57 co-eluting,
M42, and M7 represented an average of less than 1% of the administered
radioactive dose. In regard to excretion, the average radioactivity recovery in
urine and feces was 3.40 and 27.4% of the administered radioactive dose,
respectively.

Study #:
8242516

Title: Identification of Human UGT Isoenzymes Involved in the Metabolism of 14CTivozanib and Associated Inhibitory Interactions
GLP: No
Objectives:
To determine the involvement of specific uridine diphosphateglucuronosyltransferase (UGT) isoenzymes in the metabolism of 14C-tivozanib
using cDNA expressed human UGTs, and to determine the general inhibitory
potential of tivozanib towards microsomal UGT activity.
Methods:
x The identification of UGT isoenzymes involved in the metabolism of 14Ctivozanib were determined by incubation of the test article with cDNA
expressed human UGTs. The rate of disappearance of 14C-tivozanib or
metabolite formation was determined.
x The inhibitory effects of tivozanib on human hepatic UGT activity were
determined by measuring the effect of the test article on the metabolism
of UGT marker substrates, 4-methylumbelliferone and para-nitrophenol.
Results/Conclusion:
x Substantial levels of 14C-tivozanib desmethyl glucuronide metabolites
were formed by UGT isoenzymes.
x Metabolite M14, a desmethyl glucuronide metabolite, was the major
metabolite formed by six UGT enzymes. Other observed glucuronide
metabolites were M10 and M25. Since the major metabolite (M14) was
formed by six different UGT isoenzymes, inhibition of any one of these
enzymes was not likely to cause substantial drug-drug interactions.
x Tivozanib inhibited glucuronidation of UGT substrates 4-MU and p-NP to
a small extent. Therefore, tivozanib is not a potent inhibitor of UGT
activities.

Title: Whole Body Phosphor Imaging in Male and Female Rats Following a Single
Oral Administration of [14C] KRN951
GLP: zĞƐ
Objectives: To provide quantitative data on the tissue distribution of compound
related radioactivity in male and female albino and male pigmented rats
following a single oral administration of [14C] KRN951 at a target dose level of 1
mg/kg.
Methods:
x Twelve male albino, 4 female albino and 6 male pigmented rats were
used.
x Rats were dosed orally once with 1 mg/kg of [14C] KRN951 (10 mL/kg).
x Tissue concentrations of radioactivity were quantified from each of the
rats at different time points.
x An image representing the distribution of radioactivity across whole body
sections at each time point was taken.
x A whole blood sample collected from each rat immediately prior to
sacrifice was analyzed for radioactivity by liquid scintillation counting.
Results/Conclusion:
x The highest concentrations of radioactivity were observed in the contents
of the gastrointestinal tract.
x Distribution of radioactivity was similar between sexes and strains of rats.

Study #:
DRZZ1039

The extent of tissue penetrance was low, with tissue/blood ratios of less
than 1 in the majority of tissues.
Maximal tissue concentrations were observed at 4 hours post-dose.
Highest radioactivity tissue concentrations were measured in the
preputial gland, Harderian gland, white fat and liver.
Lowest radiation levels were in the spinal cord and brain.
In 24 hours post-dose the majority of radiation was eliminated from
tissues, and by 72 hours post-dose the radiation levels were below the
limit of quantification.
No sex differences were reported in the majority of tissues, with
concentrations in both the ovary and uterus being very similar to blood
levels, and those in testes being significantly less than that in blood.
Melanin binding was apparent and levels decreased over 120 hours postdose.

Title: Determination of the In Vitro Binding of [14C] KRN951 to Plasma Proteins in

x The binding of tivozanib hydrochloride to plasma proteins in male and

Objectives: To determine the pharmacokinetic profile of KN951 administered in
a single oral dose to Nude mice.
Methods:
x Seven-week old female nude mice
x Single dose of KRN951
x KRN951 Lot # CDM-003
x Oral gavage
x Doses of 5 or 20 mg/kg
x Blood was taken at 0, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 24, and 48 hours postdose
for pharmacokinetic analysis.
x Serum samples were analyzed using HPLC-MS/MS.
x Endpoints: Cmax, AUC, T1/2, Tmax
Results/conclusion:
x Cmax at the 5 and 20 mg/kg dose levels was 1361 and 7708 ng/mL,
respectively.
x AUC was 11513 and 68233 hr·ng/mL for the 5 and 20 mg/kg doses,
respectively.
x Both the Cmax and AUC showed dose-dependent increases.
x The t1/2 for 5 and 20 mg/kg was not dose dependent, being 6.11 and 4.58
hours, respectively.

(excerpted from Applicant’s report)

6

General Toxicology

Overall Summary:
Single-oral and intravenous (IV) dose toxicity studies in CD-1 mice and Sprague-Dawley (SD) rats
were conducted followed by oral repeat-dose studies in SD rats (up to 26 weeks) and
cynomolgus monkeys (up to 39 weeks).
IV single-dose: the maximum tolerated dose in mice and rats following a single IV
administration was established to be greater than the highest dose tested of 30 mg/kg, which
also is the maximum feasible dose.

180
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Given that the oral route is the proposed clinical route, the studies used to assess the
safety of the compound in support of the current NDA application were performed using the
oral route as follows.
Oral single-dose studies: In mice, a single-oral dose of tivozanib at 524 mg/kg (1700-fold
of the recommended human dose) was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose. A dose
of 524 mg/kg, resulted in a decrease in body weight gain, reduced lymphocyte count, total
white blood cell count and increases in ALT, AST, ALP.
In rats, a single-oral dose of tivozanib at 276 mg/kg (1790-fold of the recommended human
dose) was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose. A dose of 276 mg/kg, resulted in a
decrease in body weight, increases in RBC parameters, ALT, AST and urobilinogen.
Oral repeat-dose studies in rats: In the repeat-dose toxicology studies, rats were orally
dosed with tivozanib for 13 weeks (0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3, or 1.0 mg/kg/day) and 26 weeks (0.005,
0.02 or 0.08 mg/kg/day). In the 13-week rat study, doseƐ ш 0.1 mg/kg/day resulted in
mortalities and severe clinical observations (clinical signs typically associated with morbidity,
malocclusion, missing teeth and white teeth) with effects more pronounced at 1 mg/kg/day,
resulting in cessation of dosing at 1 mg/kg/day after 4 weeks of dosing. Body weights and food
consumption were decreased throughout the treatment period at doses ш 0.03 mg/kg/day.
These decreases were accompanied by test article-related changes in clinical pathology
parameters. The hematology parameters reportĞĚ Ăƚ ĚŽƐĞƐ ш Ϭ͘Ϭϯ ŵŐͬŬŐͬĚĂǇ ŝŶĐůƵĚĞĚ
