In a 39-week monkey study, the highest dose tested (0.1 mg/kg/day) was well tolerated.
No test article-related clinical observations were reported. There were no effects on body
weight, clinical pathology, echocardiographic, ECG, macro and microscopic findings reported at
all dose levels. The NOAEL was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED=1.95 g/day; 1.3-fold of
the maximum recommended human dose).
Overall, both the 26-week rat and 39-week monkey toxicology studies were insufficient, as the
highest dose(s) assessed produced no/minimal toxicity and dose-response relationships could
not be evaluated. Given the low safety margin noted for this study in regard to the clinical
dose, it is uncertain why the Applicant did not explore a more robust range of doses in these
studies. Given the lack of substantial toxicity in these studies, the Applicant proposed to use
the data from the 13-week rat and monkey studies to inform the package insert regarding
toxicities in nonclinical studies.

6.1 Single-Dose Toxicity
182
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Key findings:
Single oral and intravenous dose toxicity studies were performed in CD-1 mice and SpragueDawley rats to establish the maximum tolerated dose for tivozanib.
x

x

x

In mice, a single oral dose of 524 mg/kg (1700-fold the recommended human dose) was
considered to be the maximum tolerated dose. At doses ш 750 mg/kg, mortality and
clinical observations were reported. At doses ш 524 mg/kg, a decrease in body weight
gain, reduced lymphocyte count, total white blood cell count and increases in ALT, AST,
ALP were reported.
In rats, a single oral dose of 276 mg/kg (1790-fold the recommended human dose) was
considered to be the maximum tolerated dose. Higher doses resulted in mortalities (369
mg/kg and 2000 mg/kg) and clinical observations. A decrease in body weight was
observed in all dose groups. At 276 mg/kg, increases in RBC parameters, ALT, AST and
urobilinogen were reported.
An IV dose > 30 mg/kg (the maximal feasible dose) was established to be the maximum
tolerated dose in mice and rats (97- and 195-fold the recommended human dose for
mice and rats, respectively).

Reviewer comment: The Toxicokinetic data was not reviewed and discussed in this section for
the following reason. The bioanalyses and toxicokinetic data supporting the single-dose
toxicology studies (RZZ1008, RZZ 1009, RZZ 1010 and RZZ 1011) were compromised due to data
integrity issues and non-compliance with the GLP regulations. As such, the data were not
considered reliable, and the Applicant amended the final reports and excluded the data.
Study #:
RZZ1008

Title: Acute Intravenous Toxicity Study in the Rat with KRN951 Including
Toxicokinetic Sampling
GLP: zĞƐ͕ ǁŝƚŚ ƚŚĞ ĞǆĐĞƉƚŝŽŶ ŽĨ ďŝŽĂŶĂůǇƚŝĐĂů ĂŶĚ ƚŽǆŝĐŽŬŝŶĞƚŝĐ ;d<Ϳ ĂŶĂůǇƐŝƐ͘
Objectives: to evaluate the maximum tolerated dose and to assess the acute
toxicity of KRN951, an angiogenesis inhibitor in the Sprague Dawley rats following
a single dose by intravenous (IV) injection.
Methods: All rats (5/sex/group) received a single dose of the test article at 30
mg/kg (the highest soluble dose of KRN951 in vehicle) or the vehicle (40%
Dimethylacetamide) by the IV route. Rats were observed up to 14 days post-dose.
Endpoints: body weights, clinical pathology, urine, necropsy and TK.
Results:
x Clinical observations were evident only during the first 15 minutes postdose, which included subdued behavior, agitation and abnormal gait.
x No effects were reported on body weights. On Day 4, red blood cells,
hemoglobin, hematocrit and mean hemoglobin concentration levels were
increased, while platelet counts, neutrophils and reticulocytes were
reduced in males and females. By Day 15 the changes in red cell
183

Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
parameters and platelets were not evident. Clinical chemistry parameters
were increased on Day 4 (ALT, AST, Fe, cholesterol and phospholipids),
while unsaturated iron binding capacity was decreased. By Day 15 the
changes were not evident. A decrease in osmolality of the urine was
evident on Day 1, but not on Day 14.
Conclusion:
A dose > 30 mg/kg was considered to be the maximum tolerated dose in rats
treated IV with a single dose of KRN951.

Title: Acute Intravenous Toxicity Study in the Mouse with KRN951 Including

GLP: zĞƐ͕ ǁŝƚŚ ƚŚĞ ĞǆĐĞƉƚŝŽŶ ŽĨ ďŝŽĂŶĂůǇƚŝĐĂů ĂŶĚ d< ĂŶĂůǇƐŝƐ

Results:
Mortalities
Test article-related mortalities were reported at doses ш 0.1 mg/kg/day:
o Three male and three female rats at 1 mg/kg/day were found dead or moribund
sacrificed during weeks 3-4.
o At 0.3 mg/kg/day, three male and three female rats were found dead or moribund
sacrificed during weeks 7- 12 of the study.
o One female was found dead at 0.1 mg/kg/day during week 5.
o All found dead or moribund sacrificed rats at doses ш 0.3 mg/kg/day, were associated
with microscopic findings of adrenal necrosis/degeneration. The animal found dead at
0.1 mg/kg/day, had urinary tract inflammation and adrenal necrosis.

Clinical Observations
Test article-related clinical observations were reported at doses ш 0.3 mg/kg/day in male and
female rats and included hunched, thin, hypoactive, rough hair coat, yellow hair coat, cold to
touch, pale, malocclusion, missing teeth and white teeth. Additionally, test article-related
effects were evident in males at 0.1 mg/kg/day which included, rough hair coat and
malocclusion.
White teeth and malocclusion at doses ш 0.3 mg/kg/day persisted throughout the recovery
period.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
At 1 mg/kg/day test article-related decreases in mean body weights were reported in males (m)
and females (f) during the first 4 weeks of treatment (by up to 33% in males and 26% in
females), and at the end of the interim recovery period, body weight decreases persisted (up
38% in males and 19% in females). Throughout the period, (weeks 1 to 13), mean body weights
in males and females were lower than control by up to 15% and 9% at 0.1 mg/kg/day,
respectively, and by up to 41% and 32% at 0.3 mg/kg/day. The decreases in mean body
weights persisted throughout the recovery period at 0.3 mg/kg/day (by up to 25% in males and
21% in females). Decreases in mean body weights were also reported in females at 0.03
mg/kg/day (up to 13% in females) during weeks 8 to 13. No effect on mean body weight was
reported at the end of the recovery period at 0.03 mg/kg/day. In general, the effects were
more pronounced in males at all dose groups.

Body Weight Gain
Male rats gained up to 10%, 15% and 97% less weight at 0.1, 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day relative to
controls during the first 25 days of treatment, respectively. For the entire treatment period
(Days 1-92) male rats gained up to 10%, 7%, 17% and 63% at 0.01, 0.03, 0.1 and 0.3 mg/kg/day,
less body weight than the control animals, respectively. Female rats gained up to 19%, 19%,
18% and 99% less weight at 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day relative to controls during the first
25 days of treatment, respectively. For the entire treatment period (Days 1-92) female rats
gained up to 15%, 30%, 20% and 45% at 0.01, 0.03, 0.1 and 0.3 mg/kg/day, less body weight
than the control animals, respectively. No test article-related effects were reported at the end
of the recovery period.

Food Consumption
Test article-related decreases in food consumption were reported at 1 mg/kg/day in male and
female rats during the fist 25 days of treatment (up to 55% in males and 56% in females).
Decreases in food consumption were also reported during the entire treatment period (Days 192) in males and females at 0.3 mg/kg/day (46% in males and 50% in females), at 0.1 mg/kg/day
(up to 11% in males and 14% in females) and at 0.03 mg/kg/day in females (up to 13% in
females). Food consumption values continued to be lower than the control group values at the
end of the recovery period at doses ш 0.3 mg/kg/day (up to 1% in males and 15% in females).

Hematology
At 1 mg/kg/day, test article-related significant changes were reported in:
x Males, red blood cells parameters, platelet, neutrophils (NEUT), monocytes, APTT, and
myeloid/erythroid (M/E) ratio during week 4. At the end of the recovery period (week 7)
the following parameters were still evident: red blood cells (RBC), reticulocytes (RETIC),
Absolute reticulocytes (AB RETIC) , MCV and Monocytes.
x Females, red blood cells parameters, AB RETIC, platelet, and M/E ratio during week 4. At
the end of the recovery period (week 7) the following parameters were still evident:
RBC, mean corpuscular hemoglobin (MCH) and fibrinogen (Fibrin).

Clinical Chemistry
Predominant treatment-related clinical chemistry changes:
x Protein parameters (total protein, albumin, albumin/globulin ration): parameters were
decreased at doses ш0.1 mg/kg/day. Total protein and albumin were evident at a lesser
magnitude at the end of the recovery period.
x Liver parameters: ALT was increased at doses ш 0.1 mg/kg/day, total bilirubin, GammaGlutamyltransferase (U GGT), LDH and triglycerides were increased at 1 mg/kg/day. No
findings were reported at the end of the recovery period.
x LDH/creatinine was increased at 1 mg/kg/day and effect on LDH/creatinine at a lesser
magnitude was evident at the end of the recovery period.
x Iron metabolism parameters: U Iron binding capacity and percent saturation of
transferrin (%SAT) were increased at doses ш 0.03 mg/kg/day. Serum iron was increased
at 0.3 mg/kg/day.
x Renal parameters: Urea nitrogen was increased at doses ш 0.3 mg/kg/day and urea
creatinine at 1 mg/kg/day. Effect on Urea nitrogen at a lesser magnitude was evident at
the end of the recovery period.

Grades for Severity of the microscopic findings:
1 Minimal - the least amount of change observed with the light microscope
2 Slight - less than average amount of change, but readily discernible as abnormal
3 Moderate - the average amount of change expected for a lesion
4 Marked - a marked amount of change with possible loss of function of the affected cells or organs
5 Severe - a great amount of change with probable loss of function of the affected cell or organs and frequently
involves large areas of the organ.

- At 1 mg/kg/day, microscopic findings at the end of recovery were observed in the brain
(minimal to slight - vacuolated cells, choroid plexus), adrenal cortex (minimal to slight degeneration, congestion/hemorrhage), heart (slight to moderate - degeneration), kidney
(minimal to severe - glomerulopathy, casts, tubules, protein (hyaline), nephropathy, chronic);

teeth incisors and molar (slight to marked - hyperplasia, odontoblasts,

observed in the adrenal cortex (moderate to marked - atrophy, pigment, hemosiderin) and
duodenum (minimal to moderate - hypertrophy, transmural).

hypertrophy/hyperplasia, bile ducts); kidney (slight to moderate - glomerulopathy, casts,
tubules, protein (hyaline), nephropathy, chronic), pancreas (slight - atrophy, focal infiltrate
mononuclear cell, inflammation), bone (minimal-Hypertrophy of the physis and minimal to
marked -loss of the primary spongiosa), hypertrophy bone marrow (femur) and uterus (slight
to moderate- dilatation).

Conclusion
The NOAEL was considered to be 0.03 mg/kg/day (HED = 0.29g/day, or 0.2X the recommended
human dose) based on adverse microscopic findings. dŽǆŝĐŝƚŝĞƐ Ăƚ шϬ͘ϯŵŐͬŬŐͬĚĂǇ ǁĞƌĞ
significant and many were not recoverable. Partial recovery of most effects was reported at
doses ш Ϭ͘ϭ ŵŐͬŬŐͬĚĂǇ͕ ĂůƚŚŽƵŐŚ ƌĞŶĂů ĞĨĨĞĐƚƐ ǁĞƌĞ ŶŽƚ ƌĞĐŽǀĞƌĂďůĞ Ăƚ ƚŚĞƐĞ ĚŽƐĞƐ͘
Study title: KRN951: A 26-Week Oral Toxicity Study in Rats With a 6-Week Recovery Period
Study no.:
1458-005

AVEO Pharmaceuticals, Inc.
Cambridge, Massachusetts 02139

AV-951, CDM-005 and 99.53%

Key Findings
x No test article-related mortalities were reported.
x The only test article-related clinical observation reported was white teeth at doses ш
0.005 mg/kg/day. The effect was not considered to be an adverse effect.
x Female body weights at 0.08 mg/kg/day, were slightly decreased (up to 6%) during the
last 3 weeks of treatment. The effect was not considered to be an adverse effect.
x Test article-related increases in leukocytes, hemoglobin, hematocrit and erythrocytes
were reported in male and female rats at 0.08 mg/kg/day at weeks 13 and/or 26. These
findings were not considered to be adverse.
x Test article-related decreases were reported at 0.08 mg/kg/day in total protein, albumin
and globulin in both sexes at week 13 and 26. These findings were not considered to be
adverse, due to the magnitude of effect.
x There were no test article-related effects on organ weights, macroscopic and
microscopic findings at all dose levels.
x No test article-related effects were reported at the end of the recovery period.

protocol:

Daily for up to 182 days.
Oral gavage
10 mL/kg
Vehicle, 0.5% methylcellulose
Naïve male and female Crl:CD®(SD) rats
Approximately 6 weeks of age
243 to 297 g for males
182 to 218 g for females.
Blood samples were obtained from 3 animals/sex/group at one hour
post-dose on days 1, 91 and 182 (Group 5; vehicle control). Blood
samples were obtained from 3 animals/sex/group at pre-dose and at
0.5, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 and 24 hours post-dose on days 1, 91 and 182 (Groups
6 – 8).
Mortality, clinical observations, cage side observations, ophthalmic
examinations, body weight, food consumption, clinical pathology
(samples were taken on week 13, prior to terminal necropsy and prior
to recovery), immunology evaluation, macro and microscopic
evaluation and organ weights.
At the end of the 26 weeks of administration (terminal necropsy), 5
animals per sex were maintained for a 6-week recovery period for each
group (recovery necropsy). Histopathology evaluations were
performed at all dose groups.
None of the deviations affected the integrity or interpretability of the
results of the study.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Results:
Analysis of dosing formulations
The reported data indicated that the formulations were stable (up to 21 days) and
homogenous. Analyses were performed and confirmed that formulation concentrations during
weeks 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 20, and 24, were within the acceptable range (±15%) with the exception of
the formulation on week 3 (18.7%) and 12 (16.8%).
Mortality
No test article-related mortalities were reported. Two male rats at 0.005 mg/kg/day and one
male rat at 0.08 mg/kg/day were found dead or moribund sacrificed. The reported mortalities
were not considered test article-related. One male rat was inadvertently killed as the technician
was closing the cage on Day 89 (male at 0.08 mg/kg/day), the second male rat (0.005
mg/kg/day) was euthanized due to the severe clinical observations and the cause of death was
the formation of multicentric malignant lymphoma; and the cause of death for the third male
(0.005 mg/kg/day) is unknown. Due to lack of dose response relationship the deaths at 0.005
mg/kg/day were not considered test article-related.

Clinical Signs
The only test article-related clinical observation was white teeth at doses ш 0.005 mg/kg/day.
The observation was evident in male and female rats at all dose groups and lasted up to 14
weeks. The observation was noted in a small number of animals per group. The reported
observation was evident in all dose groups and was consistent with findings in prior study (13week repeat dose study in rats-study # 6691-158); therefore, the effect was considered to be
test article-related.

Microscopic Observations
There were no test article-related effects reported at all dose levels.
No histopathology peer review was performed.
Toxicokinetics
x The toxicokinetic parameters were similar in male and female rats on days 1, 91 and
182.
x AUC0-24, Cmax and t1/2 were higher on days 91 and 182 than on day 1, but were similar on
days 91 and 182.
x AUC0-24 was 1.8 to 2.6 times higher on days 91 and 182 than on day 1, indicating
accumulation.
x Cmax was 1.6 to 2.9 times higher on days 91 and 182 than on day 1, indicating
accumulation.
x The similarity in systemic exposure on days 91 and 182 suggests that steady state levels
were reached by day 91, and that no further accumulation occurred beyond day 91.
x AUC0-24 and Cmax increased in approximate proportion to dose on days 1, 91 and 182,
suggesting that systemic exposure was linear with dose.
x Tmax was unaffected by dose level or duration of treatment. The rate of absorption
(Tmax) ranged from 2 to 4 hours.
x Elimination half-life (t1/2) ranged from 9 to 45 hours.
Toxicokinetics:

{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

(excerpted from Applicant’s report)

Conclusion:
There were minimal toxicities observed at all doses up to the highest dose tested (0.08
mg/kg/day). The clinical observation of white teeth was evident at all doses. Test articlerelated findings were evident at 0.08 mg/kg/day and included decrease in body weight, and
hematological and clinical chemistry changes. The above observed effects were not considered
to be adverse, and no test article-related macroscopic or microscopic findings were reported at
any dose levels. The NOAEL was established to be the highest dose tested 0.08 mg/kg/day (0.5fold the maximum recommended human dose).
Overall this repeat-dose toxicity study was insufficient, as the highest dose(s) assessed
produced limited toxicity and dose-response relationships could not be evaluated. Given the
low safety margin noted for this study in regard to the clinical dose, it is uncertain why the
Applicant did not explore a more robust range of doses in this study. Given the lack of
substantial toxicity in this study, the Applicant proposed to use the data from the 13-week rat
study to inform the package insert regarding toxicities observed in rats.

Methods:
Species/strain: Cynomolgus monkey
Age/weight: 2 to 4 years old; 1.9 to 3.2 kg for males and 2.0 to 2.8 kg for females.
Doses Administered: 0, 0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day (Groups 1-6)
Dose volume - 10 mL/kg.
Vehicle: 0.5% methylcellulose
Sex/Number/Route: Males and females; 10, 3, 3, 5, 5, and 5/sex/Groups 1 through 6,
respectively; oral gavage.
Duration of dosing/recovery: 3 weeks/ 5 weeks recovery (Groups 1-5); Group 1 and 6 were
dosed for 4 weeks with 2 weeks recovery.
Study design:
 Three males and three females in the control and 1.0 mg/kg/day groups were sacrificed
after at least 4 weeks of treatment (interim sacrifice) and two males and two females in
the control and 1.0 mg/kg/day groups (after treatment for 4 weeks) were sacrificed
after a 2 weeks of recovery period (recovery-1 Phase).
 Remaining monkeys were sacrificed after at least 13 weeks of treatment (terminal
sacrifice) or placed on at least 5 weeks of recovery (recovery-2 Phase) after 13 weeks of
treatment.

(excerpted from Applicant’s report)

212
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Endpoints: Mortality, clinical observations, body weights, food consumption, clinical pathology,
ophthalmic examinations, electrocardiogram examinations, toxicokinetic, gross pathology,
organ weight and microscopic evaluation.
Histopathology peer review: was performed
Results:
Mortality
No unscheduled deaths occurred during the study.
Clinical Signs
Dose of 1 mg/kg/day resulted in severe clinical signs that resulted in cessation of dosing
after 4 weeks of dosing. The clinical signs included dehydrated appearance; hunched
posture; thin appearance, hypoactivity; discolored, few, no, and/or non-formed feces;
and/or low or no food consumption. The observations were more pronounced in males. At
0.3 mg/kg/day, no food consumption was reported in 2 out of 5 male monkeys and
dehydration and hunched appearance reported in one male monkey. An increased
frequency of low or no food consumption was also reported at 0.1 mg/kg/day during the
treatment period. Unremarkable clinical observations were reported during the recovery
period.
Body weights
Dose of 1 mg/kg/day resulted in 20% and 8% decreases in male and female mean body
weights relative to the control group by the end of treatment period (day 1-29). The
individual body weight gain data showed that some male and female monkeys lost up to
0.7 kg and 0.6 kg body weight, respectively, relative to the control monkeys (mean gain of
0.1 kg and 0 kg in male and females, respectively) during the treatment period (days 1-29).
Dose of 0.3 mg/kg/day resulted in 18% and 8% decreases in male and female mean body
weights relative to the control group by the end of treatment period (day 1-92). Body
weight gain data showed that male and female monkeys at 0.3 mg/kg/day had decreased
body weight gain of 0.2 kg and 0.1 kg relative to the control animals (mean gain of 0.4 kg
and 0 kg in male and female, respectively) during the entire treatment period (Days 1-92).
No significant body weight gain changes ǁĞƌĞ ƌĞƉŽƌƚĞĚ Ăƚ ĚŽƐĞƐ ч Ϭ͘ϭ ŵŐͬŬŐͬĚĂǇ͘ EŽ
significant differences were reported at the end of the recovery period at all dose groups.
Clinical Pathology
Hematological
Test article-related hematological changes were evident at 1 mg/kg/day (increases in RBC,
Hgb, Hct, Fibrin and M/E ratio; decreases in WBC, RETIC, NEUT, MCV and PT). By the end of
the recovery period the majority of the values were similar to the control values. Some of
the changes (RBC, Hct and HGB) can be attributed to the decrease in food consumption,

(increase in Hgb, Hct and/or Fibrin) were of lesser magnitude, not statistically significant

Clinical and urine chemistry
Test article-related clinical and urine chemistry changes were evident at 1 mg/kg/day
(increases in AST, SELA-ɲ Θ ɴ͕ ŵĂŐŶĞƐŝƵŵ͕ ĐƌĞĂƚŝŶŝŶĞ͕ cholesterol, CK, potassium, U
albumin, iron, SAT, phospholipids; decreases in alkaline phosphatase (ALP), amylase,
chloride, UIBC, SDH, total bile acid, free fatty acids (FFA), U LDH, U creatinine). By the end of
the recovery period the majority of the values were similar to the controls. Some of the
reported changes can be attributed to the decrease in food consumption and/or lower body
weights (i.e. increase in potassium, cholesterol, magnesium, creatinine, albumin, FFA, AST
and decreased chloride). The decreases in amylase and ALP activity are correlated with the
microscopic findings in pancreas (zymogen granules) and bone growth. The changes
reported at 0.3 mg/kg/day ( increases in creatinine, SELA-ɲ͕ <͕ AST, cholesterol and
phospholipids) and at 0.1 mg/kg/day (increases in AST, SELA-ɴ͕ <͕ cholesterol and
phospholipids) were of lesser magnitude, but considered to be test article-related. No
changes were reported at the end of the recovery period.

Macroscopic observations
Test article-related macroscopic observations were reported only at the interim sacrifice (1
mg/kg/day) and consisted of dark thyroid, dark adrenals, distended gallbladder and thickened
gastric mucosa and/or dark areas on the gastric mucosa in the stomach of males. The findings
in the thyroid and thymus (females) were evident at the end of the recovery period.
Microscopic findings
Test article-related histopathological changes were reported in multiple organs at the 1
mg/kg/day interim-sacrifice animals (3 monkeys/sex) and interim-recovery (2/sex).
These findings consisted of the following predominant effects (#/# indicates incidences in
male/female):
 Femur and tibia bone: severe atrophy of the primary spongiosa (3/3) and moderate
hypertrophy of the physis in the distal femur (2/2) and the tibia (1/1) in males and
females and slight hypertrophy of the femoral articular cartilage in males (2/0).

Toxicokinetics
 Exposure to tivozanib hydrochloride increased with dose at all collection days in both
sexes.
 The increases in Cmax and AUC for both sexes were generally less than dose
proportional on day 28 and 91 (0.01 to 1 mg/kg/day). On day 1, the increases in Cmax
and AUC were approximately dose proportional, with the exception of the values at 1
mg/kg/day which were greater than dose proportional. At doses of 0.03 to 1
mg/kg/day, the increase in Cmax and AUC were approximately dose proportional on all
collection days.
 Mean concentrations were higher after repeat dosing at doses ч 0.3 mg/kg/day, while
concentrations were similar after repeat dosing at 1 mg/kg/day.
 Tmax values on day 1, 28 and 91 ranged from 2.6-6.7 hours, 3.3-8 hours and 2-4 hours,
respectively.
 No significant (>2 fold) gender differences were reported.

 Cmax and AUC values were higher (1.1-2.9 and 1.3 to 3.8 times, respectively) on day 28
and 91 than day 1.
 Mean elimination half life (t1/2) was similar across all dose levels for both sexes on all
blood collection days. Elimination half-life (t1/2) ranged from 7.7 to 9.2 hours.

Conclusion:
The NOAEL level after 13 weeks of dosing was 0.1 mg/kg/day (HED = 1.95 mg/day). Findings
after at least 2 weeks of recovery in animals given 1.0 mg/kg/day and after at least 5 weeks of
recovery in animals given 0.3 mg/kg/day indicated either full or partial recoverability.

Mortality
x One female at 0.03 mg/kg/day (animal number 135) was found dead on day 37.
Microscopic findings indicated that the primary cause of death was due to a gavage
error with secondary pneumonia, subsequent septicemia and abdominal involvement.
Microscopic findings provided support for this assumption.
x One male (animal number 138) at 0.1 mg/kg/day was found dead on day 173. No
macroscopic or microscopic findings were reported that would indicate the cause of
death. There was no evidence of gavage error reported. The reported microscopic
findings were minimal infiltration of mononuclear cells in the heart, kidney and liver;
minimal pigmented alveolar macrophages around bronchioles in the lung and unilateral
congestion of cortical sinusoids of the adrenal glands.
The death of the male at 0.1 mg/kg/day was not considered to be test article-related for
the following reasons:
 Follow up studies were performed (troponin I, CK, CPK and BMP, blood pressure,
ECGs and echocardiographic evaluation) on the remaining surviving animals to
understand the causes of death. The findings did not provide any additional
insight regarding the cause of the reported death.
 Higher doses (0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day) administered in prior 13 week study (Study #
6691-159) showed no mortalities.
 No other study animal was reported to show severe signs of toxicity, even after
273 days of dosing.
Clinical Signs
There were no reported test article-related clinical observations at all dose levels.
Body Weights
There were no reported test article-related body weight effects at all dose levels.
Ophthalmoscopy
There were no reported test article-related ophthalmological findings at all dose levels.
Electrocardiographic Examination
There were no reported test article-related ECG findings at all dose levels.
Echocardiographic Evaluations
There were no reported test article-related echocardiographic findings (qualitative and
quantitative abnormalities in heart size and indices of left ventricular global systolic
performance) at all dose levels.
Clinical Pathology
Hematology: There were no reported test article-related hematological changes at all dose
levels.
223
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Coagulation: There were no reported test article-related coagulation changes at all dose levels.
Clinical Chemistry: There were no reported test article-related clinical chemistry changes at all
dose levels.
Urinalysis: There were no reported test article-related urinalysis changes at all dose levels.
Macroscopic Observations
There were no reported test article-related macroscopic findings at all dose levels.
Organ weights
There were no reported test article-related organ weight changes at all dose levels. Although
decreases in heart weights in males and females were noted, they were not associated with
macro-microscopic findings, ECG or echocardiographic findings.
Microscopic Observations
There were no reported test article-related microscopic findings.
Toxicokinetics
x Males and female TK parameters were not different on days 1, 91 and 273, indicating no
gender effects.
x AUC0-24, AUC0-tlast and Cmax increased in approximate proportion to dose on days 1, 91
and 273, suggesting that systemic exposure was linear.
x Mean AUC0-24, AUC0-tlast and Cmax were 0.823 to 1.57 times higher on days 91 and 273
than on day 1. The similarity in AUC0-24, AUC0-tlast and Cmax on days 91 and 273 suggests
that steady-state levels were achieved by day 91, and that no further accumulation
occurred beyond day 91.
x Tmax values were longer on day 1 in comparison to Days 91 and 273. Mean Tmax on day 1
ranged from 3.60 to 4.40 hours and from 2.10 to 2.67 hours on Days 91 and 273.
x Half life (t1/2) was unaffected by dose level or duration of treatment. Elimination half-life
(t1/2) ranged from 9.6 to 12.4 hours.
Toxicokinetics

224
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

(excerpted from Applicant’s report)

Conclusion:
The test article was well tolerated at all dose levels. Although one male was found dead at
0.1 mg/kg/day on day 173, the cause of death was not considered by the Applicant to be
test article-related. The NOAEL was established to be the highest dose tested 0.1
mg/kg/day (1.3-fold the maximum recommended human dose). As such, the doses in this
study were insufficient to adequately establish a dose response, and this study does not
provide adequate information regarding potential toxicity associated with AV-961 following
repeated oral administration for 39 weeks. Accordingly, the Applicant proposed to rely on
the above GLP-compliant 13-week study to inform the package insert regarding toxicities in
monkeys.

8 Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
Overall Summary:
In the dose range (at 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 or 1 mg/kg/day) and definitive fertility and reproduction
toxicity studies (at 0.01, 0.03 or 0.1 mg/kg/day), KRN951 was administered orally by gavage to
male rats for up to 28 days before mating, during mating (1 to 14 days) and up to termination
(approximately 49 total doses) and to female rats for up to 21 days prior to mating, through
mating and on Gestation Days (GD) 0 to 7.
At 1 mg/kg/day, mortalities were evident in both sexes. Clinical observations and decreases in
body weights were reported in both sexes at doses ш Ϭ͘ϯ ŵŐͬŬŐͬĚĂǇ͘ A decrease in food

In the rabbit embryo-fetal developmental study, pregnant rabbits were dosed (0.01, 0.1 or 1

Embryo-fetal toxicity was evident at 1 mg/kg/day (VSD, constricted pulmonary artery vessels
and distended aorta). The NOAEL for maternal toxicity was established to be greater than 1
mg/kg/day (13-fold the maximum recommended human clinical dose), while the NOAEL for
embryo-fetal toxicity was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day (1.3-fold the maximum
recommended human clinical dose).

Surviving male and female rats to scheduled termination at 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day, exhibited
adverse clinical signs that included:
ungroomed coat, dehydration, chromorhinorrhea, soft or liquid feces, chromodacryorrhea and
lacrimation. Other clinical signs reported only at 1 mg/kg/day were scant feces, urine-stained
abdominal fur, decreased motor activity, cold to touch, pale extremities, hunched posture,
emaciation, impaired righting reflex, brown or red per-oral substance, ptosis, hyperpnea,
labored breathing, mucoid feces and/or no feces in cage pan.
Body weight (male and female):
 At 1 mg/kg/day, a decrease ranging between 6% to 43%, in male mean body weight was
reported during SD 11 to 42. The severity of the effect was more pronounced at latter
study days.
 Decreases in male body weight gains were reported at 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 and 1 mg/kg/day by
up to 20%, 36%, 50% and 252%, respectively.
 At 1 mg/kg/day, a decrease in female mean body weight (up to 8%) was reported during
study days 18 to 21 (pre-cohabitation period) and a decrease in body weight gain (up to
101%) during the entire pre-cohabitation period.
Food consumption (male and female):
 Male mean absolute and relative feed consumption values were reduced at 0.1, 0.3 and
1.0 mg/kg/day by up to 6%, 8% and 39% during the entire study period.
 Female mean absolute and relative feed consumption values were reduced at 0.3 and
1.0 mg/kg/day during the entire pre-cohabitation period (study day 1 to 21) by 10% and
24%, respectively.
Mating and fertility:
Males
The number of days in cohabitation (number of days required to inseminate) was increased for
the male rats at 1.0 mg/kg/day, in comparison with the control group (3.7 days versus 2.7 days,
respectively). The number of male rats that mated (83.3%) was also reduced at 1.0 mg/kg/day,
compared to the control group (100%). The fertility index (number of pregnancies per number
of rats mated) and the number of rats pregnant per rats in cohabitation were 0.0% at 0.3 and
1.0 mg/kg/day.
Females
The number of days in cohabitation was increased for the female rats at 1.0 mg/kg/day, in
comparison with the control group (5.1 days versus 2.7 days, respectively). The number of
female rats that mated was also reduced at 1.0 mg/kg/day, in comparison with the control
group (71.4% versus 100%, respectively). The fertility index (number of pregnancies per number
of rats mated) and the number of rats pregnant per rats in cohabitation were 0.0% (none
mated) at 0.3 and 1.0 mg/kg/day dosage groups.

Male necropsy and organ weights:
Several gross lesions were reported at necropsy. At 0.3 mg/kg/day, one male rat had a mass on
the right cauda epididymis.
At 1 mg/kg/day, two male rats had adrenals that appeared large and red (bilateral), kidneys
that appeared pale (bilateral), and air-filled stomach; one additional rat had a liver mass.
Relative epididymis weights were increased 13%, 18%, 24% and 19% at 0.03, 0.1, 0.3 and 1
mg/kg/day, respectively. Relative testis weights were increased by 10% and 19% at 0.3 and 1
mg/kg/day.
Female Cesarean section and necropsy:
No gross lesions were reported at necropsy of rats survived to C-section.
None of the surviving female rats at either the 0.3 or 1.0 mg/kg/day were found pregnant.
At 0.1 mg/kg/day, an increases in the number of dams with any nonviable embryos (62.5% vs
28.6% at the control, respectively) and percent nonviable embryos per litter (5.8% vs. 2.8% at
the control, respectively) were reported.
Conclusion:

Analysis of test formulations:
ŽŶĐĞŶƚƌĂƚŝŽŶ ;цϭϱйͿ ĂŶĚ ŚŽŵŽŐĞŶĞŝƚǇ ;чϱй Z^Ϳ ŽĨ ƚŚĞ ƚĞƐƚ ĂƌƚŝĐůĞ ŝŶ ƚŚĞ ĨŽƌŵƵůĂƚŝŽŶƐ
were within the acceptable criteria with the exception of deviations in group 2 (0.001
mg/mL) for formulations prepared on 10/6/2003. The formulation was stable for up to 15
days after preparation at 4°C and for at least one day at room temperature.
Mortality and clinical observations (male and female):
All rats survived to scheduled termination. There were no test article-related clinical
observations.
Body weight and food consumption (male and female):
There were no reported test article effects on body weights at all dose levels in both sexes.
Male food consumption was not affected by the test article treatment at all dose levels,

mg/kg/day.

There were no test article-related effects reported on mating incidence, days taken for

effects on the number and percent motile sperm, number of non-motile sperm and total
sperm count from the vas deferens and caudal epididymal (sperm count and density).

lutea, implantations, litter size, viability of embryos at all dose levels. Placentae appeared
normal at all dose groups.
Conclusion:
KRN951 (0.01, 0.03 or 0.1 mg/kg/day) was administered orally by gavage to male rats for
28 days before mating, during mating (1 to 14 days) and up to termination (approximately
49 total doses) and to female rats for 14 days prior to mating, through mating and on GD 0
to 7. There were no effects on mating, fertility, gonadal function, or embryo viability at all
dose levels.

period (GD 7-17).

251
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Litter data:
x Mean number of dams with resorptions at 0.09 mg/kg/day, was reported to be 64%
higher than the control group. The reported increases in total resorptions were attributed
to the increases in mean number of early resorptions (not statistically significant) at 0.09
mg/kg/day.
x The percent of resorbed conceptuses per litter was higher at 0.03 mg/kg/day (5.3%) and
0.09 mg/kg/day (7.4%), relative to control (3.5%).
x There were no effects on fetal weights.
Fetal Data:
x 100%, 58%, 25% and 48% of the litters at 0.09, 0.03, 0.01, and 0.003 mg/kg/day,
respectively, were reported to have fetuses with anatomical abnormalities
(malformations and/or variations) in comparison to 8% of the fetuses at the control
group.
x The percent of fetuses with any anatomical abnormalities per litter was higher at 0.09
mg/kg/day (70%) in comparison to the control group (0.5%).
x The percent of litters with fetuses with any malformation was higher at 0.09
mg/kg/day (96%) in comparison to the control group (0%). The percent of fetuses
with malformation per litter at 0.09 mg/kg/day was 59%, in comparison to 0% at the
control group.
x The percent of fetuses with any variations per litter was higher at 0.09 mg/kg/day
(11.7%) and 0.03 mg/kg/day (4.2%) in comparison to the control group (0.5%).
x Significant increases in external malformations were reported at 0.09 mg/kg/day and
included, body edema, short tail and kinked tail.
x Significant increases in skeletal variations were reported at 0.09 mg/kg/day. The
variations were reported to be in the thoracic vertebra (centrum unilateral
ossification and centrum bifid), lumber vertebrae (bifid centrum), caudal vertebrae
(centrum unilateral ossification), sternal centra (incompletely ossified). At 0.03
mg/kg/day, the only toxicologically important variation report was in the thoracic
vertebra (centrum bifid).
x The mean number of ossification sites per fetus per litter (caudal vertebrae, forelimb
phalanges, metatarsals and hindlimb phalanges) were decreased at 0.09 mg/kg/day
in comparison to the control group. Additionally, a decrease in the number of caudal
vertebrae ossification sites per fetus per litter was reported at 0.03 mg/kg/day in
comparison to the control group. The decrease in number of ossifications may be an
indication of developmental delay.
Conclusion:
In the embryo-fetal developmental study, KRN951 at doses of 0.003, 0.01, 0.03 or
0.09 mg/kg/day was administered orally by gavage to pregnant rats throughout

food consumption, corpora lutea, implantations, resorptions, live and dead
fetuses, fetal weight, fetal sex and external (all dosage groups), visceral (control
and 1 mg/kg/day) and skeletal (control and 1 mg/kg/day) findings were recorded.

decreased (GD 12 to 29).

Mean Cmax and mean AUClast decreased from 0.01 mg/kg to 0.1 mg/kg, but increased
from 0.1 mg/kg to 1.0 mg/kg on GD 6. On GD 18, mean Cmax and mean AUClast each
increased with dosage in approximately dose-proportional relationships.
There was no test article accumulation reported as a result of repeated dosing.
Peak plasma concentrations (Tmax) were observed at 1-8 hours post dose.

Maternal data:
Mortality and clinical observations:
All rabbits survived to scheduled termination. There were no test article-related clinical
observations.
Body weight and Food consumption:
x There were no test article-related effects on mean body weight and body weight gain at
all dose levels.
x There was no test article-related effect on mean food consumption at all dose
levels.
Litter data:
x There were no test article-related effects on litter parameters at all dose levels.
Fetal Data:
x There were no reported test article-related variations at all dose groups.
x At 1 mg/kg/day, the visceral malformations occurred in one fetus (VSD, constricted
pulmonary artery and distended aorta were reported in one fetus).
Conclusion:
In contrast to the Applicant’s conclusion stating that no fetal toxicity was observed at any dose
level, this reviewer concludes that teratogenicity was evident at the highest dose assessed, 1
mg/kg/day. The reported visceral malformations at 1 mg/kg/day occurred in one fetus and
were: interventricular septal defect (VSD), constricted pulmonary artery vessels and distended
aorta . These findings were considered to be test article-related since in the dose-range study
(Study number 311-006P) at 1 mg/kg/day dose, two fetuses from two different litters (2615-3
and 2616-2) were reported to have VSD’s and fetus 2616-2 showed distended aorta and
constricted pulmonary artery vessels.
Therefore, the NOAEL for embryo-fetal toxicity was established to be 0.1 mg/kg/day and the
NOAEL for maternal toxicity was established to be greater than 1 mg/kg/day (no effects
reported at the highest dose tested).

9 Special Toxicology Studies
9.1 Phototoxicity Studies
256
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

Title: Ultraviolet Radiation and Visible Light Absorption Profile Study
GLP: zĞƐ
Objectives: “The purpose of this study was to evaluate the ultraviolet radiation (UVR)
and visible light absorption profile of tivozanib hydrochloride”.
Methods:
Tivozanib was suspended in 0.5% MC at concentrations of 0, 0.004, 0.04, 0.4, 4 and 40
mg/mL. These dilutions were evaluated spectrophotometrically in duplicate to
determine which concentrations provided the best spectra. The test plate was scanned
spectrophotometrically from 290 to 700 nm to determine if the vehicle possessed any
absorption peaks. Isolation of the major peak was conducted at the 0.4 mg/mL
concentration.
Study
#: 311003

Results:
The majority of the light did not go through both suspensions of tivozanib (4 and 40
mg/mL). The 0.4 mg/mL dilution provided an average wave length of 329.5 nm (range
290 and 350 nm) and the highest absorption differential of 2.990. The 0.004 and 0.04
mg/mL were clear, however, the peak isolated at the 0.04 mg/mL concentration
showed three peaks at approximately 293, 315 and 328 nm.
Conclusion:
The Applicant reported that the UVR and visible light absorption profile of tivozanib
revealed strong absorbance in the UVR range from 290 through 340 nm with minimal
absorption at wave lengths above 360. The peak absorbance occurred at approximately
329.5 nm. The peak isolated at the 0.04 mg/mL concentration showed three peaks at
approximately 293, 315 and 328 nm.

Title: Single-Dose Phototoxicity Study to Determine the Effects on Eyes and Skin in
Pigmented Rats
GLP: zĞƐ
Study
#: 311004

Objectives: “The purpose of this study was to determine the phototoxic effects on the
eyes and skin of Long- Evans pigmented rats, after a single oral administration of
tivozanib hydrochloride followed by exposures to radiation from a xenon lamp (to
simulate sunlight)”.
Methods:
Animals: Long- Evans pigmented rats
Study design:

257
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

(excerpted from Applicant’s report)

One lightly and one darkly pigmented skin site was exposed to UVR approximately 4,
24, and 120 hours after formulation administration in rats in Groups 1- 4. In Group 5
rats, the eyes and the 2 skin sites were exposed to UVR approximately 1 hour after
administration of 8-MOP.
Rats were examined for general appearance and general skin observations
approximately 30 minutes after the completion of UVR exposure and 1, 2 and 3 days
after UVR exposure. Rats were sacrificed and eyes were taken for histopathology
evaluation.
Results:
x A female rat that received 300 mg/kg of tivozanib hydrochloride was found
dead after UVR exposure on Day 2 of study.
x No test article-related skin toxicity reported at all dose levels tested.
x No test article-related ocular phototoxicity reported at all dose levels tested.
x There were no test article-related microscopic findings in the cornea, retina,
sclera, iris, ciliary body, choroids, lens, aqueous and vitreous chambers, optic
nerve and bulbar conjunctiva.
Conclusions:
A single oral administration of tivozanib followed by exposures to radiation from a
xenon lamp did not elicit either skin or ocular findings indicative of phototoxicity in
rats, at doses up to 300 mg/kg.

10 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation

258
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

Pharmacology: Tivozanib hydrochloride (referred to as KRN951, AV-951 or Tivozanib) is an
anticancer agent of the vascular endothelial growth factor receptor (VEGFR) Tyrosine Kinase
inhibitor class and is being developed for the treatment of advanced renal cell carcinoma. In
vitro biochemical and cellular kinase assays demonstrated that tivozanib is an inhibitor of VEGF
receptors (VEGFRs). In in vitro biochemical kinase assays, tivozanib inhibited the
phosphorylation of VEGFRs-1, -2, and -3, with IC50 of 30, 6, and 15 nM, respectively, and
inhibited other kinases at higher concentrations (IC50 ranging from 40 to 618 nM). In in vitro
cell-based kinase assays, tivozanib inhibited the phosphorylation of VEGFRs-1, -2 and -3, with
IC50 of 0.21, 0.16 and 0.24 nM (0.107, 0.081 and 0.122 ng/ml), respectively, and inhibited other
kinases (C-kit and PDGFZɴͿ Ăƚ ŚŝŐŚĞƌ ĐŽŶĐĞŶƚƌĂƚŝŽŶƐ ;/50 of 1.63 and 1.72 nM, or 0.83 and 0.88
ng/ml, respectively). The VEGFR, C-Ŭŝƚ ĂŶĚ W'&Zɴ /50 values are less than the Cmin value
achieved in RCC patients (16 to 30.9 ng/mL) following repeat dosing at the recommended dose
of 1.5 mg/day, indicating that continuous inhibition of VEGF-mediated angiogenesis and activity
of other identified kinases would be occurring during the treatment period.
In in vivo pharmacodynamic studies in mice and rats bearing human or murine xenograft
tumors, tivozanib inhibited tumor growth via changes in angiogenesis and vascular
permeability. In an oral repeat-dose study in nude rat xenograft models bearing 15 different
human cancer cell lines, treatment with tivozanib (0.2 mg/kg/day; HED 1.95 mg/day) resulted in
tumor ŐƌŽǁƚŚ ŝŶŚŝďŝƚŝŽŶ ;d'/ шϱϬйͿ ŝŶ ϭϭ ŽƵƚ ϭϱ ĐĞůů ůŝŶĞ ŵŽĚĞůƐ, and inhibition of intratumor
angiogenesis, as was evident by the reduction in intratumor vascular endothelial cell density
and vascular permeability. A similar outcome was reported in the repeat-dose studies in nude
mice bearing murine breast HER2 xenograft tumors. Tivozanib treatment result in a decrease in
viable tumor area, reduction in total number of proliferating cells, decrease in vessel crosssection area, decrease in microvascular density and an increase in tumor hypoxia, as measured
by HIF1-ɲ ƉƌŽƚĞŝŶ͘ dŚĞ ĂŶƚŝ-tumor activity dose (0.2 mg/kg) in the rat model produced a mean
Cmax of 70 ng/ml (simulated serum concentration after repeat dosing) or 108 ng/ml and AUC of
1640 ng*hr/ml following single dose. These plasma levels overlap those achieved in RCC
patients following a repeat dosing at the recommended dose of 1.5 mg/day (Cmax was 67.5 to
94.3 ng/mL and the AUC0-24 was 1180 to 1641 ng*hr/mL, respectively).
In safety pharmacology studies, no effects on respiration rate, tidal volume, neurobehavioral
endpoints or physiological parameters (Irwin test) were reported at 0.3 mg/kg in rat studies
(HED=2.9 mg/day; 1.9-fold of the recommended human dose). The in vitro hERG tail-current
study for tivozanib was designed with only a single concentration. As such, a concentrationresponse curve was not established (per ICHS7B), and the study provided no information
regarding IC50. Accordingly, this was not considered a valid study. An in vivo telemetry study
was performed in male cynomolgus monkeys administered a single oral dose of tivozanib. The
highest dose tested, 3 mg/kg (HED=54.4 mg/day; 38.9-fold of the recommended human dose)
resulted in increases in systolic, diastolic and mean arterial blood pressure 90 minutes post
dose. There were no test article-related effects on heart rates, RR, PR, QT, QTcF, QTcQ intervals
and the QRS duration at any dose level. In an in vivo repeat-dose telemetry study in male

Pharmacokinetic studies: In vitro and in vivo ADME studies were performed. Overall the data
supported the use of rats and monkey in nonclinical studies, as these species would be
expected to provide adequate coverage for potential human metabolites and associated
toxicities.
x

Tivozanib TK profile following oral administered was approximately linear in mice, rats
and monkeys, with Cmax and AUC increasing in an approximately dose proportional
manner. No significant gender differences in exposure were reported. The rate of
absorption was moderate and unaffected by dose level or duration of treatment in rats
and monkeys, while, the rate of absorption (Tmax) and elimination half-life (t1/2) were
different between species. The elimination t1/2 in rats (9-45 hours) was greater than
that reported in monkeys (8-12 hours), but was considerably shorter than that reported
for humans (4.5-5.1 days). As such, clinical regimen (QD, 3 weeks on and 1 week off)
differs from the daily nonclinical dosing regimen, to account for these differences.
o Reviewer Note: The toxicokinetics (TK) data supporting 10 toxicology studies was
compromised due to data integrity issues and therefore are in non-compliance
with the GLP regulations. The following reports were amended and data was
discarded: 6691-158 (DRZZ1047), RCK0003, RZZ1008, RZZ1009, RZZ1010,
RZZ1011, RZZ1012, RZZ1013, RZZ1014, and RZZ1015. As such, the data from
these studies cannot be used to support this application, and were not reviewed.

In vitro and in vivo metabolism studies with KRN951 were performed and showed that
in serum, the parent compound (KRN951) was the predominant circulating component
(91% and 78% of the total radioactivity exposure in rat and monkey serum, respectively)
with the metabolites M4 (mono-oxidation)/M5 (N-oxide of tivozanib) co-eluting and
M63 in rats only. The parent compound was mainly detected in feces and only less than
0.5% in urine. In feces, the parent compound was the main component and other
metabolites detected represented less than 5% of the administered radioactive dose. In
vitro metabolism studies showed that KRN951 metabolism was low in all species
studied, as was evident by the low hepatic clearance, and that KRN951 appeared to be
metabolized mainly by oxidation and demethylation. All prominent human metabolites
(M1 through M24) were present in both rat and monkey with the exception of M11
(glucuronide), which was unique to human hepatocytes (minor >0.5%) and M10 which
was not observed with rat hepatocytes. M14 was present at >10% of total radioactivity
and was designated as a major metabolite in human, monkey and rat. Comparison of
the metabolic profile of tivozanib in human, monkey, dog and rat liver microsomes
showed that monkey is the closest to human followed by the rat, and dog was
determined to be ƚŚĞ ŵŽƐƚ ĚŝĨĨĞƌĞŶƚ͘ zWϯϰ ĂŶĚ zWϭϭ ĂƌĞ ƚŚĞ ŵĂũŽƌ ŚƵŵĂŶ ŚĞƉĂƚŝĐ

In rats and monkeys the main excretion route was through the feces, and to a smaller
extent (2-3%) through urine. Excretion through the bile dust was identified in rats.

In genetic toxicology studies: Tivozanib was not considered mutagenic in the in vitro reverse
mutation assay (Ames) or aneugenic in the in vivo clastogenicity assay in rodents. In addition,
tivozanib was not considered clastogenic in the in vitro mammalian cell cytogenetic test;
however, tivozanib produced changes in chromosomal number (polyploidy, hyperdiploidy) at
doses ш Ϯ ʅŐͬŵ> (1.2-fold the plasma level at the recommended human dose).

Single-dose toxicology: The maximum tolerated dose in mice and rats following a single IV
administration was established to be greater than the highest dose tested of 30 mg/kg. This
dose was established as the maximum feasible dose. Given that the oral route is the proposed
clinical route, the studies used to assess the safety of the compound in support of the current
NDA application were conducted using the oral route as follows. The MTDs for a single oral
dose in mice and rats were 524 mg/kg and 276 mg/kg, respectively (1700 and 1790-fold of the
recommended human dose). These doses resulted in body weight decrease, an increases in
ALT, AST and ALP, an increase in hematology parameters (red blood cells, hemoglobin,
hematocrit, and neutrophils), and decreases in lymphocyte and total white blood cell counts.

Discussion of toxicities: Of note was that in the monkey study, the ossification findings
were limited to bone. Given that teeth do not grow at a constant rate in monkeys, the
incisor findings in the rat study were not present in the monkey studies. The growth
plate hypertrophy effect on the femur and/or tibia in the rat and monkey is an
important finding, and would be a significant safety concern in young/young adult
populations, as the epiphyseal plate remains active until the age of ~20 years in the
human. Given the targeted age patient population for tivozanib, the bone toxicities may
not be relevant. In the monkey, microscopic findings in the adrenal cortex were
observed in the zona glomerulosa. This tissue is responsible for the production of
mineralocorticoids (e.g. aldosterone) that plays a role in regulating blood pressure and
kidney function, and as such may contribute to the blood pressure and renal function
changes reported in the cardiovascular safety pharmacology study in monkeys.

Overall both the 26-week rat and 39-week monkey toxicology studies were insufficient, as the
highest dose(s) assessed produced no/minimal toxicity and dose-response relationships could
not be evaluated. Given the low safety margin noted for this study in regard to the clinical
dose, it is uncertain why the Applicant did not explore a more robust range of doses in these
studies. Given the lack of substantial toxicity in these studies, the Applicant uses the data from
the 13-week rat and monkey studies to inform the label regarding acute toxicities.
Phototoxicity studies: Ocular and cutaneous phototoxicity studies with tivozanib in rats did not
show cutaneous or ocular phototoxicity.

Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

19.4 OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP
recommendations)
As mentioned above, tivozanib has been reviewed by the Office of Clinical Pharmacology under
NDA 204408 in 2012-2013. Only two more clinical studies were included in current submission,
the dedicated hepatic impairment study AV-951-12-118 and the pivotal study AV-951-15-303
(TIVO-3). Therefore, the current review had an overview of the clinical pharmacology
characteristics of tivozanib and focused on the intrinsic factors that may influence exposure.
19.4.1 Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
Were relevant metabolite concentrations measured in the clinical pharmacology and
biopharmaceutics studies?

metabolites. Therefore, only tivozanib was measured in the clinical pharmacology and

For all moieties measured, is free, bound, or total measured? What is the basis for that
decision, if any, and is it appropriate?
The total plasma concentration of tivozanib was measured in the clinical trials. This was
appropriate as tivozanib is greater than 99% plasma protein bound over the clinically relevant
concentration range studied.
What bioanalytical methods are used to assess concentrations?
Method TVZHSP (Validation Report: 8253837) is an LC-MS/MS method for the determination of
tivozanib (also known as AV951, AV-951, and KRN951) in human serum using a liquid-liquid
extraction method. KRN633 (also known as CDU) was used as the internal standard.
Concentrations were calculated with a 1/x linear regression over a concentration range of 0.100

A total of 13 runs were performed using method TVZHSP during sample analysis of TIVO-3. The
precision of the QC samples was -2.5 to -0.3% and the accuracy was 97.5 to 99.8%. A total of 23
samples were repeated due to the original result of tivozanib being above the limit of
quantitation (100 ng/mL). Samples were diluted up to 1:10, which was within the validated
dilution range. Incurred sample reanalysis for tivozanib was performed on 32 samples and
passed acceptance criteria since 100% of the ISR samples had a percent difference from the
original result of less than 20%.
A total of 17 runs were performed using method TVZHSP during sample analysis of AV-951-12118. The precision of the QC samples was 1.5 to 7.3% and the accuracy was 98.9 to 110.8%. A
total of 8 samples were repeated due to a positive pre-dose (2/8 samples), re-assay to confirm
results (5/8 samples), and pharmacokinetic repeat (1/8 samples). Incurred sample reanalysis for
tivozanib was performed on 72 samples and passed acceptance criteria since 99% of the ISR
samples had a percent difference from the original result of less than 20%.
19.4.2 Clinical PK
Seven clinical pharmacology Phase 1 studies have been completed to characterize the PK
of oral tivozanib; six have been completed in healthy or hepatically impaired volunteers
(Studies AV-951-10-111, AV-951-10-115, AV-951-11-116, AV-951-11-117, AV-951-12-118
and AV-951-09-109) and one in patients with advanced solid tumors (Study AV-951-10112). An additional six clinical monotherapy studies conducted in patients with solid
tumors also included PK endpoints (Studies AV-951-03-B01, AV-951-08-105, AV-951-07201, AV-951-10-202, AV-951-09-301, and AV-951-15-303).
A summary of these trials and their top-line findings is provided in Table 2 below.

Effect of Renal Impairment
No dose adjustment is recommended for patients with renal impairment (creatinine clearance
[CLcr] 15-89 mL/min, estimated by Cockcroft-Gault). The basis for this recommendation is listed
as follows:

272
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
1) The lack of relationship between tivozanib clearace and creatinine clearance. In the
PopPK analysis, renal impairment, as quantified by creatinine clearance (CLcr) with
median 76.2 mL/min and range from 26.8 to 210 mL/min, did not have a statistically
significant effect on the PK of tivozanib.
2) A small fraction (11.8%) of the administerd tivozanib was excreted in urine.
3) The lack of renal function relation AE observed in the clinical trials, including 2 patients
with severe renal impairment. The Incidence rate of acute renal failure is low (Grade1-5,
5.2%, grade3-4, 2.3%) in the pivotal study; no increased safety signals were observed
when the AE profile for the 2 patients with severe renal impairment were analyzed, and
they were both on therapy for about 1.3 years, indicating tivozanib was well tolerated.
Renal and urinary disorders data is summarized in table 4 below.
Given that a higher proportion of patients within the proposed indication, refractory/relapsed
renal cell carcinoma, may have severe renal impairment compared to the general population,
who can potentially benefit from tivozanib treatment, no dose adjustment is recommended for
patients with renal impairment (creatinine clearance [CLcr] 15-89 mL/min, estimated by
Cockcroft-Gault).

Effect of Hepatic Impairment
The effect of hepatic impairment on the PK of tivozanib was assessed in a dedicated hepatic
impairment study and by population PK analysis.
In the dedicated study, the patients were originally classified according to Child-Pugh Scores,
there were 8 patients with mild hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh A), 8 patients with moderate
hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B) and 6 patients with severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh
C). Results showed that the exposure to tivozanib in patients with mild hepatic impairment was
similar to that in patients with normal hepatic function (GMR of AUC00-ь͗ ϭ͘ϭ Ϳ͘ ,ŽǁĞǀĞƌ͕
tivozanib was eliminated more slowly in patients with moderate or severe hepatic impairment.
The mean tivozanib exposure (AUC0-ь) increased 2.2-fold in patients with moderate (Child-Pugh
B) hepatic impairment and 3.4-fold in patients with severe (Child-Pugh C) hepatic impairment.
Table 5. Geometric Mean Ratio (90% CI) relative to normal patients for tivozanib PK
parameters

Source: Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Studies Table 29 (Section 2.7.2)
After re-classifying the patients in the dedicated study by NCI-ODWG criteria, there were 12
patients with mild hepatic impairment, 5 patients with moderate hepatic impairment, 0
patients with severe hepatic impairment. Mean tivozanib exposure (AUC0-ь) increased 1.79-fold
in patients with mild hepatic impairment and 3.49-fold in patients with moderate hepatic
impairment. The effect of hepatic impairment on the clearance of tivozanib was lager when
274
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
patients were classified by NCI-ODWG criteria compared to Child-Pugh criteria in the dedicated
study.
Table 6. Study AV-951-12-118 NCA for Hepatic Impairment based on NCI-ODWG Criteria

Source: Applicant’s response to FDA clinical pharmacology IR received on 07/30/2020
Therefore, in the PopPK analysis, NCI-ODWG criteria was used to classify the patients, not only
because that dosing recommendation based on NCI-ODWG criteria is more conservative, given
the larger fold changes in AUC observed, but also that the majority of the patients were cancer
patients. In the whole PopPK dataset, there were 570 patients with normal hepatic function, 83
patients with mild hepatic impairment, 10 patients with moderate hepatic impairment, 0
patients with severe hepatic impairment. The PopPK analysis indicated that mild hepatic
impairment did not have an effect on the tivozanib CL/F, while patients with moderate hepatic
impairment were found to have a 38.9% lower tivozanib CL/F compared to patients with
normal hepatic function or mild hepatic impairment.
The PopPK analysis, using all data with more patients for both mild hepatic impairment and
moderate hepatic impairment, provided a reliable estimate for the hepatic impairment effect,
given disease status was not found to be a significant covariate. Therefore, simulations were
conducted based on the PK metrics generated from PopPK analysis. Simulation results (figure 1
below) indicated that 0.89 mg once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off in patients with
moderate hepatic impairment is estimated to result in tivozanib exposure comparable to that in
patients with normal hepatic function treated with 1.34 mg QD tivozanib for 3 weeks on and 1
week off. Therefore, in the absence of safety issues in the clinical trials, 0.89 mg once daily (QD)
for 3 weeks on and 1 week off is recommended for patients with moderate hepatic impairment.

19.4.3 Pharmacometrics Review
Population pharmacokinetics and exposure response analyses
Aim: 1) Incorporate data from AV-951-15-303 into the previously developed PopPK model
describing the serum concentration of tivozanib over time; 2) Evaluate the impact of covariates
on the PK of tivozanib; 3) Establish ER models describing efficacy (change in tumor size, PFS)
and incidence of adverse events (increased blood pressure, HFS) as a function of predicted
tivozanib exposure; 4)Evaluate the impact of covariates on the ER of tivozanib.
Data: This population pharmacokinetic (PopPK) and exposure response (ER) analyses is based
on tivozanib exposure and clinical efficacy and safety data obtained from seven clinical studies
(See Table 36). A total of 4351 tivozanib plasma PK concentration samples obtained from 666
patients were included in the updated PopPK model. Patient characteristics are summarized in
Table 35.
Table 35. Summary of Clinical Studies used in PopPK analyses.

{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

Source: Table 2 in the PopPK and ER report dated 7/22/2020.
Model: The reference model was developed without the hepatic impairment study (AV-95112-118) and consisted of a one-compartment structural pharmacokinetic (PK) model with a
transit compartment absorption model (TCAM) and linear elimination. The structure of the
model is shown in Figure 4. The model updated and re-evaluated with the dedicated hepatic
impairment study (AV-951-12-118). Covariates that were evaluated include weight on V/F
and sex, disease status, dose, creatinine clearance (CLcr), hepatic impairment on CL/F.
Initially, hepatic impairment was included as a categorical covariate (normal versus mild or
moderate impairment). Although the drop in OFV was significant (ȴOFV -19), the effect size of
mild hepatic impairment was estimated to be approximately 0. The final model includes
inter-individual variability (IIV) on all structural parameters, including mean transit time
(MTT), apparent clearance (CL/F), and apparent central volume of distribution unadjusted for
bioavailability (V/F) and covariates body weight effect on V/F and effects of sex and
moderate hepatic impairment on CL/F. The parameter estimates of the final model are in
Table 37. The final model was evaluated via Goodness of Fit and Visual Predictive Check plots
(see

278
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Figure 5 and Figure 6). The T1/2 of tivozanib was 129 hours and 110 hours in women and men,
respectively. Typical CL/F was 0.651 L/h in women and 0.862 L/h in men. Volume of distribution
was found to increase nearly proportionally with body weight. Typical value of volume
distribution (95% CI) is 123 (121-126) L. Population PK modelling showed slightly high inter
individual variability (82.5%, square root of estimated variability in %) in mean transit time
(MTT) for absorption, likely due to enterohepatic recirculation of tivozanib, but moderate
variability in distribution and elimination parameters (40.5% and 28.1% for CL/F and V1/F,
respectively).

Special Populations: The PopPK analysis indicated that mild hepatic impairment did not have an
impact on the tivozanib CL/F, while patients with moderate hepatic impairment were found to
have a 38.9% lower tivozanib CL/F. To further check whether the PopPK analysis using all data
can capture the feature of the 12 mild and 5 moderate HI patients in dedicated study with
respect to CL/F, we summarized the empirical Bayesian estimates of CL/F by studies and HI
categories by NCI as in Study. The popPK results for the HI study 118 are consistent with the noncompartment analysis results with respect to hepatic effect on PK. Mild patients in the study
AV-951-12-118 (referred as 118) have lower CL than patients in other studies. In addition, the
popPK analysis results using 118 study only are also consistent with 118 study using NCA.
Overall, the PopPK results are sufficient to make conclusions on HI dosing recommendation.

To assess hepatic impairment on tivozanib exposure and further support dose adjustment, four
dosing regimens, based on either once daily (QD) or once every other day (QOD) dosing, were
simulated using the final tivozanib popPK model:
x Original: 1.5 mg capsule QD for all groups;
x Regimen 1: 1.5 mg capsule QD (normal or mild hepatic impairment) or 1.0 mg capsule
QD (moderate hepatic impairment);

Regimen 3: 1.5 mg capsule QD (normal or mild hepatic impairment) or 1.0 mg capsule

Note that 1.5 mg capsule is equivalent to 1.34 mg tivozanib, 1.0 mg capsule in this figure is
equivalent to 0.89 mg tivozanib.
For all dosing regimens, 2,000 virtual patient profiles per group were simulated and the
average concentration (Cavg) were summarized by dosing regimen. The simulation results
are summarized in Figure 7. Simulated mean tivozanib exposure (AUCtau) increased 1.62fold in patients with moderate hepatic impairment compared to patient with normal
hepatic function or mild hepatic impairment. Simulation results indicated that 0.89 mg QD
tivozanib (i.e.,1 mg capsule) for 3 weeks on and 1 week off in patients with moderate
hepatic impairment can establish tivozanib exposure comparable to that in patients with
normal hepatic function treated with 1.34 mg QD tivozanib for 3 weeks on and 1 week off.
Therefore, in the absence of safety concerns, 0.89 mg once daily (QD) for 3 weeks on and 1
week off is recommended for patients with moderate hepatic impairment.

{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

Source: Figure 6 in the PopPK and ER report dated 7/22/2020.
Renal impairment, as quantified by creatinine clearance (CLcr) with median 76.2 mL/min and
range from 26.8 to 210 mL/min, did not have a statistically significant effect on the PK of
tivozanib. Therefore, no dose adjustments are required for patients with mild or moderate
renal impairment. It is the general expectation that V/F scales linearly with WT. In addition,
females were found to have a statistically significant, 25.2% lower CL/F compared to males.
Exposure Response Analyses: Cavg over a four-week treatment period was identified as the
best predictor for efficacy compared to other exposure metrics. Cavg in cycle 2 was derived
based on individual post-hoc PK parameter estimates from the final PK model. The efficacy
endpoints of interest include tumor size and progression free survival (PFS) and the safety
endpoint of interest include hand and foot syndrome (HFS) and blood pressure. All ER analyses
were performed on data obtained in patients treated with either tivozanib or sorafenib in
studies AV-951-09-301 and AV-951-15-303. Only for HFS, data obtained in study AV-951-07-201
was included from patients with exposure metrics.
Exposure Efficacy Response Analyses:
Tumor Size: A tumor growth inhibition model was developed to describe the change is tumor
size as a function of time, tivozanib exposure, and covariates. Covariates including Cavg,
study population (AV-951-09-301 vs. AV-951-15-303), baseline ECOG, sex, prior therapy, and
International metastatic renal cell carcinoma database Consortium (IMDC) status were tested
via the stepwise covariate modeling (SCM). Effects of Cavg on tumor shrinkage rate (KD),
study population on tumor growth rate (KL), and ECOG status on baseline tumor size was
identified as the best model describing change in tumor size as a function of time and
tivozanib exposure. The model structure is shown in the equation below. The final model
parameter estimates are summarized in Table 39. The model was further assessed via
goodness of fit and predictive check plots (
Figure 8).

{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}

Source: Figure 11 in the PopPK and ER report dated 7/22/2020.

Progression Free Survival: Cox proportional hazard models were used to assess the effects of
tivozanib exposure and covariates on risk of progressive disease. ECOG, study population, and
Cavg which was categorized as quartiles were selected as significant covariates in the final
model. The parameter estimates were summarized in Table 40Table 40. The model indicates
that the highest tivozanib exposure quartile (Q4) relative to the lowest tivozanib exposure
quartile (Q1) was highly statistically significant with an estimated relative risk reduction of
approximately 33%. Patients with ECOG status 1 had a 59% higher risk relative to patients with
ECOG status 0. Similarly, advanced refractory RCC (AV-951-15-303) patients had a 58% higher
risk relative to advanced RCC (AV-951-09-301) patients. However, these results should be
interpreted with caution as it was based on data at only one dose level.
Table 40. Summary of Final Tivozanib Cox Proportional Hazard Model of Progression Free
Survival

Source: Table 16 in the PopPK and ER report dated 7/22/2020.
Exposure Safety Response Analyses:
285
Version date: April 2, 2018
Reference ID: 4759922

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation {NDA 212904}
{FOTIVDA, tivozanib}
Hand and Foot Syndrome: Logistic regression was used to evaluate the influence of tivozanib
exposure data in combination with covariates on the odds of experiencing an HFS event. No
significant covariate except for Cavg was selected in the final model. Table 41 shows parameter
estimated from the final model and the plot observed HFS occurrences with model predicted
probability vs. Tivozanib Cavg. The result demonstrated exposure-dependent increases in rates
of hand-foot syndrome (HFS, a.k.a., palmar-plantar erythrodysesthesia [PPE]).
Table 41. Summary of Final Logistic Regression Model of Effect of Tivozanib Exposure on Odds
of Hand and Foot Syndrome.

Source: Table 18 and Figure 24 in the PopPK and ER report dated 7/22/2020.
Blood Pressure: No exposure-response relationship was observed.
Review Summary
In general, the applicant’s population PK analysis is considered acceptable for the purpose of
supporting analyses objectives. The applicant’s analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no
significant discordance identified.
More specifically, the developed model was used to support the current submission as outlined
in Table 42Table 42.
